# Two static scalar spatial laws on the original near-critical word

Status: self-contained analytical draft, UNVERIFIED, no fact_id. Worker max,
allocated round56 after completed55; completed ceiling56/attempted57. No
mathematical numerical evaluation, solver, symbolic program, formal request,
installation, consult, or subagent was used. This document does not establish
the original target. Its two negative screens are actual error upper bounds,
not an interpretation of weak lower certificates.

Controlling assignment: TASK.md; initial master guidance deebd8471c9ff5a7.
Later guidance f8408daf213a461d leaves this bounded analytical task unchanged
and closes every remaining formal grant. Plan finding: 6ec9e4bb4db97c3b.

## 1. Scope recovered before choosing the laws

The following are shared, UNVERIFIED drafts, not premises of the proofs below.

* 1a23bf9f1a7799fd uses c_sc=13/5, a_sc=c_sc^(-17), and the word
  Hsc_(j+1)=(Hsc_j,a_sc*c_sc^j,Hsc_j), without a +1 bridge. It treats a
  specified log-periodic derivative made of unit ramps and plateaus. Its
  extension covers every COMMON plateau parameter ell>=1 on the two rays,
  arbitrary independent ray phases, every initial point, and all crossings.
  Its unequal-ell statement retains the stated calibrated range
  b_r<=ell_sigma<=1+(S_r-log c_sc)/(c_sc-1), m>=r>=17. Neither statement
  covers arbitrary nonperiodic scalar derivatives or the original word here.
* 3d2994746c1d983e uses that same c_sc,a_sc and no-+1 word. It allows
  arbitrary horizon-dependent functions and knots, but every nonzero queried
  secant on EACH ray must fit that ray's fixed band
  M_sigma/c_sc<=|g(x)|/|x|<=M_sigma, with 0<M_sigma<=1. The two ray scales
  and phases are unrestricted relative to one another. Its conclusion is
  still conditional on those per-ray factor13/5 bands.
* 4d3911499920adbe uses that same no-+1 word. Its channels are precisely
  accepted F_ray, all scalar quadratics, its explicitly defined single-cap
  quadratic-prefix/affine-suffix channel, and the continuous two-curvature
  homogeneous family f(x)=u*x^2/2 on x>=0 and v*x^2/2 on x<=0. Its direct
  two-curvature proof is for u,v in (0,1], with zero curvatures addressed by
  continuity if included. It is not a bound for derivatives with additional
  spatial slope changes. Its horizon bounds start at m>=18.
* e08ee0674ce368d3 DOES use the original word, for m>=128. It fixes profiles
  g(sigma*R*t)=sigma*mu*R*G_sigma(t), with G_sigma(t)=t on [0,1],
  0<=t*G_sigma'(t)<=p_grow*G_sigma(t) outside, and a finite mutual ray ratio
  kappa_ray. Its first sector has mu=1/d_r for an integer r>=0,
  1/2<=p_grow<=3/4 and kappa_ray*(p_grow/(1-p_grow))^p_grow<=23/10.
  Its second has 0<mu<=1/d_2, 1/2<=p_grow<=2/3 and the corresponding product
  <=7/4. These are genuine restrictions, not a complete scalar reduction.

Accepted scopes were also checked. Fact 1fca3e5dd555cb4a constructs its
specified outward alternating-ray stages, including offsets and retained
areas; it does not exhaust scalar trajectories. Fact b98bb807d514e80d gives
K_n and the original benchmark recurrences, not an upper bound on all Huber
trajectories or all scalar functions. Fact 1252b8823bb5a290 supplies complete
directed interpolation and its stated unqueried-terminal reduction, with no
scalar losslessness claim. Fact 62a7d9329121fd75 compares its finite triangle
only to scalar quadratics, the complete specified ray channel, and the full
specified complex channel, NOT to all nonlinear scalar functions.

Only fact b98bb807d514e80d is needed as a mathematical predecessor below,
for the definitions and accepted bounds on the ORIGINAL benchmark. Every
new scalar calculation is reproduced in this joint draft. In particular no
shared unverified closure is imported as a lemma.

## 2. Original word, exact prefix products, and benchmark

Fix an integer m>=32 and retain this SAME m in every child. Put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), t=1/m, beta=1-t,
    c_m=lambda*(1+t), a_m=beta/c_m,
    c=c_m, a=a_m (local aliases), b_j=a*c^j, d_j=1+b_j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j), j=0,...,m-1,
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j.

The letter b_j in this document is the bridge EXCESS, not a physical step.
Let ell(i)=nu_2(i+1), where nu_2(k) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive
integer k. The actual physical step at index i is h_i=d_(ell(i)). Let s_2(i)
be the number of ones in the binary expansion of i, including s_2(0)=0.
For 0<=i<=n define

    Prod_i=product_(0<=r<i)(h_r-1)=beta^i*c^(-s_2(i)).              (2.1)

Here and below all finite sums and products are mathematical definitions;
none was evaluated by a program. To prove (2.1), binary carrying gives
s_2(i+1)=s_2(i)+1-nu_2(i+1). The ratio of its right sides at i+1 and i
is beta*c^(ell(i)-1)=a*c^ell(i)=h_i-1. Both sides start at1. In particular

    Prod_n=beta^n*c^(-m).                                        (2.2)

The S_j recurrence S_(j+1)=2*S_j+1+a*c^j gives

    S_j+1=A*c^j+(1-A)*2^j, A=a/(c-2)=beta/[c*(c-2)].          (2.3)

Since c>lambda>2 and lambda*(lambda-2)=1, we have 0<A<1, and hence
S_m+1<=c^m. These parameters also have 0<a<1, 0<beta<1, and c<3:
lambda<5/2 and c<=(5/2)*(33/32)=165/64<3.

Write C_k=1+S_circle(k), B_k=1+2*S_bullet(k), U_k^ZJ=1/B_k.
Fact b98bb807d514e80d supplies

    C_0=B_0=1,
    C_k=max_(0<=r<k) Omega(C_r,C_(k-r-1)),
    B_k=max_(0<=r<k) G(C_r,B_(k-r-1)),
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4*u*v))/2,
    G(u,v)=2*u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8*u*v)/2,
    B_k<=1+4*k^(log_2(3)).

Thus, on the original physical length n=2^m-1,

    B_n<=5*3^m.                                              (2.4)

No asymptotic constant or numerically evaluated benchmark is needed here.
All payoffs use L=1 and TRUE distance to the minimizer set:

    V=2*(f(x_n)-min f)/dist(x_0,argmin f)^2.

Multiplying f by an arbitrary L>0 and using steps h_i/L preserves all
positions and this normalized payoff.

## 3. Law I: a flat minimizer interval with two unit-curvature sides

For any width w>=0 let I_w=[-w,0] and define the single static function

              (x+w)^2/2, x<-w,
    f_w(x)=   0,         -w<=x<=0,
              x^2/2,    x>0.

Let p_w(x)=min(0,max(-w,x)) be the interval projection, and put
g_w(x)=x-p_w(x). The derivative is continuous, nondecreasing, and has slopes
one, zero, one in spatial order. Consequently f_w is convex and1-smooth,
with exactly the minimizer set I_w and minimum0. For w>0 it is genuinely
nonlinear. We always normalize using distance to I_w, not distance to its
midpoint or another more distant chosen minimizer.

### 3.1 All directed inequalities and all integrated values

At every point, with y=p_w(x), we have

    x=g+y, f_w(x)=g^2/2, y in I_w.

For any two sampled points i,j, including ANY chosen optimum, the full
signed directed interpolation residual is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+g_i*(x_j-x_i)+(g_i-g_j)^2/2
        =g_i*(y_j-y_i)<=0.                                  (3.1)

Indeed g_i>0 implies y_i=0>=y_j; g_i<0 implies y_i=-w<=y_j;
and g_i=0 gives equality. This is an explicit all-pairs proof, including
both directions, all optimum rows, repeated points, and every plateau area.
The formula f=g^2/2 integrates the fixed derivative from the actual interval;
it is not a deletion of a previously accumulated area.

### 3.2 Actual GD, zero steps, capture, and exact revisits

Let Dist_i=dist(x_i,I_w), with Dist_0>0. At any outside point, a step h>=0 gives

    Dist_next=(1-h)*Dist_i,                     0<=h<=1,
    Dist_next=((h-1)*Dist_i-w)_+,               h>1.                (3.2)

For h<=1 the point stays on its current side or lands on its boundary.
For h>1 it either lands inside I_w or crosses to the opposite outside side.
An inside point has zero gradient and stays at precisely that same point.
This proves (3.2) directly on both sides of the interval. A zero step repeats
the complete jet. There is no restart at a different minimizer after capture.

Exact outside revisits are also legal: for any w>0,D>0, two consecutive
steps h=2+w/D take D to -w-D and then back to D. Each outside value is D^2/2.
This illustration uses the SAME law, not an additional candidate family.
Its only purpose is to make the exact-revisit convention explicit.

On our original word all h_i>1. Define Ret_0=0 and

    Ret_(i+1)=(h_i-1)*Ret_i+1,
    Ret_i=Prod_i*sum_(r=1)^i 1/Prod_r.                              (3.3)

Repeated application of (3.2) gives the exact formula

    Dist_i=(Prod_i*Dist_0-w*Ret_i)_+.                                 (3.4)

For the induction, ((b*z)_+-w)_+=(b*z-w)_+ when b,w>=0;
applying this to the preceding affine expression proves the next step.
If capture has occurred, the expression remains zero as required.

Consequently, for ALL w>=0 and every initial point outside I_w,

    V_w=(Dist_n/Dist_0)^2<=Prod_n^2=beta^(2*n)*c^(-2*m).             (3.5)

This is an upper bound on the actual error of the entire specified law,
including horizon-dependent widths, either initial side, and arbitrary
initial distances. It is not an upper bound on arbitrary scalar functions.

### 3.3 A concrete nonlinear, noncaptured trajectory and its full spatial order

Here is one exact choice with w>0 and every queried distance positive.
The Prod_i in (2.1) are pairwise distinct. To see this, all are in Q(sqrt(2)).
The nontrivial conjugation fixes beta and sends c to
c'=(1-sqrt(2))*(1+1/m). If beta^(i-j)*c^(-s_2(i)+s_2(j))=1,
divide by its conjugate. Since |c/c'|=lambda^2>1, the integer exponent
of c must be zero. Then beta^(i-j)=1 implies i=j.

Define the positive finite quantities

    Delta_sp=min_(0<=i<j<=n)|Prod_i-Prod_j|,
    Ret_max=max_(0<=i<=n) Ret_i,
    w=min{Prod_n/(2*Ret_n), Delta_sp/(4*Ret_max)}.                    (3.6)

These finite expressions define the width before the run; they are not a
numerical search or an unproved optimization problem. Start at x_0=1 and
choose the closest minimizer0, so the TRUE initial squared distance is1.
Since Ret_i/Prod_i is increasing in i, (3.6) implies

    Dist_i=Prod_i-w*Ret_i>=Prod_i/2>0,
    x_i=Dist_i if i is even, and x_i=-w-Dist_i if i is odd.         (3.7)

Every step thus crosses the whole minimizer interval. Equations (3.2)--(3.7)
give every physical point, derivative, value, and GD update without deleting
any interval or knot.

For completeness the spatial order, which is not chronological, is exact.
Put alpha=-log(beta)/log(c)>0 and order_i=s_2(i)+alpha*i. Then
Prod_i=c^(-order_i), and the order_i are distinct by the preceding argument.
For every pair, |w*(Ret_i-Ret_j)|<=Delta_sp/4<|Prod_i-Prod_j|, so Dist_i-Dist_j has exactly
the sign of Prod_i-Prod_j. Every odd-indexed point is less than -w; every
even-indexed point is positive. Among even indices, increasing order_i means
decreasing x; among odd indices, increasing order_i means increasing x.
This completely specifies the total order by explicit, distinct real keys
for each m, with NO guessed phase comparisons and no ties. The fixed
minimizer interval lies between the two groups. Other widths may have ties;
(3.1)--(3.4) still apply without changing any previously defined value.

The terminal point is -w-Dist_n, since n is odd, and

    f_w(x_n)=(Prod_n-w*Ret_n)^2/2,
    Prod_n^2/4<=V_w=(Prod_n-w*Ret_n)^2<Prod_n^2.                     (3.8)

Thus this nonlinear example has a proved exact payoff, not just a lower
certificate. Nevertheless (2.4)--(3.5) imply uniformly for the whole law

    B_n*V_w<=5*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0.                         (3.9)

The flat-minimum mechanism is weak on the original word even when its
width is chosen from the entire word and every queried point remains outside.

## 4. Law II: a one-sided Huber shelf with an actual inward return

Fix ONE function, independent of m:

              x^2/2, x<=1,
    F_sh(x)=  x-1/2, x>=1,
    g_sh(x)=min(x,1).

It is convex, differentiable, globally1-smooth, with unique minimizer0 and
minimum0. Its negative ray is quadratic and its positive ray eventually
has constant gradient. In particular the two outer ray gradients have no
finite multiplicative comparison bound of the type in e08ee0674ce368d3.
The complete function is fixed throughout the trajectory.

Define y(x)=(x-1)_+. Then at every x

    x=g+y, F_sh(x)=g^2/2+y, g<=1, y>=0, (1-g)*y=0.          (4.1)

The positive proximal value y is retained on every return to the shelf.
For ANY two points, including optimum0 and repeated queries,

    Q_ij=(1-g_i)*(y_i-y_j)=-(1-g_i)*y_j<=0.                (4.2)

This explicit factorization proves ALL directed inequalities for the entire
trajectory, including every incoming and outgoing terminal row. It uses
the integrated value in (4.1), not just the slope or a pointwise envelope.

### 4.1 Parameters of the selected return branch

Use exactly the enclosing-m d_0,d_1,d_2,d_3 from Section2 and put

    w_sh=beta^2*c,
    v_sh=w_sh-1-beta,
    R_sh=S_3+1=8+4*a+2*beta+beta*c.

For every m>=32 the following strict inequalities hold:

    0<v_sh<a*beta,
    c*v_sh<beta^2<1,
    1+beta<w_sh<2+a.                                     (4.3)

Here is an exact proof without numerical evaluation. Since beta>=31/32
and c>12/5, w_sh>(31/32)^2*(12/5)>2, the last inequality following from
12*31^2>10*32^2. Thus v_sh>0. Also

    c*v_sh-beta^2
      =lambda^2*(1-t^2)^2-lambda*(2+t-t^2)-(1-2*t+t^2)
      <=(2-lambda)*t-(lambda+2)*t^2<0.

The inequality uses (1-t^2)^2<=1-t^2 and lambda^2=2*lambda+1.
It proves c*v_sh<beta^2 and hence v_sh<a*beta. Finally

    c*(w_sh-2-a)
      =beta^2*c^2-2*c-beta
      <=lambda^2-2*lambda*(1+t)-(1-t)
      =-(2*lambda-1)*t<0.

Choose any delta in the FULL interval [0,v_sh/a] and start at

    X_0=R_sh+delta, z_sh=v_sh-a*delta>=0.                  (4.4)

The squared distance to the unique minimizer is exactly (R_sh+delta)^2.
The endpoint delta=v_sh/a is permitted and will give a genuine zero landing.

### 4.2 First ten physical steps, with the old shelf intact

The first seven steps are the actual word H_3, with levels

    0,1,0,2,0,1,0.

For 0<=i<=7 let Prefix_sh(i) be the sum of its first i physical steps. Then

    X_i=R_sh+delta-Prefix_sh(i)>=1+delta,
    g_i=1, F_sh(X_i)=X_i-1/2.                             (4.5)

All seven updates are therefore exactly the original GD updates, and
X_7=1+delta. The next three original physical levels are3,0,1, giving

    X_8=delta-b_3,      g_8=X_8,   F_sh(X_8)=X_8^2/2,
    X_9=w_sh-a*delta,   g_9=1,     F_sh(X_9)=X_9-1/2,
    X_10=z_sh,          g_10=z_sh, F_sh(X_10)=z_sh^2/2.      (4.6)

The equations follow in order from
X_8=X_7-d_3, X_9=-a*X_8, and X_10=X_9-d_1.
Their region assumptions are proved, not guessed:

    b_3-delta>=(w_sh-v_sh)/a=(1+beta)/a>1,
    X_9=1+beta+z_sh>1,
    0<=z_sh<=v_sh<1.

Furthermore delta<=v_sh/a<beta implies

    X_7<X_9<X_6, with X_6=2+a+delta.                      (4.7)

The right inequality follows from X_9<=w_sh<2+a<=X_6.
Thus X_9 is a strict inward return into the SAME spatial interval [X_7,X_6]
traversed during the last step of the earlier H_3. The shelf gradient there
is1 and its retained proximal value is y_9=X_9-1>0. Nothing is redefined on
this return. This is not an outward-only fresh-ray construction.

### 4.3 Every remaining query, spatial order, and ties

For EVERY actual physical index 10<=i<=n the exact formula is

    X_i=(-1)^(i-10)*z_sh*beta^(i-10)*c^(2-s_2(i)).          (4.8)

If z_sh=0 this means precisely that the actual point0 is repeated forever.
Assume z_sh>0. Since s_2(i)>=1 for i>=10,

    |X_i|<=c*z_sh<=c*v_sh<beta^2<1.                       (4.9)

Thus every one of these queries really has gradient X_i and value X_i^2/2.
At i=10, s_2(10)=2 gives the correct point z_sh. Binary carrying then gives

    X_(i+1)/X_i=-beta*c^(ell(i)-1)=-(h_i-1)=1-h_i.

This verifies every remaining GD update on the original physical word.
No child clock was restarted. In particular the bound (4.9) certifies every
intermediate query, not merely the endpoints of large blocks. Gradients
continue to vary inside those blocks.

Here is the complete spatial order. X_8<-1 is below every later negative
point. Every positive point of the tail is less than1. The old positive
queries are ordered

    1<=X_7<X_9<X_6<X_5<...<X_0.

The optimum0 separates the positive and negative tail points. For i>=10,
even i means positive and odd i means negative. Within either sign, their
magnitudes are proportional to c^(-order_i), with the SAME explicit keys
order_i=s_2(i)+alpha*i used in Section3.3. Thus increasing order_i gives
decreasing positive coordinates and increasing negative coordinates.
There are no ties among those keys: an equality of two magnitudes would
give beta^(i-j)*c^(-s_2(i)+s_2(j))=1, already proved to force i=j.
There are also no ties with the older groups by their strict separating
intervals. When z_sh=0, all indices i>=10 instead coincide with optimum0,
with gradient and value0. These are all ties in this construction.

The word itself has no zero steps. If a zero step is inserted, its actual
map x->x-0*g_sh(x) is the identity and repeats its jet; (4.2) remains valid.
Formula (4.8) refers to the original positive steps and can be pulled back
along their index injection. No claim transfers B_n to a new padded length.

### 4.4 Exact payoff and a uniform upper screen for this branch

The original n is odd and s_2(n)=m. Therefore

    X_n=-z_sh*beta^(n-10)*c^(2-m),
    F_sh(X_n)=z_sh^2*beta^(2*(n-10))*c^(4-2*m)/2,
    V_sh(delta)=((v_sh-a*delta)/(R_sh+delta))^2
                 *beta^(2*(n-10))*c^(4-2*m).              (4.10)

Over the ENTIRE selected interval 0<=delta<=v_sh/a, the numerator of the
fraction decreases to zero while its positive denominator increases. Its
maximum is attained at delta=0 and equals the displayed expression with
v_sh/R_sh. In particular, using c*v_sh<1, c<3, R_sh>1, and beta<1,

    V_sh(delta)<9*c^(-2*m),
    B_n*V_sh(delta)<45*(3/lambda^2)^m ->0.                 (4.11)

These are actual error upper bounds on a fully specified returning family.
For all m>=32 they already give V_sh<U_n^ZJ: lambda^2>9/2 implies
3/lambda^2<2/3, and 45*(2/3)^10=46080/59049<1. The same comparison for
Law I follows from (3.9). No guessed decimal or numerical phase is involved.

## 5. Counterchecks preventing an overstatement or a reset

### 5.1 Erasing the return's old area violates a directed inequality

On the trajectory (4.4)--(4.8), replace ONLY the value at X_9 by the falsely
reset core value g_9^2/2=1/2, leaving its position and derivative unchanged.
The earlier point X_7 has gradient1 and value X_7-1/2. Its row to point9 is

    Q_(7,9)=(X_7-1/2)-1/2+(X_9-X_7)=X_9-1>0.

This is an exact illegal directed pair, not an appeal to intuition about
history. The correct value X_9-1/2 has retained area X_9-1 and satisfies
every row by (4.2). The construction therefore directly tests the nonzero
proximal-height issue in the assignment.

### 5.2 The branch bound is FALSE for all starts of the same shelf function

Keep exactly F_sh and the same original H_m, but start at

    X_0^Hub=2*S_m+1.

Every actual query stays in the positive shelf, since even the terminal
point is S_m+1>1. Thus all gradients are1, the terminal value is S_m+1/2,
the true initial radius is 2*S_m+1, and

    V_Hub=1/(2*S_m+1).                                   (5.1)

This is the already familiar canonical Huber mechanism, not a third law
or a new claim of useful profile improvement. It prevents extrapolating
(4.11) to the full one-sided Huber family.

One can see its contrast at the ORIGINAL B scale without an external
asymptotic constant. The accepted recurrences imply inductively
C_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j, by choosing equal children and Omega(u,u)=lambda*u.
They also imply B_(2^j-1)>=lambda^j: choose equal child lengths, use
monotonicity of G (each displayed term increases in either nonnegative
argument), and G(lambda^(j-1),lambda^(j-1))=4*lambda^(j-1)
>=lambda^j. The base j=0 is1. From (2.3),

    B_n*V_Hub>lambda^m/(2*c^m)
              =1/[2*(1+1/m)^m]>1/6.                     (5.2)

For the last bound, the binomial theorem gives
(1+1/m)^m<=sum_(k=0)^m 1/k!<3: for k>=2, k!>=2^(k-1), and the resulting
geometric upper bound has sum1, with a strict inequality by k=3.
Thus a vanishing B-scaled bound for ALL starts of this same function would
be contradicted by (5.1)--(5.2). No such broader bound is asserted here.

## 6. Achieved scope and the missing interface

There are exactly two selected derivative laws. Law I has an actual uniform
upper bound for its entire interval-distance family, plus a fully defined
nonlinear example with exact ordering and a two-sided terminal estimate.
Law II has an exact returning trajectory for every m>=32 and every delta in
its specified interval, a full spatial-order description, nonzero retained
proximal heights, all directed inequalities, genuine within-block variation,
and an exact terminal payoff. Its whole selected returning branch is weak.
The zero-landing endpoint and inserted zero-step convention preserve all jets.

Neither diagnostic supplies a new witness with B_n*V approaching1. Even a
successful diagnostic on all these H_m would leave the every-n/every-h
target as a separate obligation. The present result is narrower: the two
specified screens do NOT exclude all nonlinear scalar functions, do NOT
exclude other initial points of F_sh, and do NOT bound unrestricted W_n.
No proposed bang-bang, monotonicity, symmetry, or spatial-order reduction
has been declared lossless. The old closed sectors are not enlarged by name.

The surviving interface is to construct a static scalar derivative with
additional retained spatial information (or another initialization on a
candidate function) whose ACTUAL original-word payoff is large enough at
B_n scale, with the same complete directed and true-distance audit. A list
of feasible interpolation variables alone would not resolve this interface.
All new mathematics in this document remains UNVERIFIED and cannot be used
as a fact predecessor until a separately authorized verification succeeds.

# Cap-active obtuse triangles: exact whole-word family obstruction

**ONE JOINT UNVERIFIED ANALYTICAL DRAFT. Original target OPEN.**

Project `gd-exact-profile-20260926`, worker `max2`, invocation73 after
ended baseline72, controller cap1, ended ceiling73 / attempted ceiling74.
The assignment prohibits formal submission. No assertion newly derived
here is a fact, and no `fact_submit` is performed. All new ingredients,
including the scalar comparison, are proved together in this report.

## 1. Candidate result and unchanged scope

Fix ANY integer m>=1536, with the SAME enclosing parameters everywhere:

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/(4m), beta=1-epsilon,
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c, d_j=a*c^j (0<=j<=m),
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j) (0<=j<m),
    S_j=sum H_j, A_j=1+S_j, n=2^m-1, D_m=1+2S_m.

The quantity d_m is only an auxiliary ruler, not an extra step.
All original steps, both copies of every child, and the actual last leaf
1+a are retained. No child resets m or any other parameter.

Choose arbitrary b,p,q,R>0 with pq<b^2, fixed before the run, and put

    w_+=(b,p), w_-=(-b,q), D=conv{0,w_+,w_-},
    F_D(Z)=max_(g in D) [<Z,g>-||g||^2/2],
    Z_0=(X,-R), Z_(i+1)=Z_i-h_i grad F_D(Z_i), h=H_m.

Here X is an arbitrary real number. Parameters may depend on the entire
known word. The two assigned mechanisms are (I) p=q<b and (II) p!=q,
pq<b^2. There is no restriction on their cap ratio and no cap dilation.
Let Z_min be the COMPLETE minimum set, and let
rho_0=dist(Z_0,Z_min). For rho_0>0 define the actual performance

    E=2F_D(Z_n)/rho_0^2.

The candidate conclusion is, uniformly on BOTH entire declared laws,

    E <= 1/D_m.                                           (1.1)

For every fixed admissible geometry b,p,q and every R>0 this bound is
attained by a below-axis start, with strictly positive cap-normal height
at EVERY original query. Consequently the exact supremum over all
admissible starts for each fixed geometry and R is 1/D_m. The same
conclusion holds for the full finite Walsh bodies and invariant starts
specified and proved in Section10.

This is a restricted-family upper bound and exact family optimum, NOT an
upper bound on the full W_n. The original problem, unchanged in
PROBLEM.md, uses EVERY horizon n, EVERY nonnegative predetermined word,
all finite dimensions, all smooth convex objectives with an attained
minimum, and W=2R. Nothing here changes those quantifiers or resolves
the original minimax target.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used only for the original normalization
and interpretation of ordered interpolation residuals; all rows for the
present actual function are derived below. In particular its terminal
reduction does not authorize deleting prefix rows. Accepted
b98bb807d514e80d defines the complete K channel. Accepted
1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n<=F_ray_n<=W_n. Accepted
931b9653ce0f7201 supplies B_n/D_m<47/50 only in that UPPER direction.
Its separate full-class hypothesis W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D_m) is UNPROVED.

Earlier cone and scalar drafts are awareness, not accepted premises.
The common-radius scalar interval argument is rederived in Section8.
The new asymmetric step in Section7 uses both angle-cap inequalities,
not a monotonicity assumption when changing clipping levels.

## 2. One static smooth convex function and its full minimum

This paragraph applies to any nonempty compact convex body D containing0
in a finite-dimensional Euclidean space. Maximizing the expression in
F_D is equivalent to minimizing ||Z-g||^2 over g in D. The minimizer is
unique by strict convexity; write g=P_D(Z). Its necessary and sufficient
normal inequalities are

    <Z-g,v-g><=0 for EVERY v in D.                        (2.1)

Necessity follows by differentiating along the segment to v. Conversely,
expanding ||Z-v||^2-||Z-g||^2 gives a nonnegative quantity under (2.1).
For g=P_D(Z) and g'=P_D(Z'), adding the two normal inequalities gives

    ||g-g'||^2 <= <Z-Z',g-g'>,

so P_D is 1-Lipschitz. Evaluating F_D at its two maximizers gives

    0 <= F_D(Z')-F_D(Z)-<g,Z'-Z>
       <= <g'-g,Z'-Z> <= ||Z'-Z||^2.

Thus F_D is differentiable with gradient P_D and is 1-smooth. It is
finite and convex as a supremum of affine functions over compact D.
It is nonnegative because0 is a feasible maximizer candidate.

For the triangle, F_D(Z)=0 if and only if

    <Z,w_+><=0 and <Z,w_-><=0.                            (2.2)

If both inequalities hold, every g in D has <Z,g><=0, and the maximum
is0 at g=0. If one inner product is positive, g=t*w_sigma for a small
enough t in(0,1] makes t<Z,w_sigma>-t^2||w_sigma||^2/2 positive.
This proves both directions and the complete attained minimum set

    Z_min={ (x,y): b*x+p*y<=0, -b*x+q*y<=0 }.

It is the polar cone of cone{w_+,w_-}; the minimum value is0.
For y<0 its section is q*y/b<=x<=-p*y/b. The origin is a minimum,
but it will NOT replace the closest minimum in the denominator.

For arbitrary advertised L>0 use f=L*F_D and the physical steps h_i/L.
The same points and gradients divided by L result, and the original
W-normalized performance is exactly E. No smaller effective word is
substituted.

## 3. The angle, the finite caps, and all below-axis projection faces

Define local geometric symbols

    ell_+=sqrt(b^2+p^2), ell_-=sqrt(b^2+q^2),
    e_+=w_+/ell_+, e_-=w_-/ell_-,
    n_+=(p,-b)/ell_+, n_-=(-q,-b)/ell_-,
    M=b^2-pq,
    kappa=M/(ell_+*ell_-), omega=b*(p+q)/(ell_+*ell_-).

Each (e_sigma,n_sigma) is an orthonormal basis; n_sigma points below
its ray. Direct products give

    <e_+,e_->=-kappa,
    <e_+,n_->=<e_-,n_+>=-omega,
    <n_+,n_->=kappa,
    ell_+^2*ell_-^2-M^2=b^2*(p+q)^2.

It follows that kappa,omega are in(0,1) and kappa^2+omega^2=1.
Moreover M<ell_+^2 and M<ell_-^2, since p,q are positive. Hence

    kappa*ell_+ < ell_-,
    kappa*ell_- < ell_+.                                 (3.1)

In particular kappa*max(ell_+,ell_-)<min(ell_+,ell_-).
This proves the proposed compatibility, without assuming it.

For Z=(x,y) with y<0 put t_+=<Z,e_+>, t_-=<Z,e_->. Since

    ell_+*t_+ + ell_-*t_-=(p+q)*y<0,

they cannot both be positive. If neither is positive, Z is in Z_min
and g=0. If t_sigma=t>0, the corresponding horizontal sign is positive
for sigma=+ and negative for sigma=-. In either case

    Z=t*e_sigma+z*n_sigma, z=<Z,n_sigma>>0,
    <Z,e_opposite>=-kappa*t-omega*z<0.                    (3.2)

Here and below opposite means the other of the two signs. Define

    u=min(t,ell_sigma), g=u*e_sigma,
    r=Z-g=(t-u)*e_sigma+z*n_sigma.

The COMPLETE finite vertex normal guards are

    <r,0-g>=-u*(t-u)<=0,
    <r,w_sigma-g>=(ell_sigma-u)*(t-u)=0,
    <r,w_opposite-g>
       =-(t-u)*(kappa*ell_opposite+u)-omega*z*ell_opposite<0.
                                                               (3.3)

Their convex combinations give (2.1) for every point of D. Therefore
this is the unique projection. These formulas establish that below the
axis the only possible projection faces are0, the relative interiors
of the two origin-ray segments, and their finite endpoints. The edge
joining w_+ to w_- and the triangle interior cannot be projection faces
there. Endpoint ties t=ell_sigma are included in (3.3).

For later use define the one-sided Huber value on t>=0,

    phi_ell(t)=t^2/2                 if 0<=t<=ell,
               ell*t-ell^2/2        if t>=ell.

At an active below-axis state, F_D(Z)=phi_(ell_sigma)(t).
Its finite-cap normal height is EXACTLY

    delta=<Z-g,g>=u*(t-u).

Thus delta>0 exactly when t>ell_sigma. A cap-active projection is not
being replaced by conic projection. If t<=ell_sigma then delta=0.

## 4. True initial closest minimum and vertical motion

If Z_0=(X,-R) lies outside Z_min, exactly one initial ray sigma is active:

    sigma=+ if X>p*R/b, rho_0=(b*X-p*R)/ell_+;
    sigma=- if X<-q*R/b, rho_0=(-b*X-q*R)/ell_-.

When -q*R/b<=X<=p*R/b the initial point is a minimum, all later points
are stationary, and the gap is0. Such a point has closest radius0 and
does not enter a positive-radius quotient.

For an active start write Z_0=t_0*e_sigma+z_0*n_sigma as in (3.2).
Set

    Z_star=Z_0-t_0*e_sigma=z_0*n_sigma.

Its two scores are0 and -omega*z_0<0, so it is in the complete polar.
For any U in Z_min,

    <Z_0-Z_star,U-Z_star>=t_0*<e_sigma,U><=0.

Expansion of ||Z_0-U||^2 now shows it is at least t_0^2, with equality
at Z_star. Thus Z_star is the unique closest point and rho_0=t_0.
The nearest point is not assumed to be (0,-R) or the origin.

At any active physical query with h>=0 the vertical coordinate obeys

    y_new=y-h*u*p/ell_+  on the plus ray,
    y_new=y-h*u*q/ell_-  on the minus ray.

It never increases. Since y_0=-R<0, EVERY query and the actual terminal
remain below the axis, and Sections3--4 apply throughout. All diagnostic
steps are strictly positive, so the decrease is strict at active queries.
Every vertical coordinate is updated by actual GD.

## 5. Complete cap/ray/polar transition law

At an active state with ray sigma and coordinates (t,z) from (3.2), take
any physical step h=1+d>1, set u=min(t,ell_sigma), and put

    v=h*u-t, eta=omega*z>0.

The new point is -v*e_sigma+z*n_sigma. Its two ray scores are

    -v, kappa*v-eta.                                    (5.1)

Consequently the FULL transition, with all boundaries, is:

* If v<0, stay on sigma with t_new=-v and z_new=z.
* If v>=0 and kappa*v<=eta, enter Z_min and remain there forever.
* If kappa*v>eta, switch to the opposite ray with

      t_new=kappa*v-eta,
      z_new=omega*v+kappa*z,
      eta_new=(1-kappa^2)*v+kappa*eta.                   (5.2)

In the last case z_new>0. The next projection is on its new ray interior
if t_new<ell_opposite, its endpoint if t_new>=ell_opposite; the normal
height is positive only for strict inequality. The staying case is
classified by the same test against ell_sigma. At v=0 or at equality
kappa*v=eta, both scores are nonpositive and the polar case applies.

These are exact formulas, not a recurrence offered as the final result.
If t<=ell_sigma then v=d*t; if t>ell_sigma then v=h*ell_sigma-t.
Thus every core overshoot, cap persistence, cap-to-core transition,
cross-ray jump, and absorption is specified. In particular every return
loses the positive reserve eta, and changing rays also changes its cap.

For mechanismI, p=q<b gives

    ell_+=ell_-=ell=sqrt(b^2+p^2),
    kappa=(b^2-p^2)/(b^2+p^2), omega=2*b*p/(b^2+p^2).

The initial polar interval is |X|<=p*R/b; outside it,
rho_0=(b*|X|-p*R)/ell. Equations(3.3),(5.1),(5.2), including the
moving vertical coordinate, are the entire symmetric transition law.
The following sections evaluate its whole-word score and also close
mechanismII without any upper bound on ell_+/ell_-.

## 6. Every original query, remote row, terminal row, and optimum row

For each actual physical point including the terminal, let

    g_i=P_D(Z_i), r_i=Z_i-g_i,
    f_i=F_D(Z_i)=<Z_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.

The single static function already specifies all data. For EVERY ordered
pair i,j of these samples, expansion gives

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,Z_j-Z_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<r_j,g_i-g_j><=0,                              (6.1)

because g_i is in D and r_j is a full normal to D at g_j. This includes
remote pairs, both orientations, every repeated return, the original
last query, and BOTH orientations involving the actual terminal.
No incoming terminal row is deleted.

At the true closest minimum Z_star, use g_star=f_star=0. Then

    Q_(i,star)=<Z_star,g_i><=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-<r_i,g_i>=-delta_i<=0.                  (6.2)

The first inequality uses the COMPLETE polar, and the second uses the
normal at0. Positive outgoing-optimum slack -<Z_star,g_i> and positive
cap height delta_i are different quantities. Both are paid here.
All Gram matrices are literal Euclidean Grams of the displayed points
and gradients, hence positive semidefinite. Translation by Z_star and
division by rho_0 give the normalized original formulation if desired;
that operation does not change the word or omit any rows.

## 7. Asymmetric comparison that retains cap changes and objective weights

Fix the initial active sign sigma_0 and NEVER change this reference.
Let C=ell_(sigma_0) and O=ell_(opposite sigma_0). The two inequalities
(3.1) imply

    O>=kappa*C and kappa*O<=C.                           (7.1)

For r>=0 define an envelope set of possible actual below-axis states:
polar states are allowed; an active sigma_0 state must have amplitude
at most r; an active opposite state must have amplitude at most kappa*r.
There is at most ONE active ray at a time. Call this condition A(r).
Initially A(t_0) holds, with t_0 equal to the true closest radius.

For a step1+d define the scalar nondecreasing envelope

    T_(d,C)(r)=max{d*min(r,C), r-(1+d)*C}.               (7.2)

It is nonnegative since its first term is nonnegative. We claim that
EVERY actual transition from A(r) satisfies A(T_(d,C)(r)).
This is a set inclusion, not an assertion that all such states can be
attained simultaneously.

First note two consequences of (5.1) for an active ray with cap J and
amplitude t<=s. If it stays on that ray, its new amplitude is at most

    (s-h*J)_+.

Indeed for t<=J the signed same-ray output is -d*t<=0; for t>=J
it is t-h*J. If it switches, its amplitude is at most

    kappa*d*min(s,J).                                   (7.3)

For t<=J, h*min(t,J)-t=d*t. For t>=J this expression decreases from
d*J, so its maximum over t<=s is d*min(s,J). The actual switch in
(5.2) subtracts eta>0; dropping eta only enlarges this upper bound.

If the source is sigma_0, the staying amplitude is at most
(r-h*C)_+<=T_(d,C)(r), and (7.3) bounds the opposite amplitude by
kappa*d*min(r,C)<=kappa*T_(d,C)(r).

If the source is opposite sigma_0, its amplitude is at most kappa*r.
The staying bound and the first inequality of (7.1) give

    (kappa*r-h*O)_+
       <=kappa*(r-h*C)_+<=kappa*T_(d,C)(r).

Its return to sigma_0 has amplitude at most
kappa*d*min(kappa*r,O). The SECOND inequality of (7.1) is essential:

    kappa*min(kappa*r,O)<=r,
    kappa*min(kappa*r,O)<=C,

using respectively kappa^2<=1 and kappa*O<=C. Therefore that return
amplitude is at most d*min(r,C)<=T_(d,C)(r). Polar states stay polar.
This proves the claimed inclusion in every case, including cap/core
boundaries and every later return.

The objective comparison also keeps its actual cap. If an A(r) state
is on sigma_0 then its value is at most phi_C(r). If it is opposite,
its value is at most phi_O(kappa*r). For all r>=0,

    phi_O(kappa*r)<=phi_C(r).                            (7.4)

Both sides vanish at0. Their derivatives with respect to r are
kappa*min(kappa*r,O) and min(r,C), and the former is no larger by
the two inequalities just proved. Integrating gives (7.4), including
every change of quadratic/affine objective branch. Thus

    A(r) implies F_D(Z)<=phi_C(r).                       (7.5)

Define r_0=t_0 and, for EACH original chronological step h_i=1+e_i,
define the auxiliary r_(i+1)=T_(e_i,C)(r_i). This is a symbolic finite
definition, never a computed trajectory. Induction on the physical
indices proves A(r_i) for EVERY i including n, and (7.5) gives

    2F_D(Z_n)/t_0^2 <= 2phi_C(r_n)/t_0^2.              (7.6)

The reference cap C and the opposite weight kappa remain fixed from the
original start. No child gets a new reference, a freely optimized
starting state, or a new cap. This resolves the clipping-imbalance
obstruction in the naive common-radius comparison: small caps and
large caps cannot be varied independently of the return angle.

The symmetric mechanism is the special case C=O. It could also use a
common physical radius for both signs, but (7.1)--(7.6) give the stronger
direction-sensitive comparison and do not discard the angle.

## 8. Complete evaluation of the auxiliary scalar whole-word envelope

Everything in this section is rederived, not imported as a scalar fact.
Write Hub(t)=t^2/2 for |t|<=1 and |t|-1/2 otherwise. Its scalar GD map
for a step1+d is

    T_d(t)=t-(1+d)*clip(t,[-1,1]).

It is continuous and odd. The image of [-r,r] is exactly [-E_d(r),E_d(r)],
where elementary extrema on its linear pieces give

    E_d(r)=d*r           for 0<=r<=1,
           d             for 1<=r<=1+2d,
           r-1-d         for r>=1+2d.                   (8.1)

Equivalently E_d(r)=max{d*min(r,1),r-1-d}. For r>=1 the extremal
magnitudes are d and |r-1-d|; for r<=1 they are d*r. Continuity and
oddness ensure that the image contains every point between its extrema.
E_d is continuous and nondecreasing, with matching formulas at both
thresholds. Equation(7.2) is exactly C*E_d(r/C).

Let Psi_j be the ACTUAL scalar chronological map for the entire word H_j,
and let F_j(r)=max_(|t|<=r)|Psi_j(t)|. This F_j is an envelope, not the
objective F_D. Every Psi_j is continuous and odd, so its interval image
is exactly [-F_j(r),F_j(r)]. Consequently

    F_0(r)=r,
    F_(j+1)=F_j composed with E_(d_j) composed with F_j. (8.2)

No maximizer is selected independently for the two children: (8.2)
composes exact interval images. In particular, composing (7.2) along
all physical steps gives

    r_n=C*F_m(t_0/C).                                    (8.3)

We now EVALUATE the bound needed for all positive amplitudes, rather
than leaving (8.2) as an unresolved terminal inequality.

8a. Hierarchy parameters and masses.

Since epsilon<=1/6144<1/100, beta>99/100. Using sqrt(2)<29/20 gives

    2<lambda<c<(49/20)*(101/100)<5/2,
    1/3<99/250<a<1/lambda<1/2.

Let v_0=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)]. The identity lambda(lambda-2)=1 and
strict increase of t(t-2) for t>2 give 0<v_0<1. Summing the literal
word gives A_0=1, A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, hence, by substitution and induction,

    A_j=(1-v_0)*2^j+v_0*c^j,
    d_j/A_j<=c-2<1/2,
    A_j<c^j for j>=1.                                   (8.4)

In particular d_j<A_j for every j. All these are symbolic identities
and inequalities with the same enclosing m.

8b. Interior absorption and exterior formula for exact interval images.

For every 0<=j<=m,

    F_j(r)<=1 for 0<=r<=A_j,
    F_j(r)=r-S_j for r>=A_j.                             (8.5)

This follows directly by induction from (8.1)--(8.2). It holds for j=0.
Assume it for j. At r>=A_(j+1)=2A_j+d_j, the first child's output
radius is r-S_j>=A_j+1+d_j>1+2d_j. The bridge therefore subtracts
1+d_j and leaves radius at least A_j. The second child subtracts S_j.
The resulting radius is r-[2S_j+1+d_j]=r-S_(j+1). At the boundary
r=A_(j+1) this is1. Monotonicity gives the interior bound at smaller
r. This proves both assertions and all boundary equalities.

8c. Exact plateau placement.

Put theta=a*beta, so 0<theta<1/2 and c*theta=beta^2<1. For 2<=j<=m,

    A_(j-1)<d_j<A_(j-1)+2d_(j-1).                     (8.6)

For the first lower inequality at j=2,

    c*(d_2-A_1)=beta*c^2-2c-beta
               =2epsilon-lambda^2*epsilon^2*(1+epsilon)>0.

The last sign follows from lambda^2<6 and epsilon<1/100, which give
6epsilon(1+epsilon)<2. Since c>lambda and lambda-1/lambda=2,
c-1/c>2. If d_j>A_(j-1), then

    d_(j+1)-A_j=(c-1/c)*d_j-2A_(j-1)>0.

This propagates the lower inequality. For the upper inequality,
d_j-2d_(j-1)=(c-2)*d_(j-1)<A_(j-1) by (8.4).

We claim

    F_j(d_j)=theta for EVERY 1<=j<=m.                  (8.7)

At j=1, d_1=beta<1 and F_1=E_a, so this is a*beta. At j>=2,
(8.5)--(8.6) send d_j through the first child to
d_j-S_(j-1), strictly between1 and1+2d_(j-1). Its bridge image radius
is exactly d_(j-1) by (8.1). The second child sends this to theta by
induction. Both complete children remain in the argument.

8d. All real interior amplitudes, including arbitrarily small ones.

For EVERY integer k>=0 and EVERY 1<=j<=m,

    F_j(d_j*theta^k)<=theta^(k+1).                     (8.8)

Prove this simultaneously in all k by induction in j. At j=1 the
input beta*theta^k is below1, so equality follows from E_a. At a later
j, k=0 is (8.7). For k>=1,

    d_j*theta^k=(c*theta)*d_(j-1)*theta^(k-1)
                <=d_(j-1)*theta^(k-1).

Monotonicity and the first-child induction give an output at most
theta^k<=1. Its bridge image radius is at most d_(j-1)*theta^k.
The second-child induction at k gives at most theta^(k+1).

For any real 0<r<=d_j, choose an integer k>=0 such that

    d_j*theta^(k+1)<r<=d_j*theta^k.

Such k exists because 0<theta<1. At a geometric endpoint use it as
the weak upper endpoint. From (8.8) and monotonicity,
F_j(r)<=theta^(k+1)<r/d_j. For d_j<r<=A_j, (8.5) gives
F_j(r)<=1<r/d_j. At r=0 the value is0. Thus uniformly on the entire
interior, with no excluded threshold or preimage,

    F_j(r)<=min(1,r/d_j), 0<=r<=A_j.                   (8.9)

This controls any number of core entries, departures and later returns
in the auxiliary map. It therefore controls all actual triangle returns
already enclosed by Section7.

8e. Evaluated normalized objective for every positive amplitude.

For 0<r<=A_m, (8.9) gives

    2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2=F_m(r)^2/r^2<=1/d_m^2<1/D_m.

The strict comparison follows without asymptotics. From a>1/3,
A_m<c^m and m>=1536>=5, one has c^m>2^m>=32>18, hence

    d_m^2>c^(2m)/9>2c^m>D_m=2A_m-1.                 (8.10)

For r>=A_m, (8.5) gives F_m(r)=r-S_m>=1 and therefore

    2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2=(2r-D_m)/r^2<=1/D_m,             (8.11)

because r^2-D_m*(2r-D_m)=(r-D_m)^2>=0. Equality in this last bound
holds exactly at r=D_m, which is in the exterior since D_m>=A_m.
We have thus proved the fully evaluated envelope bound

    2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2<=1/D_m for EVERY real r>0.          (8.12)

## 9. Whole-word score, exact attainment, and cap-active conclusion

Combine (7.6), (8.3), and phi_C(C*t)=C^2*Hub(t) for t>=0. With
r=t_0/C>0, the ACTUAL whole-word score satisfies

    E=2F_D(Z_n)/t_0^2
       <=2Hub(F_m(r))/r^2<=1/D_m.                      (9.1)

This proves the desired uniform quantitative obstruction on all starts
and all parameters of mechanismI and mechanismII. There is no remaining
supremum to bound, no terminal inequality left open, and no resetting
of the reference side or cap at a child boundary.

For exact attainment fix ANY b,p,q,R in the declared domain and choose

    X=[ell_+^2*D_m+p*R]/b, Z_0=(X,-R).

Its initial plus-ray score is t_0=ell_+*D_m and its true closest radius
is exactly that number by Section4. Let P_i=sum_(t<i) h_t, the mass of
the first i original physical entries, for 0<=i<=n. Then the actual
trajectory and projections are

    Z_i=Z_0-P_i*w_+,
    t_i=ell_+*(D_m-P_i), g_i=w_+,
    y_i=-R-p*P_i<0.                                    (9.2)

Indeed 0<=P_i<=S_m, so t_i>=ell_+*(D_m-S_m)=ell_+*A_m>ell_+.
The projection guards (3.3) apply at every point, proving the induction
for actual GD, including the terminal. The cap-normal height is

    delta_i=ell_+^2*(D_m-P_i-1)>0

at EVERY original query and at the terminal. Thus the attaining family
really uses active finite caps; this is not the previously excluded
zero-cap cone regime. The terminal value is

    F_D(Z_n)=ell_+^2*(A_m-1/2)=ell_+^2*D_m/2.

Dividing by t_0^2=ell_+^2*D_m^2 proves E=1/D_m. A corresponding
minus-ray member is obtained with X=-[ell_-^2*D_m+q*R]/b.
All ordered rows and the own closest radius are already established.

No assertion that these are the only maximizing triangle starts is
needed. The global upper bound plus these actual members proves the
exact supremum for EACH fixed geometry and R, and hence for the union
over all word-dependent choices of geometry and R.

The complete channel from accepted b98 is

    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2sum h),
       max_(0<=i<n) (h_i-1)_+^2/
                    [(1+sum_(t<i)h_t)^2*(1+2sum_(t>i)h_t)]}.

It contains every original cut. On H_m it is at least1/D_m; therefore
the exact optimum (9.1)--(9.2) is at most COMPLETE K_n and hence at
most the complete F_ray_n of accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a. We do not assume
that K_n or the complete ray channel equals its global Huber term.

Using accepted931 in its stated direction gives

    B_n*sup E=B_n/D_m<47/50 for EVERY m>=1536.

This is a family deficit, not a lower witness reaching 1/B_n, and not
B_n*W_n(H_m)<47/50. The original full-W17/(16D_m) hypothesis remains
unproved, and no minimax completion follows.

## 10. Full finite Walsh lift, all remote vertices, and true full radius

This section proves the advertised lift rather than assuming a quotient
also preserves its nearest minimum. Fix any finite integer r>=2 and the
group G=(F_2)^r. Labels are s in G; nonzero characters are indexed by
t in G\{0}, with chi_t(s)=(-1)^(t dot s), the dot product being in F_2.
Fix positive horizontal weights b_t, choose one nonzero character u,
and set b_u=b. In the real space R^(2^r), with coordinates indexed by
nonzero t followed by one vertical coordinate, put

    v_s=((b_t*chi_t(s))_(t!=0), k_s),
    k_s=p if chi_u(s)=+1, and q if chi_u(s)=-1,
    D_full=conv({0} union {v_s:s in G}).

These are fixed actual finite-dimensional vectors, not formal independent
gradients. Their full Gram entries are

    <v_s,v_v>=sum_(t!=0) b_t^2*chi_t(s)*chi_t(v)+k_s*k_v.

No character or mixed component is dropped from the body. Let E_plane
be the two-dimensional coordinate plane spanned by horizontal coordinate
u and the vertical coordinate. Start at Z_0=X*e_u-R*e_vertical.

For tau in the subgroup ker(u), let U_tau multiply horizontal coordinate
t by chi_t(tau) and fix the vertical coordinate. It is orthogonal,
permutes v_s to v_(s+tau), fixes0, and preserves D_full. Its common
fixed space is exactly E_plane: a nonzero character t is identically1
on ker(u) if and only if t=u. To justify the latter, any linear form
vanishing on the codimension-one kernel of u is a scalar multiple of u;
over F_2 the only nonzero such multiple is u itself.

Averaging U_tau over ker(u) is the orthogonal projection onto E_plane.
It maps the plus coset of vertices to (b,p) and the minus coset to
(-b,q) in that plane. For a character not trivial on the subgroup,
pairing tau with tau+tau_0 where chi_t(tau_0)=-1 proves its average0.
The averaging map sends any point of D_full into the displayed triangle.
Conversely both averaged vertices and0 belong to D_full by convexity.
It follows that

    D_full intersect E_plane = conv{0,(b,p),(-b,q)}.      (10.1)

For any Z in E_plane, its unique projection onto D_full is fixed by
every U_tau, since its images minimize the same strictly convex distance.
Thus it lies in E_plane and is exactly the triangle projection in (10.1).
The full projection potential restricted to E_plane is therefore F_D,
and its full gradient lies in the plane. Actual full-body GD stays in
that plane and agrees with the triangle trajectory at every physical
query, terminal, and vertical update. No cap is enlarged.

The complete full-space minimum is

    Z_full_min={Z:<Z,v_s><=0 for EVERY s in G},          (10.2)

by the same small-segment proof as Section2. For an active initial point,
the planar closest point Z_star from Section4 obeys every inequality
(10.2), because its vertex score depends only on chi_u(s). Also
e_sigma=w_sigma/ell_sigma belongs to cone(D_full), since w_sigma is
the actual averaged vertex in (10.1). Thus for every U in Z_full_min,
<e_sigma,U><=0, and

    <Z_0-Z_star,U-Z_star>=t_0*<e_sigma,U><=0.

The same distance expansion proves Z_star is the TRUE full-space closest
minimum and the full closest radius is t_0. There is no hidden smaller
radius in the discarded coordinates. Equivalently, uniqueness and the
group symmetries force the closest minimum to lie in the fixed plane;
the direct normal argument above avoids relying on this equivalence.

For every actual full residual r_i, <r_i,v_s-g_i> equals the plus or
minus finite guard in (3.3). All remote vertices of the ORIGINAL body
are therefore checked, and (6.1)--(6.2) hold for the full function too.
The exact score bound and attaining family (9.2) lift with unchanged
normalization. Arbitrary anisotropy in the other horizontal characters
and arbitrary finite rank, including ranks depending on m, are allowed.

This proves only the two-height invariant-plane law. It does NOT prove
the same obstruction for arbitrary multilevel heights, a noninvariant
start, or a trajectory using higher-dimensional active faces.

## 11. Audit, provenance, and the surviving interface

The decisive new ingredient is the FIXED-reference envelope A(r), with
opposite amplitude weight kappa. The two directions of cap compatibility
have separate roles: O>=kappa*C pays persistence on the opposite cap;
kappa*O<=C pays a return and the terminal objective. Their combination
permits arbitrary cap imbalance in the obtuse triangle. Replacing this
with scalar clipping at two unrelated thresholds would remove a needed
hypothesis; that unrestricted scalar family is not closed here.

The author check covers these potential failure points explicitly:

* Every below-axis face is determined by finite vertex guards, including
  the zero vertex, and a positive cap normal is retained.
* Vertical motion is the actual GD update and preserves y<0.
* The complete polar and true initial closest point are proved; both
  optimum row orientations and all remote prefix/terminal rows are kept.
* Cross-ray motion contracts by kappa and loses its real reserve eta;
  upper bounding by removal of eta never asserts realizability.
* The initial cap C is fixed through all children and all returns.
* The objective comparison uses phi_O(kappa*r), not an unweighted norm
  or an unproved monotonicity under changing caps.
* The scalar interval images are exact connected images; they do not
  select independent child maximizers. The final inequality is evaluated
  for every real amplitude, every threshold, and arbitrarily small ones.
* The auxiliary d_m adds no physical step. Enclosing m stays fixed and
  the original last leaf is included in every whole-word composition.
* The full body and its complete minimum, not just its planar section,
  satisfy the lift and the closest-radius assertion.
* Attainment uses one fixed function and delta_i>0 at every original
  query. It is a baseline member, not an improving witness.

This author audit is not an independent attack or formal verification.
There is no claim of a new accepted fact. The entire report is a single
joint unverified proof attempt because the formal allowance is zero.

Recall used current TASK.md, master_guidance7a968d92bc20b0bd, the
accepted facts named above, prior max2r72's handoff (full joint record
21d26350c9141917 is awareness), and the shared symmetric/asymmetric scalar
drafts bac703f383b0fbee and05423f48b823e3bf. The scalar material actually
needed was rederived in Section8. None of the remaining scalar draft
claims or other workers' private files was used as a premise.

Matlas search for the projection/conjugate identity returned a prox
inequality in arXiv2106.11938 Lemma12 and a Bregman projection identity
in arXiv2212.06409 Lemma2.24. Neither is an applicable input here, and
neither is cited as a theorem in the proof. Web retrieval found the
Boyd--Vandenberghe convex-optimization and Parikh--Boyd proximal-method
background pages. No external theorem is used: Section2 directly proves
every required projection-potential property. Some broad recall outputs
were truncated; targeted facts and the needed mathematical bodies were
read in bounded calls. No missing or truncated text is a premise.

The scoped conclusion excludes retuning either assigned triangle law,
including all of its cap-active trajectories, to obtain a nonvanishing
gain over complete K/ray on the specified H_m. It does NOT exclude all
cap-active simplices. A surviving geometric search would have to leave
this proved two-ray comparison domain, for example by losing the
invariant-plane/two-height structure and using additional active faces.
That is a boundary of this obstruction, not a newly assigned mechanism
or a claim that any such extension succeeds. The separate native radial
Gray search is not duplicated, read privately, or imported as a fact.

Resource accounting: zero mathematical programs, arithmetic/trajectory/
recurrence replays, numerical evaluations, optimizers, solvers, benchmark
DP, formal calls, fact_submit, installations, consults, or subagents.
Only analytical text reasoning, retrieval, own artifact writes and
administrative integrity checks were used. No active TASK overwrite,

The assigned bounded investigation ends at cap1; original target OPEN.
