# A finite Huber perturbation with paid interpolation slack

Status: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. Worker max2, round59,
under TASK.md and master_guidance7b221a98307db859. Plan80f6f693d6dac1ed.
No new fact, verifier call, numerical evaluation, mathematical program,
executed recurrence, solver, benchmark replay, consult, installation or
subagent. This is one assigned round and does not renew itself.

## 1. Result and scope

The selected perturbation is realizable at a fixed, nonzero amplitude.
It retains every physical step, every queried sample, the unqueried
terminal, both directions of every interpolation pair, and initial norm1.
Its transverse gradient is born at zero and grows with elapsed physical
step mass; its covariance is correlated. A decreasing scalar mean pays
for that growth. The payment gives an exact finite construction, rather
than a first-order direction with an uncontrolled remainder.

This mechanism does not improve Huber. For every positive finite word
with mass S>=2, its exact optimized payoff at amplitude epsilon=1/16 is
between (7/8)H_base and (49/54)H_base, where H_base=1/(1+2S).
The constructed static function has a UNIQUE minimizer at0, so this is
also its true closest-distance payoff. A necessary terminal row proves
the upper bound even if all values and the common gradient scale are
reselected. A broader terminal-row calculation excludes improvement by
any positive scalar retuning within the stated affine-clock class while
the terminal gradient still has positive initial-direction component.

On the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy this is a genuine benchmark-order
perturbation, but with a fixed loss. It supplies neither a finite
counterexample to Huber optimality nor a lower witness above1/B_n.
It excludes no nonlinear temporal covariance policy, arbitrary initial
correlation, terminal sign reversal, or unrestricted smooth-convex
function. The original all-word exact-profile problem remains OPEN.

Only accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 is used for the interpolation convention
and, AFTER all rows have been proved, its explicit conjugate interpolant.
Only accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7 is used for its separately stated
same-word Huber/benchmark comparison. Every new calculation is proved
jointly here; earlier unverified findings are not premises.

## 2. Original word, normalization, and notation

For the assigned hierarchy fix an integer m>=2 and put

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta_word=1-1/(4m),
    c=lambda*(1+1/(4m)), a_word=beta_word/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a_word*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m.

The physical word is h=H_m, with true length n=2^m-1. Every child keeps
these SAME enclosing-m parameters. There is no terminal-step deletion or
word compression. Since the first H_2 has three steps greater than1,
its mass, and hence the mass of H_m, exceeds3.

The construction below in fact works for ANY positive finite word
h=(h_0,...,h_(n-1)) whose mass S=sum_(t<n)h_t is at least2. Define

    P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t,
    T_i=sum_(t<i)h_t*P_t,       0<=i<=n,
    T=T_n, eta=T/S, M=1+2S, H_base=1/M.                 (1)

Thus P_0=T_0=0 and P_n=S. These are written finite sums, not executed
recurrences. Expanding the square of the step sum gives

    2T=S^2-sum_(t<n)h_t^2,
    0<=eta<=S/2.                                       (2)

Use Euclidean space R^2 with orthonormal vectors e0,e1. Set
x_star=g_star=0 and f_star=0; x0=e0 has norm exactly1. Smoothness is1.
For sampled vectors and values the original residual convention is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.         (3)

All ordered distinct pairs i,j in {star,0,...,n} must satisfy Q_ij<=0.
At the end, a general advertised smoothness L>0 is obtained by using
L*F and physical steps h_i/L. The normalized error stays2f_n.

## 3. One explicit finite policy

Choose an amplitude epsilon with

    0<epsilon<=1/16, alpha=1-2epsilon,
    J=1+2eta+4alpha*(S-eta),
    D=M*alpha^2+epsilon*J,
    sigma=alpha/D.                                    (4)

In particular 7/8<=alpha<1, J>0, D>0, and sigma>0. Define EVERY original
gradient, including the terminal gradient, by

    g_i=sigma*((1-2epsilon*P_i/S)*e0
                         +sqrt(epsilon)*(P_i/S)*e1).   (5)

Let

    v=sigma*e0,
    w=(sigma/S)*(-2epsilon*e0+sqrt(epsilon)*e1).

Then g_i=v+P_i*w. Define every position by the actual GD substitution:

    x_i=e0-P_i*v-T_i*w.                                (6)

Equations(1),(5),(6) give x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i*g_i exactly.
No averaging of different functions or transcripts occurs.

The full Gram matrix of (x0,g_0,...,g_n) is the Gram of these displayed
two-dimensional vectors and is therefore positive semidefinite. More
explicitly, write q_i=P_i/S. Its entries are

    <x0,x0>=1,
    <x0,g_i>=sigma*(1-2epsilon*q_i),
    <g_i,g_j>=sigma^2*((1-2epsilon*q_i)*(1-2epsilon*q_j)
                                             +epsilon*q_i*q_j). (7)

The transverse covariance is epsilon*sigma^2*q_i*q_j. It is nonzero,
positive semidefinite, and zero at the initial query. It has strong
cross-time correlations. Indeed all pairwise gradient inner products
are positive, since their e0 components are at least7sigma/8 and their
e1 components are nonnegative. This is outside the expected-orthogonality
hypothesis of accepted0e7e78056dc6258c, without using that fact as a bound.

Define one common value array by

    f_n=<g_n,x_n>-||g_n||^2/2,
    f_i=f_n+sum_(t=i)^(n-1)
                    (h_t/2)*<g_t+g_(t+1),g_t>, i<n.   (8)

Thus the exact value differences are

    f_(i+1)-f_i=-(h_i/2)*<g_i+g_(i+1),g_i>.            (9)

Every value in(8) is attached to an original sample. No value is discarded
or assigned separately for different inequality checks.

## 4. The exact terminal value and the initial scale

Equations(5),(6) give

    g_n=sigma*(alpha*e0+sqrt(epsilon)*e1),
    x_n=(1-sigma*S+2epsilon*sigma*eta)*e0
                                      -sqrt(epsilon)*sigma*eta*e1.

Expanding the terminal expression in(8) gives

    2f_n=2sigma*alpha-sigma^2*D,                        (10)

because

    D=2alpha*S+alpha^2+epsilon
                         +2epsilon*eta*(1-2alpha)
     =M*alpha^2+epsilon*[1+2eta+4alpha*(S-eta)].        (11)

The choice sigma=alpha/D therefore yields

    2f_n=alpha^2/D=sigma^2*D.                          (12)

We will need two elementary bounds, both finite and uniform:

    D>=M*alpha^2>1,
    1/sigma=D/alpha>=M*alpha>S+1.                      (13)

For the first, M>=5 and alpha>=7/8. For the second,
(7/8)(2S+1)-(S+1)=(6S-1)/8>0 when S>=2.

The factor sigma is explicitly optimized, not kept at its unperturbed
value. Hence the loss proved later cannot be repaired merely by retuning
the common scalar gradient level.

## 5. Payment for EVERY finite directed interpolation row

Put z=||w||^2>0 and

    d=-<v,w>/z=2S/(1+4epsilon),
    u=w/sqrt(z), r_i=<u,x_i>, k_i=<u,g_i>.             (14)

Here u is a unit vector; d is an auxiliary scalar, not a physical step.
Since epsilon<=1/16 and S>=2,

    d>=8S/5, d-S>=3S/5>=6/5>1.                       (15)

By(6), the projected position increments and gradient increments are

    r_(i+1)-r_i=sqrt(z)*h_i*(d-P_i)>0,
    k_(i+1)-k_i=sqrt(z)*h_i>0.                        (16)

Define k(r) on[r_0,r_n] to be the continuous function affine on each
interval[r_i,r_(i+1)] and equal to k_i at r_i. Its slope on that
interval is exactly

    1/(d-P_i) <= ell:=1/(d-S)<1.                       (17)

Thus k is nondecreasing and ell-Lipschitz. This is the new available
payment. The longitudinal decrease in(5) supplies a finite scalar
curvature margin while the transverse component grows. No signed
quadratic cost is dropped or left to an unknown suffix budget.

The perpendicular component b=v-<u,v>*u of g_i is independent of i.
On a single interval, affine integration of k and(16) gives

    <b,x_(i+1)-x_i> + integral_(r_i)^(r_(i+1)) k(r)dr
       =<(g_i+g_(i+1))/2,x_(i+1)-x_i>
       =-(h_i/2)*<g_i+g_(i+1),g_i>.

Summing these EXACT equalities and using(9), for all i<j,

    f_j-f_i=<b,x_j-x_i>+integral_(r_i)^(r_j) k(r)dr.    (18)

Let Delta=k_j-k_i=sqrt(z)*(P_j-P_i)>0. Since k has Lipschitz
constant at most ell, r_j-r_i>=Delta/ell. On the final subinterval
of length Delta/ell one has
k(r)-k_i >= Delta-ell*(r_j-r); elsewhere k(r)-k_i>=0.
Consequently

    integral_(r_i)^(r_j)(k(r)-k_i)dr>=Delta^2/(2ell).

On the initial subinterval of the same length,
k_j-k(r)>=Delta-ell*(r-r_i), so also

    integral_(r_i)^(r_j)(k_j-k(r))dr>=Delta^2/(2ell).

The difference g_j-g_i is parallel to u and has norm Delta. Applying
(18) to these two area inequalities proves BOTH original residuals:

    Q_ij <= -(d-S-1)*z*(P_j-P_i)^2/2 <0,
    Q_ji <= -(d-S-1)*z*(P_j-P_i)^2/2 <0.              (19)

This proof covers every pair, however remote, and includes j=n. It is
not an adjacent-row test or a first-order Taylor expansion. Every term
of the finite-amplitude policy has been retained.

## 6. Payment for both optimum directions

For each finite i set

    E_i=<g_i,x_i>-||g_i||^2/2-f_i.

By(8), E_n=0 and Q_(i,star)=-E_i. To check all earlier E_i, write
P=P_i, Tloc=T_i, h=h_i. From(6), (9), and g_(i+1)=g_i+h*w,
direct expansion gives

    E_(i+1)-E_i
      =h*[<w,x_i>-(1+h/2)*<w,g_i>-(h/2)*z]
      =h*z*[d*(P+1+h/2-1/sigma)
                           -Tloc-P-(h/2)*(P+1)].       (20)

For the second line use
<w,e0>/z=-d/sigma, <w,v>/z=-d, and g_i=v+P*w.
Since P+h<=S and 1/sigma>S+1 by(13), the bracket in(20) is strictly
negative. Thus E_i>E_(i+1), and E_n=0 implies

    Q_(i,star)<0 for every i<n, Q_(n,star)=0.          (21)

For the OTHER optimum direction,

    Q_(star,i)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2.

All gradient coordinates in(5) are nonnegative, with a strictly positive
e0 component. Equation(9) therefore shows f_i>=f_n. The gradient norm
decreases with P: the derivative of ||v+P*w||^2 as a written polynomial
in P is 2z*(P-d)<0 for0<=P<=S. Hence ||g_i||^2<=||g_0||^2=sigma^2.
Using(12),(13) gives the uniform strict margin

    f_i-||g_i||^2/2 >= (sigma^2/2)*(D-1)>0,
    Q_(star,i) <= -(sigma^2/2)*(D-1)<0.                (22)

This includes i=n. Equations(19),(21),(22) are every original ordered
interpolation inequality. The initially slack optimum rows have not
been omitted. The perturbation preserves their strict signs at every
allowed finite epsilon.

## 7. One static function, and the TRUE radius

Only now invoke the sufficiency construction proved in accepted
fact1252b8823bb5a290. For y in R^2 define

    K_star(y)=||y||^2/2,
    K_i(y)=<x_i,y>-f_i+||y-g_i||^2/2, 0<=i<=n,
    K(y)=max{K_star(y),K_0(y),...,K_n(y)},
    F(x)=sup_(y in R^2)(<x,y>-K(y)).                  (23)

The full Gram(7) is PSD and every row has already been proved.
The cited accepted construction therefore gives one everywhere finite
convex differentiable F with1-Lipschitz gradient, interpolating ALL the
triples(x_i,g_i,f_i), including the terminal and the optimum triple.
It has F(0)=0=min F. The physical GD trajectory of this ONE function
is exactly(6) by induction from its prescribed queried gradients.

The minimizer is actually unique. Equation(22) gives

    K_i(0)=-f_i+||g_i||^2/2<0=K_star(0)

for every finite i. There are finitely many i, so continuity gives a
neighborhood of0 on which K=K_star. Therefore the subdifferential of
K at0 is the singleton{0}. In(23) the unique maximizing y is grad F(x);
grad F(x)=0 holds exactly when x belongs to that subdifferential.
Thus argmin F={0}, and

    dist(x0,argmin F)=||e0||=1.                       (24)

This proves the closest-radius assertion instead of substituting a
convenient optimum for an unknown closer one. It also shows the advertised
smoothness1 is sharp: locally K(y)=||y||^2/2, hence F(x)=||x||^2/2 near0.
For this last claim, at sufficiently small y the differentiable K has
gradient y; strong convexity makes that y the unique maximizer for x=y.

## 8. Exact family optimum and the loss at finite amplitude

Keep S,T,epsilon and the vectors(5),(6), but allow ANY positive scalar
scale sigma and ANY common values from a legal transcript with the same
optimum sample0. The necessary original terminal row alone gives

    2f_n<=2sigma*alpha-D*sigma^2
         =alpha^2/D-D*(sigma-alpha/D)^2.              (25)

Here D depends on S,T,epsilon, not on sigma. The complete legal
construction(4)--(24) attains equality at sigma=alpha/D with true radius1.
Therefore, in the stated class with0 as its radius1 minimizer, the exact
maximum over sigma and all values is

    V_epsilon(h)=alpha^2/(M*alpha^2+epsilon*J),
    V_epsilon/H_base
          =alpha^2/(alpha^2+epsilon*J/M)<1.            (26)

This class definition retains x0=e0 and the initial gradient g0=sigma e0.
It does not secretly optimize another minimum point or a rotated initial
gradient. The attaining member already has the true closest minimum0.

At the fixed nonzero amplitude epsilon=1/16, alpha=7/8. From eta in[0,S/2]
and 2-4alpha<0,

    1+(2alpha+1)*S <= J <= 1+4alpha*S.

The right bound implies J/M<=2alpha because 1<=2alpha. For S>=1,
the left bound implies J/M>=(2alpha+2)/3; after cross multiplication
this assertion is equivalent to (2alpha-1)*(S-1)>=0. Thus

    alpha^2/(alpha^2+2epsilon*alpha)
       <=V_epsilon/H_base
       <=alpha^2/(alpha^2+epsilon*(2alpha+2)/3).

Substituting the displayed rational alpha and epsilon by symbolic
simplification gives

    7/8 <= V_(1/16)(h)/H_base(h) <=49/54<1.           (27)

These bounds concern an actual nonzero finite perturbation and hold
uniformly in the word length; no amplitude tends to zero with m.
The exact gain over Huber is NEGATIVE:

    V_epsilon-H_base
       =-epsilon*J/[M*(M*alpha^2+epsilon*J)].          (28)

In particular at epsilon=1/16 the relative loss is at least5/54.
The full expression, including every higher power of epsilon contained
in alpha, sigma and D, is retained in(26),(28). There is no remainder
to estimate or extrapolation from a tangent cone.

At epsilon=0 the limiting vectors have sigma=1/M, g_i=e0/M and
x_i=(1-P_i/M)e0. Equation(8) becomes

    f_i=(1/M)*(1-P_i/M)-1/(2M^2).

This is exactly the same-word Huber transcript. All finite rows and
all finite-to-optimum rows are then tight. The policies(4)--(8) therefore
give a genuine finite deformation of that transcript.

## 9. Retuning the scalar correction does not rescue this class

There is a broader necessary obstruction which does NOT assume(8).
Keep x0=e0, the same physical word and clocks, but choose any

    sigma>0, 0<delta<1, nu>0,
    g_i=sigma*((1-delta*P_i/S)*e0+sqrt(nu)*(P_i/S)*e1),
    x_i=e0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.                          (29)

Allow all function values to be chosen freely and require the original
optimum triple0. Set A=1-delta>0. Direct expansion of Q_(n,star)<=0 gives

    2f_n <= 2sigma*A-D_gen*sigma^2,
    D_gen=M*A^2+2delta*A*(S-eta)+nu*(1+2eta).          (30)

Both extra terms in D_gen are strictly positive. Therefore every legal
radius1 transcript in(29), with arbitrary values, satisfies

    2f_n <= A^2/D_gen <1/M.                           (31)

The equality-producing scale would be A/D_gen; its mere existence as a
number is not a claim that every delta,nu is legal. Legality and equality
were established only for the finite subfamily delta=2epsilon, nu=epsilon
in Sections3--7. The necessary bound(31) nevertheless rules out improvement
through arbitrary positive retuning of that scalar drift, covariance size,
common scale, or value array inside(29).

To see exactly where the correction matters, omit it: delta=0,nu>0.
At the first physical step, g0=sigma e0 and
g1-g0=sigma*sqrt(nu)*(h0/S)e1, whereas x1-x0=-h0*sigma e0.
For ANY two values, summing the two original rows yields

    Q_01+Q_10=||g1-g0||^2-<g1-g0,x1-x0>
             =nu*sigma^2*h0^2/S^2>0.                 (32)

Thus the zero-initial transverse covariance is not itself a legal
addition to Huber. The mean decrease really pays an otherwise impossible
row. For delta<0, the extra positive longitudinal contribution already
makes the same pair fail. The upper scope delta<1 in(29) is a substantive
restriction: a terminal sign reversal is NOT excluded by this argument.

This is a normalization/objective obstruction in an explicit class,
not a global impossibility claim. It comes from the ORIGINAL terminal
row with the full radius fixed, not from an unproved matrix sign,
orthogonality assumption, or unavailable boundary covariance budget.

## 10. Scale on the unchanged tau1/4 hierarchy

For m>=1536, accepted fact5fd396eba75651e7, Section10 and its statement,
gives for this SAME H_m

    B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_m)<237/250,
    lim_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)*H_base(H_m)
                              =Z_1/(2exp(1/4))>0.    (33)

Here B_n is the original all-split Zhang--Jiang denominator, and
Z_1=lim lambda^(-m)B_(2^m-1)>0 is its accepted unconditional ray limit.
No seed assumption, replacement benchmark, or executed benchmark
recurrence is involved. The predecessor is used ONLY for(33), not as
an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.

Combining(27),(33) proves, at every m>=1536,

    B_(2^m-1)*V_(1/16)(H_m)
                     <(49/54)*(237/250)<1.            (34)

Also

    liminf B_(2^m-1)*V_(1/16)(H_m)
                      >=(7/8)*Z_1/(2exp(1/4))>0.     (35)

Hence the prospective signal was not trivially diluted below the
benchmark scale: a fixed transverse amplitude survives at that scale.
Its exact payment has the wrong sign for improvement, by(28),(30).
Equations(34),(35) are sufficient quantitative scale information; no
new infinite-product or moment asymptotic is needed.

The original target is still whether W_n^*/U_n^ZJ tends to1, with
W_n^*=inf_(h>=0)W_n(h) and U_n^ZJ=1/B_n. The affirmative direction
needs a uniform all-word lower bound. The present family does not give
even an improved lower bound on its assigned hierarchy, much less that
separate uniform comparison.

## 11. Prior failures, literature, and what is actually new

Required provenance was recalled before choosing the policy:

- 19f0eaeb0e7c3518 retains full conditional covariance but cannot pay the
  signed costs of its original temporal kernel. Its negative H4/H5 pulse
  directions also fail original Q. No such covariance domination is used here.
- 0fda962c46583d52 requires the FULL certificate PSD after the Huber flow
  equations. The present argument constructs a primal Gram from vectors;
  it imports no unverified dual matrix or secondary-dual numerical data.
- 88f3db7b0a2bb506 passes adjacent/optimum tests but has a positive original
  three-query cycle. Section5 here proves both directions for EVERY pair.
- 75d019fd344b8136 has a remote original-row failure and a value-rigidity
  obstruction. Our one value array(8) is checked jointly in Sections5--6;
  arbitrary value changes remain covered by the terminal upper bound.
- 160f4ed4f7544996 and cebf601250c82dc2 cover specified two-complex-mode
  laws on the tau1 ruler with a deleted final step, first zero-initial
  additions and then a full common PSD metric. They are unverified and
  do not apply automatically to the present tau1/4, full-word, affine-clock
  gradient deformation. Their initial-correlation scope was not generalized.

The new available payment is(17)--(22), not a renamed conditional
covariance identity. It is finite, nonzero, includes complete remainder
terms automatically, and leads to a realized static function and exact
minimum. The cost is then exposed by(25),(30).

The query-memory, search-math-results, direct-proving and
construct-counterexamples skills guided this round. Matlas queries
returned nearby results, including nonconvex C^(1,1) interpolation and a
smooth strongly convex interpolation statement; neither was imported.
Primary web search identified Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur, "Smooth Strongly
Convex Interpolation and Exact Worst-case Performance of First-order
Methods", arXiv:1502.05666. The exact PDF and extracted text were already
present in this worker's downloads; Section2.5, Theorem4, its proof, and
Corollary1 were read. In its notation F_(0,1) means convex functions with
1-Lipschitz gradient. Its finite-pair criterion matches(3) after reversing
indices. This confirms terminology only: interpolation sufficiency in
this proof is the self-contained accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290.
No external theorem is a new mathematical premise.

Resource use: mathematical programs0; numerical evaluations0;
arithmetic replays0; executed recurrences0; solvers0; benchmark DP0;
fact_submit0; formal gates0; installations0; consult0; subagents0.
Administrative source/memory reads and text/hash persistence are separate.
Mechanism repairs0. No finite discovery matrix was evaluated or treated
as exact. No publication, finalization, revocation, shutdown, or original
Goal completion is asserted.

## 12. Glossary and handoff

All notation is local unless an accepted predecessor is explicitly cited.
lambda,beta_word,c,a_word,H_j,m,n define the unchanged physical hierarchy.
h_i are its actual steps; S is total mass; P_i and T_i are the complete
physical prefix sums in(1); eta=T_n/S; M=1+2S; H_base=1/M.
epsilon is a finite perturbation amplitude; alpha,J,D,sigma are(4).
e0,e1 are orthonormal coordinate vectors; x_i,g_i,f_i are the one joint
sample array; star is the optimum triple. v,w are the constant and
clock-increment gradient vectors. Q_ij is the original signed residual(3).
z,d,u,r_i,k_i,k(r),ell,b are the scalar projection quantities in Section5.
E_i is the outgoing-optimum height. K_i,K,F are the explicit conjugate
construction(23). V_epsilon is the optimized legal payoff(26).
delta,nu,A,D_gen belong only to the broader necessary class(29)--(31).
B_n,Z_1,U_n^ZJ,W_n,W_n^* are the original benchmark and minimax notation
as defined in the cited predecessor and Section10.

All new assertions in this file remain UNVERIFIED. The useful handoff is
a fully paid, finite, legal Huber deformation and its exact scoped failure:
positive longitudinal component plus affine dependence of all gradient
increments on elapsed mass cannot provide the desired gain when g0 is
aligned with the unit initial radius. A future separately assigned kernel
must leave at least one of these restrictions. No new round is authorized
by this handoff.

# C81: a four-step triangle with an interior-edge query

Status: complete new joint UNVERIFIED construction. No verifier call is
allocated. This is analytical continuation, cumulative round19/19, of
the CLOSED C79 discovery allocation. No optimization, candidate sweep,
or exact numerical replay is performed here. The only fixed schedule
specialized below is the already saved record100.

The accepted fact04219554e41d9750, Section10, supplies the original
primitive energy and its full certificate identity. All new geometry and
energy calculations below are proved directly rather than imported from
the unverified C79/C80 drafts.

## 1. General actual function and four-step trajectory

Let h=(a,b,c,d), with a>1, b>1, c>1, d>=0. Define

 B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, s=a+b+c+d, A=1+s.

Choose u>0 and z>0 satisfying

 u>=B,
 z^2 <= (u+1)((a-1)u-1),
 z^2 >= C d(D-BC)/D^2.                                  (1)

The second inequality implies (a-1)u-1>0. The third imposes
no additional restriction if its right side is negative. Put

 p=(u,0), q=(-1,z), r=(-BC/D,0), K=conv{p,q,r},
 F(x)=max_(g in K) {<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.                      (2)

This nondegenerate compact triangle contains0 in the relative interior
of its horizontal base. Completing the square shows that the unique
maximizer in (2) is the Euclidean projection P_K(x).

For completeness, if p_x=P_K(x), p_y=P_K(y), the two projection
normal inequalities give

 ||p_x-p_y||^2 <= <p_x-p_y,x-y>.

Hence P_K is1-Lipschitz. The maximizing formula gives

 0<=F(x+t)-F(x)-<p_x,t>
       <=<P_K(x+t)-p_x,t><=||t||^2,

so F is differentiable with gradient P_K; it is convex as a supremum
of affine functions. Since0 belongs to K, F>=0 and F(0)=0. Moreover
P_K(x)=0 exactly when x is in the normal cone of K at0. Pairing x
with both signs of the horizontal base forces its first coordinate
to vanish; pairing it with q then forces its second coordinate<=0.
Thus argmin F={(0,t):t<=0}.

The proposed ORIGINAL iterates and gradients are

 x0=(a u-1,z),       g0=(u,0),
 x1=(-1,z),         g1=(-1,z),
 x2=(B,-Bz),        g2=(B,0),
 x3=(-BC,-Bz),      g3=(-BC/D,0),
 x4=(-BC/D,-Bz),    g4=(-BC/D,0).                        (3)

The four equations x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i follow by subtraction.
The initial point has positive second coordinate, so its closest
minimizer is0 and its squared distance is(a u-1)^2+z^2.

We check every required projection normal, including both terminal
orientations implicitly through the single actual function.

At x0, the residual y0=x0-g0=((a-1)u-1,z) has

 <q-p,y0>=z^2-(u+1)((a-1)u-1)<=0,
 <r-p,y0>=(-BC/D-u)((a-1)u-1)<0.

At x1 the residual is0. At x2, g2 is on the base because
-BC/D<0<B<=u, and y2=x2-g2=(0,-Bz) has zero pairing with
both base directions and pairing-Bz^2<0 with q-g2.

At x3 the residual is y3=(-BCd/D,-Bz). Its base comparison
with p-r is nonpositive, and

 <q-r,y3>=B{C d(D-BC)/D^2-z^2}<=0.

At x4 the residual y4=(0,-Bz) has zero pairing with the base
and pairing-Bz^2<0 with q-r. These are all vertices of K,
so (3) gives the actual gradient of the same F at every query.

For all five indices define f_i=F(x_i)=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
Every original ordered interpolation residual has the exact form

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
     =<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                              (4)

This follows from the proved projection normals. The optimum sample0
also obeys all ordered comparisons. Thus no guessed rank or deleted
gradient is needed to certify legality.

## 2. Exact intrinsic energy and a necessary family of inequalities

Use the energy from accepted04219554e41d9750, Section10:

 V_h=2sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(f_i-f4)
       +sum_(i=0)^3 h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g4||^2.

The identity

 ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2
     =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2

follows from (3) and f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.
Summing it gives

 V_h=||x0||^2-||x4||^2-2s f4-s(s+1)||g4||^2.

Here f4=||g4||^2/2, ||x4||^2=(BC/D)^2+B^2z^2, and therefore

 V_h=(a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2-A^2(BC/D)^2.                 (5)

Consequently the original full primitive cone must satisfy

 (a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2 >= A^2(BC/D)^2                  (6)

for EVERY u,z obeying(1). Indeed its accepted exact identity is
V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>, where Lambda_ij>=0,
the flow holds, and M is PSD in all five original gradients.
The right side is nonnegative and every Q_ij<=0, so it implies V_h>=0.
A negative value in(5) contradicts this identity directly.

This is a necessary family, not a sufficient condition. The imposed
a,b,c>1 is a scope assumption of the witness and is NOT a lossless
restriction of the full four-step search domain.

## 3. Exact rejection of saved discovery record100

Keep the already proposed schedule

 h=(8/5,2,471/250,309/250),   s=168/25,   A=193/25.

Thus B=1,C=221/250,D=559/250. Choose u=2,z=3/5.
The upper inequality in(1) is

 z^2=9/25 <=3/5=(u+1)((a-1)u-1).

For its lower inequality,

 C d(D-C)/D^2
    =(221*309*338)/(250*559^2) < 9/25,

because 221*309*338=23081682 <28123290=90*559^2.
All numbers in(2)--(4) are therefore rational. Explicitly,

 K=conv{(2,0),(-1,3/5),(-221/559,0)},

 x0=(11/5,3/5),   g0=(2,0),
 x1=(-1,3/5),     g1=(-1,3/5),
 x2=(1,-3/5),     g2=(1,0),
 x3=(-221/250,-3/5), g3=(-221/559,0),
 x4=(-221/559,-3/5), g4=(-221/559,0).

The exact values, if desired independently of(4), are

 f0=12/5, f1=17/25, f2=1/2,
 f3=(d+1/2)(221/559)^2, f4=(221/559)^2/2.

Equation(5), with B=1, gives

 V_h=(11/5)^2-(193*221/(25*559))^2
    =-(11908*73398)/(625*559^2)<0.                        (7)

The comparison is exact:193*221=42653>30745=55*559.
The initial closest-minimizer distance squared is26/5. The terminal
gradient/value and the displayed negative energy concern the intrinsic
primitive criterion. They do not assert an objective minimax theorem.

This rejects the precise candidate left in the round18 handoff.
The handoff's numerical rank/value observations were only motivation;
no numerical Gram, tolerance, PSD repair or approximate identity is a
premise of this proof. No new schedule has been proposed or evaluated.

## 4. Exact one-parameter elimination when b=2

For1<a<=2, b=2, c>1 and d>0 with D>C, put

 L_81=C d(D-C)/D^2>0,
 u_81=[2-a+sqrt(a^2+4(a-1)L_81)]/[2(a-1)].

The function H(u)=(u+1)((a-1)u-1) is strictly increasing for
u>=1/(a-1); its derivative there is at least a>0. Its positive
root for H(u)=L_81 is exactly u_81. Because L_81>0,
u_81>1/(a-1)>=1. Hence u=u_81,z=sqrt(L_81) is allowed in(1).
Every full primitive certificate in this regime therefore forces

 a u_81-1 >= A C/D.                                    (8)

The left side is positive, so(8) is equivalent to(6) in this
particular specialization. This explicitly eliminates the witness
parameters; it is not a complete classification of b=2 schedules,
let alone of all four-step schedules.

## 5. Retrieval and correctness boundary

Before proving, fact_search found the accepted conditional bridge and
the general interpolation fact1252b8823bb5a290, but no accepted version
of(1)--(8). Shared-memory search found C80 and older projection-lift
drafts, whose claims are not used as facts here.

The required Matlas query returned three unrelated descent results;
none is used. The existing exact local copy of Taylor--Hendrickx--Glineur,
arXiv1502.05666, Sections2.4--2.5 and the proof of Theorem4, was reread
for the conjugate construction. All needed projection/differentiability
claims are proved above, so this candidate imports no external theorem.

The original four-step existence question and original asymptotic
target remain OPEN. Accepted04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional lift
if a different actual full seed is found. No fact_submit is authorized
or performed for this draft.


# C82: exact elimination of the C81 witness parameters

Status: UNVERIFIED joint extension. For the complete construction and
proof, concatenate the C81 proof with this file. C81 is not an accepted
fact; its geometry is part of the joint proof, not an imported premise.
There are no new word proposals, optimizer calls or numerical replays.

Let h=(a,b,c,d), a,b,c>1,d>=0, and put

 alpha=a-1, B=b-1, C=c-1, D=d+1, A=1+a+b+c+d,
 H(u)=(u+1)(alpha u-1),
 ell=max{0,C d(D-BC)/D^2},
 R=[2-a+sqrt(a^2+4alpha ell)]/(2alpha),
 U=max{B,R}, K0=A^2(BC/D)^2.

Define three scalar coefficients

 q2=a^2+(1-B^2)alpha,
 q1=-2a+(1-B^2)(a-2),
 q0=B^2-K0,

and q(u)=q2 u^2+q1 u+q0.

## 1. Feasible parameter domain

The C81 triangle has parameters u>=B,z>0 with

 ell<=z^2<=H(u).

Since alpha>0, H has its only nonnegative root at1/alpha;
H'(u)=2alpha u+a-2>=a>0 for u>=1/alpha.
Solving H(u)=ell therefore gives R>=1/alpha exactly as above.
The closure of the admissible parameter domain is

 u>=U, ell<=z^2<=H(u).                                  (1)

Every boundary point in(1) with z=0 is a limit of points with
u>U,z>0 obeying the original normal inequalities. In particular
H(u)>ell for u>U. Taking such a limit in the explicit polynomial
energy is justified by continuity; no assertion about strict normals
at the limiting point is needed.

## 2. Closed formula for the infimum over this entire witness family

The energy is

 E(u,z)=(a u-1)^2+(1-B^2)z^2-K0.

If0<B<=1, its smallest value at fixed u uses z^2=ell.
Moreover a u-1>0 throughout u>=U>=1/alpha, so that value is
strictly increasing in u. Its infimum over the family is

 E_min=(a U-1)^2+(1-B^2)ell-K0.                         (2)

If B>1, its smallest value at fixed u uses z^2=H(u).
Expanding the resulting expression gives exactly q(u). Hence:

 * If q2<0, or q2=0 and q1<0, then E_min=-infinity.
 * If q2=0 and q1>=0, then E_min=q(U).
 * If q2>0, set u_opt=max{U,-q1/(2q2)}. Then
   E_min=q(u_opt).                                      (3)

These statements follow respectively from the leading term, from
linearity on the half-line, and from completing the square
q(u)=q2(u+q1/(2q2))^2+q0-q1^2/(4q2).

Every original full primitive certificate with a,b,c>1,d>=0
therefore must satisfy E_min>=0, with E_min defined by(2)--(3).
If E_min<0, an actual finite u and z>0 give a negative energy:
for an attained strict negative value this follows directly or by
continuity from a zero-z boundary; for E_min=-infinity take a
sufficiently large finite u. The C81 normals then give an actual
convex1-smooth function with a minimizer. This is a completely
parameter-eliminated necessary test for this particular family.

## 3. A broad monotone regime

Suppose1<a<=2 and1<b<=1+sqrt(3). If B<=1, formula(2)
already applies. If1<B<=sqrt(3), then

 q2>=a^2-2(a-1)=(a-1)^2+1>0,
 q'(1/alpha)=a[2/alpha+1-B^2]>=0,

because alpha<=1 and B^2<=3. Since q2>0, q' increases;
thus q is nondecreasing throughout u>=U>=1/alpha.
Consequently no stationary-point alternative is needed in this
regime: E_min=q(U) if B>1. If additionally R>=B, then H(U)=ell
and BOTH cases reduce to the same explicit radical inequality

 (a R-1)^2+(1-B^2)ell >= A^2(BC/D)^2.                    (4)

At B=1 it specializes to Section4 of C81. These are conditional
parameter statements. No unverified global-domain reduction is used
to claim that an arbitrary four-step seed lies in this regime.

## 4. Exact scope of the remaining gap

Eliminating the witnesses is not eliminating the original twenty
nonnegative multipliers. The displayed tests are necessary because
each negative case yields an actual trajectory; their nonnegative
cases certify only that this particular triangle family cannot reject
the schedule. No converse to the full flow/PSD interface is proved.
In particular failure to derive a contradiction from(2)--(4) does not
give a seed, and a negative saved optimization score does not give a
global impossibility theorem.

# Full original-space squares with jointly selected terminal pooling

JOINT UNVERIFIED analytical report, worker max, invocation82.
Original minimax Goal OPEN. No new fact or verifier verdict.

TASK.md and master_guidance5fee1ea5f63fd8fc authorize one invocation,
at most two substantive analytical laws, and zero mathematical programs,
numerical evaluations, recurrence/trajectory replays, solvers, formal calls,
fact_submit, installations, consults, or subagents. Those restrictions were
retained. All the new derivations below are one joint unverified attempt;
none is being adopted as an accepted intermediate fact.

## 1. Outcome and precise scope

We first specify positive square factors on the ENTIRE original space,
then match them to original interpolation rows. The factorization is not
an unknown PSD block, an arbitrary Cholesky factor, or a temporal quotient.
Every queried gradient, both child mean directions and their physical
checkpoint, every leaf, the common mode, the initial vector, and the
unqueried terminal are retained.

Law I uses a complete physical-tree multiresolution factor and a terminal
distribution positive at every prefix sample and the optimum. Its literal
coefficient matching has a negative original multiplier: either the first
query to the last queried leaf, or the first query to the root checkpoint.
The failure holds for arbitrary positive weights on ALL the separate
contrasts, not just the two-parameter weight law displayed below. A stronger
first-row invariant excludes every balanced-skew re-selection producing
this SAME factor and terminal distribution. Increasing q82 does not repair it.

Law II changes the matching by an explicit chronology-dependent skew term,
changes the terminal distribution to increasing weights on actual query
times, and pays the resulting NONZERO initial/common derivative. Its full
square identity contains the exact global charge tau^2/(2kappa). The two
literal original signs lambda_(0,z)>=0 and lambda_(z,z-2)>=0 already force
q82>1/(128n). For EVERY assigned m>=1536, qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).
Thus no member of this second literal law has both full PSD and all
nonnegative multipliers at the target. An additional balanced-skew
re-selection of Law II is outside this second exclusion.

The two results exclude two specified square/matching architectures.
They do not exclude the full dual cone, prove a lower bound on W, produce
an interpolable primal witness, or resolve the original minimax question.
They do not invoke the previous contact-routed or LCA receiver arrays.

## 2. Accepted inputs and the unchanged problem

For a predetermined nonnegative word h of length n, W_n(h) is the supremum
of 2(f(x_n)-f(x_star))/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), over every finite dimension,
convex differentiable L-smooth function with an attained minimizer x_star,
and x_0!=x_star, with x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/L)grad f(x_i). L>0 is fixed.
Thus W=2R. The original question is whether

    lim_(n->infinity) B_n inf_(h in [0,infinity)^n) W_n(h)=1.

The benchmark is the original ALL-integer-split recurrence

    C_0=B_0=1,
    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt(u^2+6uv+v^2))/2,
    G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,
    C_n=max_(0<=k<n) Omega(C_k,C_(n-k-1)),
    B_n=max_(0<=k<n) G(C_k,B_(n-k-1)), U_n^ZJ=1/B_n.

Accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies the original interpolation
orientation, the independent-vector certificate interpretation, and the
exact incoming-terminal-free completion. It removes NO prefix row.
Accepted fact931b9653ce0f7201 supplies the same-word benchmark comparison
B_n/D<47/50 and its CONDITIONAL implication from W_n(H_m)<=17/(16D).
That full-class upper hypothesis remains unproved. Its elementary mass
formula is also available. No other mathematical fact is a premise here.

Fix an integer m>=1536. Throughout this report use the SAME enclosing m:

    ell=1+sqrt(2), eps=1/(4m), beta=1-eps,
    c=ell*(1+eps), a=beta/c,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,
    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, S=sum_(i<n)h_i, D=1+2S,
    qtar82=17/(16D), z=n-1, r=2^(m-1)-1, b=1+a.

Here z is the last QUERIED leaf, r is the whole-word checkpoint, and n
is the ACTUAL terminal. All hierarchy symbols in this report are local.
The physical step at i is h_i=1+a*c^v2(i+1), where v2 is the exponent
of 2 dividing a positive integer. Put Ppos_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and
t_i=1+Ppos_i for 0<=i<n. The column t is an auxiliary clock, not time
or a gradient. Write e for the all-ones column of length n and e_i for
its coordinate unit columns. Superscript T denotes transpose.
The symbol 1_E is the indicator of a stated condition E, and 1_J is the
coordinate indicator column of an index set J. I denotes the n-by-n
identity matrix. A quadratic y^T K y for vector entries means
sum_(i,j) K_ij <y_i,y_j>.

The following elementary bounds will suffice:

    12/5<c<5/2, 7/8<beta<1, 1/3<a<1/2,
    4/3<b<3/2, n>=15, S>n.

Indeed sqrt(2)>7/5, and sqrt(2)<17/12 yields
ell<29/12; multiplying by 1+1/6144 still gives c<5/2.
Then a>(7/8)/(5/2)=7/20>1/3; and a<1/ell<1/2.
Every step exceeds one. The displayed n bound follows from m>=1536.

## 3. Full original coordinates; no projection or quotient

Normalize L=1 and the optimum jet to x_star=g_star=f_star=0. Retain
ARBITRARY independent vectors x_0,g_0,...,g_n in a real Euclidean space.
Every position always means the polynomial

    x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t*g_t.

For distinct original labels define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.

Actual legal jets obey Q_ij<=0. A certificate at level q82 requires
lambda_ij>=0, finite divergence out_i-in_i=2*1_(i=n), and PSD of

    M(q82)=q82||x_0||^2-2f_n+sum_(i!=j)lambda_ij Q_ij

on ALL these independent vectors after the value cancellation.

For each queried i<n define y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2.
Set y_star=delta_star=0. Define the lower triangular matrix T82 by

    T82_ii=1, T82_it=h_t for t<i, T82_it=0 for t>i.

Write g=(g_0,...,g_(n-1))^T and y=(y_0,...,y_(n-1))^T; in matrix
products h also denotes its column. As vector-column identities,

    y=e*x_0-T82g,  g=T82^(-1)(e*x_0-y),  T82e=t.             (3.1)

T82 has diagonal one, so (x_0,y_0,...,y_(n-1),g_n) and the original
variables are in invertible correspondence. In particular y is an
arbitrary n-tuple once x_0 is fixed. No equal-gradient relation is imposed.

Written finite products define

    pi_i=product_(t<i)(1-h_t),
    k_i=h_i*product_(i<t<n)(1-h_t), 0<=i<n.

In particular the inverse in (3.1) has the literal vector formula

    g_i=pi_i*x_0-y_i
             +sum_(j<i)h_j*product_(j<t<i)(1-h_t)*y_j.  (3.1a)

An empty product is one. Thus even the coordinate reconstruction is
given by finite scalar coefficients, not an unspecified inverse solve.

Expanding x_(i+1)=(1-h_i)x_i+h_i*y_i inductively, without evaluating a
recurrence on a machine, gives

    x_n=pi_n*x_0+sum_(i<n)k_i*y_i,
    sum_i k_i=1-pi_n, T82^T k=h.                       (3.2)

For the second identity, the coefficient sum on a suffix equals one
minus the suffix product: expand each h_i=1-(1-h_i) and telescope.
Thus k_i+h_i sum_(j>i)k_j=h_i, proving the last identity.

There are n physical factors 1-h_i=-a*c^v2(i+1). Also
sum_(i<n)v2(i+1)=sum_(j=1)^(m-1)(2^(m-j)-1)=n-m.
Since n is odd, this proves the EXACT full-word product

    pi_n=-s82,  s82=beta^n/c^m>0.                        (3.3)

Only an identity for the literal physical word is asserted; s82 is not
being substituted for a worst-case contraction factor.

Choose a parameter p82 with s82<p82<1, and a probability distribution
alpha_i>=0 on queried indices with sum_i alpha_i=1-p82. The terminal
weight at the optimum will be alpha_star=p82. Define

    v=k-alpha,
    w_i=p82+sum_(j<=i)alpha_j,
    Rvec_i=h_i*w_i-alpha_i,
    U82=sum_i Rvec_i=t^T v,
    gamma=2*p82/(p82+s82), eta=(p82-s82)/(p82+s82).                  (3.4)

Then

    e^T v=p82+s82, T82^T v=Rvec, gamma=1+eta,
    Rvec_i=h_i*(p82+sum_(j<i)alpha_j)+(h_i-1)alpha_i>0.

These identities follow from (3.2) and sum alpha=1-p82. In particular U82>0.
The exact terminal center in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is

    V=x_n-sum_i alpha_i*y_i=-s82*x_0+v^T y.            (3.5)

Every queried target and the optimum will have positive terminal weight
in both laws. Neither law forces lambda_(n,n-1)=2.

## 4. Signed coefficient matching and its exact full identity

This section gives a literal algebraic parameterization of the two laws,
including the entries that FAIL nonnegativity. It is not a claim that a
positive square factor necessarily has nonnegative row coefficients.

Let K be a specified symmetric matrix with Ke=0. In the two laws K is
given by explicit positive squares, never by an unknown PSD condition.
Let A be a specified skew matrix, A^T=-A, and put d=Ae. Thus e^T d=0.
Define the fully specified matrices

    Pmat=K+gamma*v*v^T,
    Nmat=T82^T(Pmat+A).

To make clear that this is a finite coefficient formula, each entry is

    Nmat_ij=K_ij+A_ij+h_i sum_(u>i)(K_uj+A_uj)
                                      +gamma*Rvec_i*v_j.   (4.1)

The FULL original signed row array is

    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i, i<n,
    lambda_(j,n)=0, j in {star,0,...,n-1},
    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij, i!=j<n,
    lambda_(i,star)=mu_i=(Nmat e)_i=2*p82*Rvec_i+(T82^T d)_i,
    lambda_(star,i)=nu_i=(Nmat^T e)_i-2alpha_i
                      =(K t)_i+gamma*U82*v_i-(A t)_i-2alpha_i,
    lambda_(i,i)=0.                                      (4.2)

This lists every entry; no additional rows, diagonal multipliers, or
completion freedom are presumed. Finite sums are definitions, not
numerical or recurrence executions.

All value-flow cancellations hold even before sign checking. At a prefix
node the total outgoing coefficient is Nmat_ii, and total incoming
PREFIX coefficient is Nmat_ii-2alpha_i. Thus prefix divergence is
2alpha_i. The incoming terminal edge has coefficient 2alpha_i, giving
zero divergence at that original queried node. Terminal divergence is
2*p82+2sum alpha_i=2. The optimum divergence is -2. All function values
cancel in M(q82), without imposing any relation among them.

Direct expansion yields Q_ij=delta_i-delta_j+<g_i,y_j-y_i> for prefix
indices, including star. Substituting (4.2), its surviving quadratic is
-g^T Nmat y. Incoming-optimum rows have g_star=0; their value contribution
is nevertheless present in the preceding flow calculation. From (3.1),

    -g^T Nmat y
       =-<x_0,e^T(Pmat+A)y>+y^T Pmat y,
    e^T Pmat=2*p82*v^T, e^T A=-d^T.

Accepted1252b8823bb5a290, or direct expansion of the displayed outgoing
terminal rows, now gives the ENTIRE original identity

    M(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2+R(q82),
    R(q82)=y^T K y+eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2
                         +(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2+<x_0,d^T y>.  (4.3)

For direct checking, before completing the common square the prefix form is

    (q82-s82^2)||x_0||^2+2(s82-p82)<x_0,v^T y>
                              +eta||v^T y||^2+y^TKy+<x_0,d^Ty>.

The equality eta(p82+s82)=p82-s82 completes it to (4.3). This is an identity
on arbitrary original x_0,g_0,...,g_n, not merely a legal trajectory.

The common-gradient derivative is also explicit. Set every queried
gradient to the same arbitrary vector G. Then y_i=x_0-t_i G and
v^Ty=(p82+s82)x_0-U82 G. Therefore the FULL prefix restriction is

    R_common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-<x_0,(d^T t)G>
                                      +(t^TKt+eta U82^2)||G||^2. (4.4)

In particular an imbalance d is not free: it changes the derivative in
G at G=0 by -(d^T t)x_0. Both optimum orientations are in (4.2), and
their true contribution has not been replaced by a contact assumption.

## 5. Law I: complete physical-tree squares, with separate boundaries

Use uniform queried terminal weights

    alpha_i=u=(1-p82)/n, alpha_star=p82.                    (I.1)

For each actual dyadic cell

    I_(j,bidx)={bidx*2^j,...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2},
    2<=j<=m, 0<=bidx<2^(m-j),

let Lcell and Rcell be its genuine left and right queried child sets,
each of size l_j=2^(j-1)-1, and let rootcell be its actual checkpoint.
Define two coefficient columns of length n, zero outside the cell:

    psi^-=(1_Lcell-1_Rcell)/sqrt(2l_j),
    psi^+=(1_Lcell+1_Rcell-2l_j e_rootcell)
                                      /sqrt(2l_j(2l_j+1)).  (I.2)

The first contrast retains the two child means independently. The second
retains the checkpoint relative to both children. Each has sum zero and
norm one, and their inner product is zero. A descendant contrast has sum
zero inside a child on which an ancestor contrast is constant. Thus all
these columns are mutually orthonormal. Inducting on a cell, its two
children contribute 2(l_j-1) details, the displayed columns contribute
two, and its common column contributes one, totaling 2l_j+1 coordinates.
The singleton H_1 cells need just their common column. Consequently
there are n-1 columns in (I.2), and together with e/sqrt(n) they form
an orthonormal basis of the ENTIRE original queried-coordinate space.
Explicitly, for a cell beginning at o=bidx*2^j, Lcell is
{o,...,o+2^(j-1)-2}, rootcell=o+2^(j-1)-1, and Rcell is
{o+2^(j-1),...,(bidx+1)*2^j-2}.

The literal two-parameter weight law is, for kappa>0 and theta>0,

    omega^-_(j,bidx)=kappa*c^(-j),
    omega^+_(j,bidx)=theta*kappa*c^(-j),
    K=sum_cells[omega^- psi^- (psi^-)^T
                                      +omega^+ psi^+ (psi^+)^T], A=0. (I.3)

Every square coefficient is explicitly positive at every depth. The
stronger exclusion below permits independently arbitrary positive omega
at every column, so it covers any retuning within this complete basis.
That extension does not insert an unknown PSD block.

Equations (4.1)--(4.2) give EVERY original row coefficient. In this law
mu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i>0; terminal weights are strictly positive. The incoming
optimum coefficients are literally

    nu_i=sum_cells,sign omega*psi_i*(sum_j psi_j t_j)
                                                +gamma U82 v_i-2u.  (I.4)

They are signed matching coefficients, not silently assumed nonnegative.
An original finite coefficient will necessarily be negative regardless
of whether these nu_i have acceptable signs.

For every q82>=p82^2 the complete factor is

    M_I(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2
       +sum_cells,sign omega||sum_i psi_i y_i||^2
       +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2+(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2.      (I.5)

This supplies all details and the common mode, since e^Tv=p82+s82>0 and
eta>0. With q82>p82^2 it is positive definite in all the original variables;
at q82=p82^2 it has the stated explicit nonnegative factorization. No child
PSD premise remains to prove. The only failed certificate requirement
will be the signs of the matched interpolation multipliers.

### 5.1 An exact first forced sign, with the physical root retained

Let omega^- and omega^+ now denote the two weights at the WHOLE word.
Only those two contrasts can contain both endpoints 0,z, or endpoint 0
and root r. Since the child sizes are (n-1)/2,

    K_0z=(-omega^-+omega^+/n)/(n-1),
    K_0r=-omega^+/n.                                  (I.6)

Ke=0 implies (T82^T K)_0j=(1-b)K_0j=-a K_0j.
Here Rvec_0=b*p82+a*u>0. The exact two signed entries are therefore

    E_z=Nmat_0z=a*(omega^--omega^+/n)/(n-1)
                                                   +gamma Rvec_0 v_z,
    E_r=Nmat_0r=a*omega^+/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_r.           (I.7)

The last queried leaf has k_z=b, so v_z=b-u>1. The root has a suffix
equal to the SAME-parameter H_(m-1). Write l=2^(m-1)-1. The product
calculation in (3.3), applied with that unchanged a,beta,c, gives

    k_r=-(a+c^(-(m-1)))*beta^l,
    v_r=k_r-u>-(a+1)-1/n>-2.                         (I.8)

Consequently Bpair=v_r+(n-1)v_z>n-3>0, and (I.7) gives

    E_r+(n-1)E_z=a*omega^-+gamma Rvec_0 Bpair>0.       (I.9)

This identifies a negative ORIGINAL multiplier for every parameter choice:
if E_z>0, lambda_(0,z)=-E_z<0; if E_z<=0, (I.9) forces
lambda_(0,r)=-E_r<0. All denominators and prefactors used here are positive.
The conclusion holds for every m>=1536, every s82<p82<1, every q82>=p82^2,
every kappa,theta>0, and even independently arbitrary positive detail
weights. Descendant weights cannot alter (I.6). Raising q82 cannot alter
the sign because it changes only the last x_0 square in (I.5).

The excluded rows are actual remote queried-prefix rows. They are not
incoming-terminal rows, omitted leaves, or fictitious child interfaces.

### 5.2 All coefficient re-selections of this same factor also fail

There is a useful stronger scope statement. Keep the exact factor (I.5)
and exact terminal distribution (I.1), but allow any other original prefix
row coefficients matching that polynomial. If their row matrix is Nnew,
put Bnew=T82^(-T)Nnew-Pmat. Comparing y-y and x_0-y coefficients in (4.3)
forces Bnew to be skew and e^T Bnew=0, equivalently Bnew e=0. Conversely
every such balanced-skew term leaves the same polynomial unchanged.
Thus this argument describes all coefficient re-selections of this fixed
factor, rather than assuming the symmetric matching is unique.

For any balanced-skew Bnew, the first diagonal change is

    (T82^T Bnew)_00=(1-b)Bnew_00+b(e^T Bnew)_0=0,

and the first row-sum change is zero. Hence the sum of its first-row
off-diagonal N entries is invariant and equals

    mu_0-Nmat_00
       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+s82-v_0)
       =a*K_00+gamma Rvec_0*(p82+u-s82/a).                (I.10)

Here k_0=b*s82/a, since pi_n=-s82=(1-b)*product_(1<=t<n)(1-h_t).
The last factor is strictly positive throughout the parameter domain.
Indeed p82+(1-p82)/n is increasing in p82; at p82=s82,

    n*(p82+u-s82/a)=1-s82*(n*(1/a-1)+1)>0.

For the strict last bound, a>1/3 gives n*(1/a-1)+1<2n+1<2^(m+1),
and s82<c^(-m) gives its product with s82 less than 2*(5/6)^m<1.
The inequality (6/5)^4=1296/625>2 proves this for every m>=4.
Also K_00>0, because the complete basis has a nonzero contrast at 0
and every detail weight is positive. Thus (I.10)>0.

If ALL original lambda_(0,j) were nonnegative, then all off-diagonal
Nnew_0j would be nonpositive and their sum could not be positive.
This contradiction excludes EVERY nonnegative coefficient matching of
this particular full square factor and its fixed terminal distribution.
It does not exclude a changed square factor or a changed terminal pool.

## 6. Law II: chronological terminal pool and a paid skew correction

Choose the positive increasing distribution on ACTUAL prefix indices

    alpha_i=2(i+1)(1-p82)/[n(n+1)], 0<=i<n,
    alpha_star=p82.                                      (II.1)

Its sum is 1-p82 because sum_(i=0)^(n-1)(i+1)=n(n+1)/2.
Its chronological cumulative is

    w_i=p82+(1-p82)(i+1)(i+2)/[n(n+1)].

This is not a distribution on a handful of selected terminals. Every
genuine prefix sample participates; every physical leaf and checkpoint
is still independent. In particular alpha_i<2/n.

Choose kappa>0 and tau>=0, and set

    K=kappa*(I-e e^T/n),
    d=tau*(e_z-e_0),
    A=(d e^T-e d^T)/n.                               (II.2)

Then A^T=-A and Ae=d. This is a literal dense chronology correction,
not an unidentified circulation or an uncharged antisymmetric term.
The imbalance at the original first and last queried samples changes
the common-gradient derivative and the optimum coefficients.

For a completely scalar specification, put

    Cclock_i=1+h_i*(n-1-i),
    dtilde_0=a*tau,
    dtilde_i=h_i*tau for 0<i<z,
    dtilde_z=tau,
    tbar=(sum_i t_i)/n.

All original finite coefficients come from the literal formula

    Nmat_ij=kappa*(1_(i=j)+h_i*1_(j>i)-Cclock_i/n)
            +gamma Rvec_i v_j+(dtilde_i-Cclock_i*d_j)/n,
    lambda_(i,j)=-Nmat_ij for i!=j<n.                 (II.3)

The optimum coefficients and terminal coefficients are

    mu_i=2*p82 Rvec_i+dtilde_i>0,
    nu_i=kappa*(t_i-tbar)+gamma U82 v_i-d_i*tbar
                                      +tau*Ppos_z/n-2alpha_i,
    lambda_(n,star)=2*p82, lambda_(n,i)=2alpha_i,
    lambda_(j,n)=0, lambda_(i,i)=0.                   (II.4)

They follow from (4.2), using d^T t=tau*Ppos_z. This specifies ALL
ordered entries, including both optimum orientations. The nu entries
remain signed matching expressions; their signs are not assumed.
The value-flow calculation of Section 4 applies without modification.

Write ybar=(sum_i y_i)/n. Completing ALL detail squares in (4.3) gives
the single global identity

    M_II(q82)=||g_n+s82*x_0-v^T y||^2
      +kappa*sum_(i<n)||y_i-ybar+(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0||^2
      +eta||v^T y-(p82+s82)x_0||^2
      +(q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa))*||x_0||^2.             (II.5)

Since sum d_i=0 and sum d_i^2=2tau^2, expanding the detail squares
recovers exactly kappa sum||y_i-ybar||^2+<x_0,d^T y> and the displayed
charge. There is no sum of independently minimized local Schur budgets.
The common-gradient restriction, in particular, is

    R_II,common=(q82-p82^2)||x_0||^2-tau*Ppos_z*<x_0,G>
            +[kappa sum_i(t_i-tbar)^2+eta U82^2]||G||^2.  (II.6)

It has the nonzero derivative -tau*Ppos_z*x_0 at G=0. Discarding that
derivative would discard precisely the payment this construction makes.

Full PSD of (II.5) is EQUIVALENT to

    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa).                            (II.7)

Sufficiency follows from the explicit squares. For necessity take any
nonzero scalar x_0, set y_i-ybar=-(d_i/(2kappa))*x_0, and select the
common value ybar so that v^T y=(p82+s82)x_0. This is possible because
sum v_i=p82+s82>0. Finally set g_n=-s82*x_0+v^T y. These are arbitrary
formal coordinates under the invertible map (3.1). They annihilate every
other square. A negative last coefficient would therefore give an
explicit negative original-space direction. No interpolability is claimed.
More explicitly, take x_0=1,
y_i=1+(v^T d)/(2kappa(p82+s82))-d_i/(2kappa), g_n=p82,
and use (3.1a) for each queried g_i. The value of the whole polynomial is
then q82-p82^2-tau^2/(2kappa).

### 6.1 Two literal original signs exceed the full charge budget

Let j=z-2 be the preceding queried leaf. The final three physical steps
are exactly (b,1+beta,b), so

    k_z=b, k_j=b*a*beta.

By (II.1), alpha_i<2/n. Since n>=15 and b>4/3,

    v_z>b-2/n>1,
    v_j>b*a*beta-2/n
         >7/18-2/15=23/90>1/4.                      (II.8)

Also gamma>1, Rvec_0>0. The final-row entry (II.3) becomes

    Nmat_zj=(-kappa+tau)/n+gamma v_z v_j.             (II.9)

If lambda_(z,j)=-Nmat_zj>=0, then

    kappa>=tau+n*gamma v_z v_j>tau+n/4>=n/4.          (II.10)

The first-to-last queried entry is exactly

    Nmat_0z=a*kappa/n+gamma Rvec_0 v_z
                                      -(b-2a/n)*tau. (II.11)

The factor b-2a/n is positive. If lambda_(0,z)>=0, (II.11) implies

    b*tau>a*kappa/n,
    tau>(a/b)*kappa/n>kappa/(4n).                    (II.12)

The last inequality follows from a=b-1 and b>4/3. The dropped terms
gamma Rvec_0 v_z and 2a*tau/n were nonnegative, with the first strictly
positive, so the strict comparison in (II.12) is justified.

Combining (II.7), (II.10), and (II.12) gives

    q82>=p82^2+tau^2/(2kappa)
      >kappa/(32n^2)>1/(128n).                       (II.13)

This is a parameter-independent bound for this literal law. At any
q82<=1/(128n), either its exact PSD condition fails, or one of the two
explicit original multipliers lambda_(z,z-2),lambda_(0,z) is negative.
If the PSD condition is imposed, an ordered sign audit is explicit:
test (II.9); if it is positive, lambda_(z,z-2)<0. If it is nonpositive,
(II.10) holds and (II.7) forces tau<=sqrt(2kappa q82); at the forbidden
level the inequalities used in (II.12) cannot hold, so (II.11)>0 and
lambda_(0,z)<0. No claim is made that all the remaining signs pass.

This second failure is for the displayed A in (II.2) and complete literal
array. A further balanced-skew term preserving this same factor may
change the two selected entries; unlike Section 5.2, no exclusion of
that enlarged second matching family is asserted.

## 7. All-depth comparison with the target; nonempty factor domains

The hierarchy mass can be checked directly. Put

    vword=a/(c-2)=beta/[c(c-2)], Aword=1+S.

Define A_j=1+sum H_j. The exact solution of
A_(j+1)=2A_j+a*c^j, A_0=1 is

    A_j=(1-vword)2^j+vword*c^j, 0<=j<=m.             (7.1)

Substitution proves the formula at every depth; it is not an executed
recurrence. Since c>ell>2 and ell(ell-2)=1, one has 0<vword<1.
Furthermore

    c(c-2)=1+2(ell+1)eps+ell^2 eps^2<1+7eps+6eps^2,
    (1-8eps)(1+7eps+6eps^2)
                       =1-eps-50eps^2-48eps^3<1-eps.

Thus vword>1-8eps=1-2/m>1/2. Equation (7.1) implies

    Aword<c^m, D=2Aword-1>c^m-1,
    D/n>(c/2)^m>(6/5)^m.                            (7.2)

For the middle strict ratio, (c^m-1)/(2^m-1)>c^m/2^m follows by
cross multiplying and using c^m>2^m. The binomial expansion gives

    (6/5)^8>1+8/5+28/25+56/125=521/125>4.

Raising to the fourth power yields (6/5)^32>256. Therefore EVERY
m>=1536 satisfies D>256n>136n, and hence

    qtar82=17/(16D)<1/(128n).                          (7.3)

The factor domains are not vacuous. Because s82=beta^n/c^m and
Aword<c^m, s82*Aword<1. Thus pbase82=1/Aword belongs to (s82,1). Moreover

    17*Aword^2-16*(2Aword-1)
                               =16*(Aword-1)^2+Aword^2>0

proves pbase82^2<qtar82. Law I therefore has strictly positive full factors at
the target, for example p82=pbase82 and kappa=theta=1. Their matched row signs
fail by Section 5. For Law II, p82=pbase82 and tau=0 also give a full PSD
factor at qtar82 for every kappa>0, but the literal row signs fail. Choosing
tau>0 attempts to repair signs; (II.13) proves its full common/initial
cost exceeds the target before the two indicated signs can both pass.

All comparisons above are symbolic inequalities valid throughout the
enclosing-m domain. No m-dependent child reset, dropped terminal step,
numerical asymptotic estimate, or replacement benchmark is used.

## 8. Necessary screens, former attempts, and literature boundaries

The public recall distinguished the following earlier UNVERIFIED records:

* 88f3db7b0a2bb506 is a PRIMAL Haar rank-one trajectory, rejected by an
  original directed cycle. It supplies no dual factor or accepted lemma.
* 8d6831f97708e3a9 concerns symmetric finite kernels and a normalized
  tensor-mixture cone. Their directed-transport/first-query limitations
  are awareness, not a premise in the new derivations.
* 9da28a701244ea55 excludes specified contact-routed and LCA receiver
  arrays. Their negative x_0=0 directions and reverse-cycle failure are
  not being reused as a putative universal obstruction.
* 0073a83e08ff2e21 exposes the E80 reset transcript and an omitted remote
  prefix row. It is not an interpolable primal witness or a W lower bound.

The old H4 vector, deep zero-displacement waves, equal-scale checkpoint
pair, and the ENTIRE E80 reset transcript are all full original-vector
assignments. In the stated PSD domains, (I.5) and (II.5) are nonnegative
on EVERY assignment, so those necessary quadratic screens are covered
without numerical replay. This is stronger than checking their values
one by one, but it is NOT a certificate: the original row signs fail.
For E80 in particular, positivity of our square on its formal transcript
does not permit using a signed interpolation combination as an upper
bound. No legality or terminal payoff is inferred from that screen.

The exact primary source Altschuler--Parrilo, "Acceleration by Stepsize
Hedging II: Silver Stepsize Schedule for Smooth Convex Optimization",
arXiv:2309.16530, Sections 3.1--3.2, was read as background. Its proof
organizes gluing, rank-one corrections, and sparse corrections; the
component corrections need not all be positive, while final multipliers
must be. Its residual orientation is Qpaper_ij=-2Q_(j,i) here, and its
word is the undeformed Silver word. No theorem from it is imported for
our altered word or normalized W bound. The coefficient checks above
are self-contained; this is a proof-pattern comparison only.

The Matlas query returned unrelated PL, minimax-extragradient, and
stochastic-subgradient results; none was used. Web retrieval located
the exact source just named. The PDF was downloaded into this worker's
downloads directory. Local extraction failed with exit127,
"pdftotext: command not found"; pypdf/PyPDF2/fitz were absent. No parser
was installed. An arXiv HTML endpoint returned404; the exact PDF text
and proof were then read through the web tool. No successful LOCAL text
extraction is claimed. No external theorem needs external_refs because
there is no submission and no external result in the proof dependencies.

## 9. Failure synthesis and the remaining interface

Law I has an explicit full PSD factor but no nonnegative original-row
representation with its fixed terminal pool. The first-row invariant
survives every balanced-skew re-selection preserving that exact factor.
This is an original coefficient obstruction, not a missing full-space
term, an untested child block, or failure of a restricted kernel test.

Law II allows a nonzero skew divergence and accounts for its genuine
common-gradient derivative. Its correction costs tau^2/(2kappa) ONCE,
globally. The literal correction can make a first-query edge less
positive in Nmat, but the last-to-previous-leaf edge demands a larger
detail coefficient. The two demands force the stated excessive charge.
The obstruction concerns this one skew pattern and terminal distribution,
not arbitrary chronological corrections or further coefficient re-selection.

The still-needed mechanism is an explicit full factor AND a nonnegative
original-row matching at q82<=17/(16D) for every SAME-m m>=1536. A changed
terminal distribution, a nonisotropic chronology-dependent detail system,
or a more general skew correction with an analytically bounded aggregate
charge lies outside these exclusions. Those are open interfaces, not
additional laws attempted under this two-law allocation, and no unknown
PSD or sign-feasibility problem is offered as their solution.

The original all-word/all-horizon minimax Goal remains OPEN. There is no
full-class upper certificate, no new fact ID, no legal lower witness,
and no basis to finalize, revoke, publish outward, stop the project, or
renew this invocation automatically.
