<!-- BEGIN_SOURCE_BODY DOC-003054 -->
# C72: full primitive cone, a lossless optimum lift, and the exact reuse threshold

Status: self-contained research draft, UNVERIFIED. Sixth launch, first round,
cumulative round 14. No verifier call is authorized. All new results are proved
jointly here; shared findings are reference evidence, not predecessors.
Accepted background: b98bb807d514e80d (benchmark definitions),
0be8b54e0a33381c (square-root mass contraction), and 1252b8823bb5a290
(full smooth-convex interpolation and its explicit conjugate interpolant).

## 1. The intrinsic property and every original vector

Normalize L=1. For a nonnegative word h of length n let s=sum h_i,
P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t, x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t, and

 Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
 V_h=2sum_(i<n)h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2
                                      -s(s+1)||g_n||^2.

Call h primitive if V_h>=0 on every actual smooth convex run. This is exactly
Zhang--Jiang's primitive property: expansion of their difference of squared
positions cancels x_0 and all mixed gradient terms and leaves V_h. No norm
of x_0 is being substituted for the original objective distance.

Let e denote the n+1 column of ones; define A_ii=1, A_it=h_t for t<i,
and A_it=0 for t>i. The formal gradients g_0,...,g_n are independent.
Choose ALL n(n+1) ordered finite multipliers lambda_ij>=0, i!=j; their
zero diagonal is conventional. Define L=diag(lambda e)-lambda and

 omega=(-2h_0,...,-2h_(n-1),2s)^T,
 D=diag(h_0(2-h_0),...,h_(n-1)(2-h_(n-1)),-s(s+2)).

Then the full sufficient cone is exactly

 L^T e=omega,                 M=D+(LA+A^T L^T)/2 >=0.            (1)

Indeed value cancellation in V_h+sum lambda Q is the displayed flow.
The position terms have matrix sym(L(A-I)); the squared-difference terms
have matrix sym(L)-diag(omega)/2. Adding V's diagonal gives (1), with

                  V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g)>.          (2)

Thus (1) implies V_h>=0 because Q_ij<=0. All incoming terminal edges are
present. There is no rank, symmetry, child, sparsity, or fixed-remainder
restriction on lambda or M. In particular accepted terminal reduction
1252b8823bb5a290 is NOT used to delete primitive constraints.

Adding optimum indices to this certificate cannot enlarge its feasible
cone. With x_star=g_star=f_star=0, the x_0 squared coefficient remains
zero. Its mixed coefficient with g_i is -lambda_(i,star). A PSD matrix with
a zero diagonal entry has that entire row zero (test the corresponding
2x2 principal minors). Therefore all lambda_(i,star)=0. The sum of finite
value-flow equations is then -sum lambda_(star,i)=0, since the V value
coefficients sum to zero. Nonnegativity forces all lambda_(star,i)=0.
Consequently the full optimum/position column is forced to be identically
zero; (1) preserves the entire remaining cone, not a guessed reduction.

A unit-slope Huber run started beyond 1+s has g_i=1, Q_ij=0, and V_h=0.
For any certificate (1) this gives e^T M e=0 and hence M e=0. This is an
EXACT equality kernel, never a positive full-space eigenvalue margin. The
numerical formulation below uses the full matrix without a kernel quotient.

## 2. Lossless intrinsic primal: explicitly restore a minimizer

For arbitrary finite sample jets obeying every Q_ij<=0 and the GD recursion,
there is a legal smooth convex function with a minimizer and the SAME V_h.
This justifies the intrinsic model at the level of actual functions as well
as the preceding full-certificate column argument.

Fix epsilon>0 and append one coordinate. Put

 g'_i=(g_i,epsilon), x'_i=(x_i,T-epsilon P_i),
 f'_i=f_i+c-epsilon^2 P_i.

The GD recursion remains exact, and direct substitution shows Q'_ij=Q_ij:
the extra function difference -epsilon^2(P_i-H_j) cancels the extra
position term epsilon^2(P_i-H_j). Choose the finite constants

 c>=max_i{-f_i+epsilon^2 P_i+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2},
 epsilon T>=max_i{f_i+c-<g_i,x_i>+(||g_i||^2+epsilon^2)/2}.

After adding the optimum jet (0,0,0), these are exactly Q'_(star,i)<=0
and Q'_(i,star)<=0. The full conjugate interpolation construction in
accepted fact1252b8823bb5a290 supplies an everywhere finite convex
1-smooth interpolant with this optimum and every displayed jet. Thus it
is a genuine same-function GD run with an actual minimizer.

The additional V contribution is epsilon^2 times

 2sum_i h_i(s-P_i)+sum_i h_i(1-h_i)-s(s+1)=0,

because 2sum_i h_i(s-P_i)=s^2+sum_i h_i^2. Hence V'_h=V_h exactly.
This lift does NOT preserve the original distance or normalized objective,
and is used ONLY for intrinsic primitive validity. Zero steps and n=0
cause no exception; identical original queries stay identical after a zero
step. All formal original gradients remain in the final cone model.

It follows that minimizing V_h over the full Gram G_prim>=0, trace(G_prim)=1, all
ordered Q_ij<=0, and a harmless value gauge f_n=0 detects primitive failure
exactly at the sign level. Every negative feasible V yields a legal failure
by the lift. Conversely every nonzero-gradient legal failure can be divided
by its positive total squared gradient to meet trace(G_prim)=1. Zero-gradient
jets cannot have negative V, since all values then agree. A common-gradient
Huber transcript has V=0, so this minimum is always <=0.

For positive steps the primal also has strict feasibility: use a diagonal
quadratic with n+1 distinct curvatures in (0,1) and nonzero initial
coordinates, then scale to trace(G_prim)=1. The gradient polynomial columns
z product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) have distinct degrees 1,...,n+1 and are independent
at n+1 nonzero distinct nodes (divide by z). Every finite distinct-query
residual is strictly negative. This is the same Vandermonde argument as in
accepted1252b8823bb5a290. Values are bounded after fixing f_n because the
two ordered residuals bound every f_i-f_n and trace(G_prim)=1 bounds every Gram
entry. Thus no unbounded objective is concealed by the gauge.

## 3. Composition identities, including arbitrary PSD child remainders

These identities are rederived from the Q definition and coincide with the
proofs of Zhang--Jiang, arXiv:2410.12395v2, Theorems3.1/3.2. Their exact
local paper, definitions, and both proofs were reread. No external result
with unexamined hypotheses is needed for the algebra below.

Primitive join. Let a,b be primitive, A=1+sum a, C=1+sum b. Let d>0 solve

 d(A+C+d)=AC; T=A+C+d, kappa=(T/C)^2.

Form h=(a,1+d,b). Let p be the bridge query and z the terminal query, and
let j range over the nonterminal queries in b. Direct expansion gives

 V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)[sum_j b_j Q_(j,p)+Q_(z,p)
                                  +d Q_(p,z)+sum_j b_j Q_(j,z)]. (3)

One way to audit all coefficients is to call the bracket C_aux; its value
part is 2sum_j b_j f_j+(d-C)f_p+(2-C-d)f_z, and its gradient part is

 sum_j b_j(1-b_j)||g_j||^2+[(C-d)/2-d^2]||g_p||^2
                                          +(C+d)||g_z||^2/2.

Insert these in (3), and use (kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d), kappa d^2=A^2,
kappa C^2=T^2. The mixed terms cancel. Since C>d and kappa>1, every
bracket coefficient is nonnegative. Child certificates with matrices M_a,
M_b produce the FULL parent remainder M_a+kappa M_b on disjoint original
child-gradient sets. They may have arbitrary PSD rank. Its mass is

 T=Omega(A,C)=(A+C+sqrt((A+C)^2+4AC))/2.

Dominant join. Define, for weights u_i>=0 of mass D=2sum h+1,

 J_(h,u)=||x_0||^2+2sum_i u_i Q_(i,star)-2D f_z
                              -||x_0-sum_i u_i g_i||^2.

Dominance means J>=0 on every legal run; it implies W(h)<=1/D by Q_i*<=0.
The empty word has u=1 and J=0. A primitive word is dominant with
u=(h,s+1), because expanding the last square gives J=V_h exactly.
For a primitive left child of mass A, and a dominant right child of mass D
and weights v, put

 mu=2A/D, r=(1+sqrt(1+8A/D))/2, gamma=A+1, beta=1+A/r.

The parent word is (a,beta,b). Its weights are u=(a,gamma,r v), all
nonnegative, of total G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2=Dr^2. Its identity is

 J_parent=V_a+r^2 J_right-2mu sum_(i>p) v_i Q_(i,p).             (4)

To verify the equality expand both sides. The value flow uses
mu D=(sum a)+gamma=2A. The gradient coefficients reduce to
(sum a)(sum a+1)+mu D+gamma=gamma^2,
r^2=r+mu, and r^2 beta=mu+gamma r; these cancel every mixed term and
leave the defining parent square. This proves (4), including empty
children. It proves exactly the ConPD rule in accepted benchmark notation.

## 4. A finite seed threshold that compares the ORIGINAL exact B_n

Let lambda=1+sqrt(2), rho=log_2(lambda), q=1/rho. Suppose an actual
nonnegative primitive word a has length r_seed and A_seed=1+sum a. Put
m_seed=r_seed+1. The sufficient target condition is

                       A_seed>m_seed^rho.                      (5)

A gain merely above C_(r_seed) does not imply (5).
Here are the full amplification argument and the parameter losses.
First Omega(x,y)^q<=x^q+y^q for x,y>=0. For positive arguments use
x=exp(u-t), y=exp(u+t), t>=0. It becomes J(t)>=0 where

 J(t)=q^(-1)log(2cosh(qt))-arsinh(cosh t).

J(0)=0 and J(t) tends to zero at infinity. Its derivative has the sign
of qt-alpha(t), where alpha=artanh(sinh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2)). Direct
differentiation gives alpha'=cosh(t)/sqrt(1+cosh(t)^2), strictly increasing
from 1/sqrt(2) to1. Also 1/sqrt(2)<q<1: lambda>2 and lambda^3<16 imply
1>q>3/4>1/sqrt(2). Thus alpha-qt starts by decreasing, has strictly
increasing derivative, and tends to infinity; it crosses zero once on
t>0. Therefore J first increases and then decreases to zero, proving the
inequality. Zero arguments follow directly from Omega(x,0)=x.

Induction on each primitive binary tree now gives C_k<= (k+1)^rho.
Unrolling a maximizing dominant tree, its primitive side branches have
leaf counts l_1,...,l_t summing to n. Since G(A,D)<=D+4A,

 B_n<=1+4sum l_i^rho<=1+4n^rho.

Balanced primitive trees give C_(2^k-1)=lambda^k, by the preceding upper
bound and Omega(A,A)=lambda A. Hence B_(2^k)>=G(lambda^k,1)>=2lambda^k.
The number K=limsup B_n/n^rho thus satisfies 2<=K<=4. No phase limit is
assumed and this is the ORIGINAL exact DP sequence.

Take a fully expanded optimizing dominant tree at length n, with smallest
maximizing split as deterministic tie rule. It has exactly n primitive
empty leaves and one final dominant empty leaf: induction on the unique
right dominant chain proves this count. Replace every primitive empty
leaf by a and recompute all physical bridges by (3)-(4). Leave the final
dominant empty leaf unchanged. The actual resulting word Lift_n has length
m_seed n and is dominant. Homogeneity of Omega makes each primitive mass
exactly A_seed times its former mass.

Let D_n=2sum Lift_n+1. In an auxiliary scalar evaluation also change the
terminal dominant mass from1 to A_seed. Homogeneity of G makes that
auxiliary root A_seed B_n. Accepted0be8b54e0a33381c proves
sqrt(G(A,d))=(sqrt(d)+sqrt(d+8A))/2 and that this is increasing and
1-Lipschitz as a function of sqrt(d). Propagating the unchanged terminal
leaf discrepancy therefore gives

 sqrt(A_seed B_n)-(sqrt(A_seed)-1)<=sqrt(D_n)<=sqrt(A_seed B_n). (6)

Choose n_j with B_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to K. Equation(6) gives
D_(n_j)/n_j^rho tending to A_seed K, whereas by the SAME global limsup
limsup B_(m_seed n_j)/(m_seed n_j)^rho<=K. Consequently

 limsup_j B_(m_seed n_j) W_(m_seed n_j)(Lift_(n_j))
                           <= m_seed^rho/A_seed <1.            (7)

For delta=(1-m_seed^rho/A_seed)/2>0, every sufficiently large output
horizon in this subsequence has ratio <=1-delta. This is a fixed relative
all-function upper separation and suffices to refute the original uniform
asymptotic matching claim, conditional on the actual certified seed.
The unused dominant leaf and its O(sqrt(B_n)) mass loss are explicit.

## 5. Before-solve design, calibration signs, and scope

The new discovery lane searches length15 only, with every coordinate free
and fixed capacity A_target=(1+eta)lambda^4, initially eta=1/1000. The
known Silver word has length15 and exactly lambda^4 capacity, so (5) has
zero baseline threshold gap. Source order, unequal step magnitudes, and the
full all-pair sensitivity guide proposals; no symmetry or tensor class is
imposed. Length4 is used only for two exact model calibrations.

Calibration expected signs BEFORE any solve:
* h=(1,2,1,1) has min V=0. Its nonzero lambda entries are 01:2,10:4,
  21:3,31:3,41:3,14:3,23:8,32:16,34:35,43:48,24:3. Expansion gives
  M=|g0-g1|^2+4|g2-g3|^2+16|g3-g4|^2. Huber attains0.
* h=(3,1,1,1) is nonprimitive. Use x=(1,-2,-3/2,-1,-1/2),
  g=(1,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2,-1/2), f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2. This is the
  convex1-smooth function with derivative clipped to[-1/2,1]. Direct
  substitution gives V=-45/4 and sum||g_i||^2=2. The trace-one model
  therefore has min V<=-45/8. Every actual query and optimum Q<=0.
* The full exact length15 Silver word has min V=0 by (3), starting with
  empty leaves. Its full nonnegative array is generated by (3), with
  identically zero PSD remainder. Its e kernel is not made strict.

The primal objective is NOT normalized objective error. Only an exactly
reconstructed nonnegative certificate satisfying (1) and (5), followed by
(3)-(7), could settle the original negative route. Solver feasibility,
positive residual tolerances, clipped eigenvalues, or a finite capacity
gain below (5) do not do so. All original statuses and raw primal/dual
arrays must be retained. The allowance is at most72 evaluations,120 measured
build-plus-solve seconds,n<=15,40 distinct nonbaseline words. No old budget
is renewed and no verifier call is made.

## 6. C74: a full-array source rigidity lemma and its local scope

This appendix is part of the C72 joint draft. It is UNVERIFIED. It is not a
proof that an improved primitive seed is impossible. The numerical normal
at the end is not an exact PSD certificate.

Define the balanced primitive words Sil_0=(), Sil_(m+1)=(Sil_m,1+lambda^(m-1),Sil_m),
where lambda=1+sqrt(2). Thus Sil_m has length2^m-1 and capacity lambda^m.
The complete array Lambda_m is obtained from (3) starting with the empty
array, and has identically zero full remainder. It is the UNIQUE full
certificate even if all incoming-terminal coefficients are allowed to be
signed, while every other coefficient is nonnegative. Here and below
"certificate" includes cancellation of all values and PSD on all original
gradients. The optimum columns are already forced zero by Section1.

Here is a full proof rather than an assumption of a unique optimizer.
At a root write h=(a,1+d,b), A=1+sum a, C=1+sum b, T=A+C+d, dT=AC,
kappa=(T/C)^2, eta=d/C. Let p be the bridge query, z the final query,
P={queries through p}, and B={queries after p through z}.
There are two legal equality runs. A far-out unit-slope Huber ray has all
gradients1 and all Q=V=0. The clipped derivative f'(x)=min(1,max(x,-eta)),
with f(0)=0 and initial x_0=A, has gradients1 on P and -eta on B. If
R_i^a is the remaining left step sum before p and R_j^b the remaining
right sum before z, its finite data are

 x_i=1+R_i^a, f_i=R_i^a+1/2       (i in P),
 x_j=-eta(1+R_j^b), f_j=eta^2(R_j^b+1/2) (j in B).

Its same-group residuals vanish and its cross residuals are exactly

 Q_(i,j)=-(1+eta)eta R_j^b, Q_(j,i)=-(1+eta)R_i^a.             (8)

Moreover f_i=g_i x_i-g_i^2/2 throughout, so telescoping gives
V=A^2-T^2 eta^2=0. Every incoming-terminal Q is zero. Therefore even with
signed incoming-terminal multipliers, evaluating any supplied PSD
certificate on these runs gives zero, and every nonterminal negative-Q
edge has zero coefficient. Positivity of all source steps leaves only
cross edges i->z (i in P) and j->p (j in B).

Write their coefficients nu_i and mu_j respectively. Both equality
gradient vectors lie in the PSD kernel; they span the block indicators
of P and B. The full mixed form, before using those kernels, is

 -sum_(i in P) nu_i <g_i,sum_(j in B,j<z)b_j g_j+g_z>
                        +d sum_(j in B)mu_j <g_p,g_j>.

Rows i<p of the matrix applied to the B indicator force C nu_i=0.
Rows in B applied to the P indicator then force
mu_j=nu_p b_j/d for j<z and mu_z=nu_p/d. Summing value-flow equations
through P gives sum mu-nu_p=2(A+d). Hence

 nu_p=d(kappa-1), mu_j=(kappa-1)b_j, mu_z=kappa-1.              (9)

In particular every full mixed coefficient is zero. Formula(3) then
splits the supplied full matrix as M_a+kappa M_b, on disjoint original
gradient blocks, where the prefix array is unchanged and the tail array is

 lambda^b_ij=lambda_ij/kappa
                 -(1-1/kappa)b_i 1_(i<z,j=z).

Both blocks are PSD. The prefix array is ordinary; the tail may have
signed terminal coefficients but all other signs remain nonnegative.
Thus induction may be applied inside both balanced children. The base
empty word has the zero array. For the one-step base, writing u=sqrt(2),
value cancellation gives lambda_10=lambda_01+2u, while the constant
kernel forces lambda_01=2; its matrix is (2-u^2)|g0-g1|^2=0.
The induction with (9) uniquely reconstructs Lambda_m, and forces all
full child and parent matrices to be zero. This proves the stated rigidity.
It is rigidity at this exact word, not a neighborhood or global theorem.

## 7. Exact continuation identity away from a saturated cut

For ANY positive a,b and bridge1+d with 0<d<C=1+sum b, put
A=1+sum a, T=A+C+d, and now define

 kappa=1+2(A+d)/(C-d),
 R=(A+d)(AC-dT)/(C-d).

No primitive assumption and no saturation equation is used. Direct
coefficient comparison, retaining every value and gradient, yields

 V_h=V_a+kappa V_b-(kappa-1)C_aux
                              +R(|g_p|^2-|g_z|^2),             (10)

where C_aux is the same bracket in(3). The values match because
(kappa-1)(C-d)=2(A+d). The unmatched squared-gradient coefficients are
A^2-kappa d^2 and kappa C^2-T^2; their sum is zero and the first equals
R. This verifies all terms using the explicit expansion in Section3.

Recursively use(10) on the fixed complete binary tree near Sil_m. Every
actual coefficient in the resulting array Lambda(h) is nonnegative,
since d>0,C>d,kappa>1 persist. Its FULL remainder is the diagonal form

 sum_(nodes v) w_v R_v (|g_(p(v))|^2-|g_(z(v))|^2),             (11)

where w_v is the product of kappa along those ancestors at which the
node lies in the right subtree. This is an exact formal identity, NOT
a PSD claim: away from the source its diagonal terms need not be positive.
It uses no fixed-remainder, rank, or symmetry assumption about OTHER
certificates that might exist for the same changed word.

At the source Sil_m, let a node have depth d_v (root depth0) and r_v right
turns among its ancestors. Its two child capacities are
A_v=lambda^(m-d_v-1), its bridge gap is A_v/lambda, and w_v=lambda^(2r_v).
For an arbitrary first variation u of ALL the physical steps,

 dR_v[u]=sqrt(2) A_v [sum_(steps in left or right child)u_i
                                             -2lambda u_(p(v))]. (12)

To check this derivative, the factor AC-dT vanishes at the source, and
(A+d)/(C-d)=lambda. Its derivative is
(C-d)dA+(A-d)dC-(T+d)dd, giving(12). No derivative of w_v contributes
because R_v=0 at the source.

## 8. A concrete exact normal-completion interface

Consider any full finite jet (G_prim,f) for the actual source Sil_m, where G_prim is
the Gram matrix of its2^m original gradients, satisfying

 G_prim>=0, Q_ij<=0 for EVERY ordered finite pair,
 Q_ij=0 on EVERY positive entry of Lambda_m.                    (13)

All quantities Q here use the source word and the independent f_i;
a common value shift may set the last value to zero. Suppose for some
c_tan>0 its diagonals further satisfy the explicit2^m-1 equations

 G_(p(v),p(v))-G_(z(v),z(v))
             = c_tan lambda^(2d_v-m-2r_v)/(2^(d_v+1)sqrt(2)).       (14)

Then the envelope sensitivity of the COMPLETE canonical array is exactly

                  S(G_prim,f)=-c_tan(1,...,1).                         (15)

Proof: differentiate (2) along the explicitly defined array Lambda(h)
in(11). Every derivative of a multiplier is supported where Lambda_m>0,
so its contribution vanishes on(13). By(12), put
 t_v=w_v sqrt(2)A_v(G_pp-G_zz). Equation(14) says
 t_v=c_tan lambda^(d_v-1)/2^(d_v+1).
For the step at a node of depth d the resulting sensitivity is
sum_(proper ancestors v)t_v-2lambda t_node. The geometric sum is
c_tan[(lambda/2)^d-1], because lambda/2=1+1/(2lambda).
The second term is c_tan(lambda/2)^d, so their difference is -c_tan, proving(15).
This yields the sensitivity without assuming favorable cross-gradient
terms: the ENTIRE matrix and all-Q legality remain obligations in(13).

If a solution of(13)-(14) is actually proved, every sequence of fully
certifiable words h_l tending to Sil_m obeys

 limsup_l [sum(h_l)-sum(Sil_m)]/||h_l-Sil_m|| <=0,                 (16)

with zero differences omitted. Thus a C^1 primitive-certificate path out
of this source cannot have a positive first-order capacity derivative.
Here is a proof that retains arbitrary re-selection of all coefficients.
A fixed sufficiently small-curvature quadratic, run with nearby positive
words, has every finite Q strictly negative, uniformly in a small
neighborhood: choose theta so all theta h_i<1 and use x_0=1. Its iterates
are positive and distinct; Q_ij=-theta(1-theta)(x_i-x_j)^2/2.
Evaluating a certificate on this actual run bounds the sum of ALL its
nonnegative coefficients by a common constant. Hence along any sequence
of nearby certificates, compactness gives convergent subsequences.
Every limit is a full source certificate and therefore equals Lambda_m
by Section6. Thus every selected array tends to Lambda_m.

Evaluate the nearby PSD remainder on the fixed G_prim from(13). At the
source, V=0 by the zero remainder and complementarity. The unchanged
source part is sum lambda_l Q(source)<=0: positive source coefficients
multiply zero, and all other coefficients are nonnegative. The change
with h therefore bounds the nonnegative remainder above by

 S(G_prim,f) dot(h_l-Sil_m)+o(||h_l-Sil_m||).

This follows directly from the polynomial V and Q expressions, the
convergence lambda_l->Lambda_m, and their uniform boundedness. Equation(15)
now gives(16). This uses no derivative or pseudoinverse of the selected
lambda_l and no strict full-space remainder margin.

Crucial scope: even(16) would NOT prohibit a positive capacity gain of
second or higher order along a curved path, nor a distant seed. It does
not prove C_(2^m-1) is globally optimal over primitive schedules. No such
normal is asserted to exist merely because its finite SDP appears feasible.
For an explicit logical scope check, the set {(x,y):y<=x^2} has tangent
cone y<=0 at(0,0), but contains(t,t^2) with positive y for every t!=0.
Indeed for convergent normalized directions the inequality gives the
y-component<=0, whereas t^2/sqrt(t^2+t^4) tends to zero. Thus a first-order
normal alone cannot exclude higher-order objective gains in a nonconvex
feasible set. This example is not asserted to be a GD schedule.

The all-depth algebra makes the missing construction specific. In a parent
normal of strength c_tan, the required left/right child normal strengths are
c_tan_left=(lambda/2)c_tan and c_tan_right=c_tan/(2lambda). The root also needs
G_pp-G_zz=c_tan/(2sqrt(2)lambda^m). Cross-child Q and full PSD must be realized
simultaneously at those exact scales. Independent child scaling without
this original-space completion proves nothing. At length15 this is one
16-gradient Gram completion with fixed diagonal differences, not an
asymptotic benchmark comparison or a surrogate finite objective gain.

## 9. Counted finite discovery and its limits

The full primitive model used all240 ordered finite inequalities and the
complete16-gradient Gram matrix. At the exact source, its canonical
remainder is zero. Two bounded all-coordinate sensitivity probes completed
at capacity ratios1.001 and1.0001 above lambda^4; no feasible improved seed
was found. The best reported energies were -0.06933970529217337 and
-0.006501508090531515, respectively. Both those best outputs had status
`optimal`; other statuses, all residuals, primal/dual arrays and exact
source versions are retained. These are finite non-discovery observations,
not exact nonprimitive proofs or a theorem about arbitrary schedules.

The counted auxiliary evaluation44, at the SAME known baseline word Sil_4,
maximized c_tan under(13), trace(G_prim)=1, and the full sensitivity equations(15).
It reported `optimal`, c_tan=1.0908231587401278, max equality error below1e-14,
and smallest full Gram eigenvalue approximately -3.47e-11. That last value
is NEGATIVE; nothing was clipped and no PSD certificate is claimed.
Raw file: research/max3_full_primitive_discovery_raw/044.json in the public
project research directory. Exact reconstruction of(13)-(14), and then a
second-order or nonlocal analysis if such a normal is proved, is the next
analytic interface. No source-face numerical output is promoted to a fact.

All numerical work in this wave stops at44 calls and26.517837785992016
measured build-plus-solve seconds, with39 distinct nonbaseline words.
This includes five calibration calls (two inaccurate), two initial search
calls (one inaccurate and one user_limit), sixteen1.001 proposals,
twenty1.0001 proposals, and the single auxiliary normal call. The old closed672 plus this worker's44 calls total716; independently
allocated sibling calls are not included in that subtotal. The latest
main guidance f719b26e39837b65 records an additional sibling allocation
of8 and a possible project ceiling752; this worker's allowance is unchanged. Unused numerical
capacity is closed, not transferred or renewed. No verifier was invoked;
no original-target proof or disproof, publication, or finalization follows.

# Full transverse coupling: exact separation of the actual 63-step parent

Status: **UNVERIFIED self-contained joint proof candidate**, worker max,
ninth launch, cumulative round 20. The verifier has not accepted this result.
The current TASK and public ledger allow zero verification calls. The only
accepted fact used is 1252b8823bb5a290, for the definition and necessity of the
original smooth-convex inequalities Q. Every other assertion used in this
package is derived below, including the construction of the input children.

## 1. Statement and scope

Put s=sqrt(2), lambda=1+s, theta=s-1=1/lambda. Choose a>0 and xi>=theta,
and define H=a+1, b=1+H*xi, D=1+3xi+xi^2. The seed word is (a,b,a).
Its actual orthogonal-core transcript is given in section 3. Recursively
form the words h[0]=(a,b,a) and

    A_j=1+sum h[j]=lambda^j A_0,  A_0=H(2+xi),
    r_j=theta A_j,  h[j+1]=(h[j],1+r_j,h[j]).              (1)

Thus h[j] has 4*2^j-1 steps. At every join use isometric copies of the
complete child transcripts, leave the left copy unscaled, scale the right
copy by alpha=r_j ||w||/||x_0||, and align its initial point with -r_j w.
Keep the projection values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 and the optimum jet 0.
No extra gradient coordinate, translation, or function-value change is made.

The following are the claims of this joint candidate.

1. The most general real cross Gram for this operation is parameterized
   by a completely unrestricted real contraction between the two fixed
   transverse spaces. Section 2 gives its exact PSD condition, both sets of
   cross normals, all bridge/optimum constraints, explicit realization,
   common projection function when feasible, and true distance.
2. For zero transverse cross map at the first three joins, the complete
   7-, 15-, and 31-step children are legal. If xi>theta they have strictly
   positive core defect. Nevertheless, for the two actual 31-step children,
   **every** real transverse map at the 63-step join violates

       Q_(36,30)=e_30 [lambda T(xi)-1]>0,
       T(xi)=xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)/D^2,  e_30=a||w_3||^2>0.   (2)

   This includes all maps satisfying the full PSD condition, without a rank,
   sign, symmetry, or boundary-positivity restriction.
3. The previously observed failed row Q_(32,25) is NOT invariant. Its
   transverse coefficient is nonzero. The different row (2) is the exact
   separator; it was not licensed to be inferred from the planar example.
4. If xi>theta, infeasibility persists even when arbitrary transverse maps
   are chosen at every earlier join, and even if different choices are made
   in different subtrees. Every such 63-step construction, with all earlier
   children legal if possible, satisfies the two-row identity

       [(1+xi) Q_(36,30)+Q_(38,30)]/[(2+xi)e_30]
          = lambda q-1
          = (xi-theta)(s*xi+lambda)/D >0,
       q=xi(2+xi)/D.                                    (3)

   The two weights are strictly positive. Thus the claimed parent cannot
   satisfy all original Q inequalities. This broader claim needs xi>theta;
   (2), for the fixed rank-one-built children, also covers xi=theta.
5. Re-selecting arbitrary real function values cannot repair either
   obstruction while the displayed positions, gradients, and optimum at
   zero are retained. The projection values are forced separately on every
   embedded seed copy, as proved at the end of section 5.

For the particular modified-word interval requested by TASK, let

    p0=(16-11s)/3, q0=(8s-10)/3,
    a=s-p0*t, b=2+q0*t, 0<=t<=1/50,
    xi=(b-1)/(a+1).                                     (4)

Then a>0 and xi>=theta, with strict inequality for t>0. The SAME algebraic
seed t=1/100 was used for every discovery comparison. Explicitly it is

    a=(311s-16)/300, b=(590+8s)/300,
    H=(284+311s)/300, xi=(290+8s)/(284+311s),
    A_0=(143+105s)/50.                                  (5)

The words, physical child transcripts, and normalization are unchanged
between the zero-map and full-map 63-step tests. These claims exclude this
specified recursive projection construction. They do not exclude different
seed transcripts, lifts, translations, different optimum alignment, or operations, and
do not resolve the original all-schedule, all-horizon exact-profile goal.

## 2. All real transverse freedom and every original constraint

A child input consists of a finite nonnegative word p of length ell and
vectors x_i,g_i for 0<=i<=ell, including all duplicates and the endpoint.
Define y_i=x_i-g_i, e_i=<g_i,y_i>, w=x_ell=g_ell!=0, and require

    x_(i+1)=x_i-p_i g_i,
    <g_j-g_i,y_i><=0 for every i,j, e_i>=0.              (6)

Set K=conv(0,g_0,...,g_ell) and

    F_K(x)=max_(v in K)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2),
    f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, f_star=0.                (7)

Completing the square proves that the maximizer is P_K(x), the Euclidean
projection. Its unique minimizer condition is
<v-P_K(x),x-P_K(x)><=0 for every v in K. Thus (6) makes g_i=P_K(x_i).
Adding this condition at two points gives
||P_K(x)-P_K(z)||^2<=<x-z,P_K(x)-P_K(z)>, hence P_K is 1-Lipschitz.
Evaluation of the two maxima at the opposite maximizers gives
0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),d><=||d||^2. Consequently F_K is differentiable,
convex and 1-smooth, with gradient P_K, and it interpolates (7).

The original residual from fact 1252b8823bb5a290 is

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2
        =<g_i-g_j,y_j>.                                 (8)

Expansion of the inner products proves this identity. In particular,
Q_i,star=0 and Q_star,i=-e_i. These include both optimum incidences.

The minimizer set of F_K is K^polar={z:<z,v><=0 for every v in K}.
For necessity, test epsilon*v in (7), divide by epsilon, and let epsilon
decrease to zero. Sufficiency follows directly from (7), including v=0.
The update equations imply x_0=w+sum_(i<ell)p_i g_i. For every minimizer z,
<x_0,z><=0, and ||x_0-z||^2>=||x_0||^2. Zero is therefore a closest
minimizer: the TRUE distance is R=||x_0||>0.

For two children L,R, set R_R=||x_R,0||, e=w_L/||w_L||, d=x_R,0/R_R,
alpha=r||w_L||/R_R for the bridge 1+r, where r>0. Let E=e^perp in the
left child span and Dspace=d^perp in the right child span. Write
u_perp=u-<u,e>e and v_perp=v-<v,d>d.

Every allowed pair of real isometries J_L,J_R satisfies J_R d=-J_L e.
It therefore has cross form

    B_C(u,v)=<J_L u,J_R v>
       =-<u,e><v,d>+<u_perp,C v_perp>,                    (9)

where C:Dspace->E is linear and ||C||<=1. To prove necessity, project
J_R v_perp orthogonally onto J_L E; its norm is at most ||v_perp||.
All axial/transverse cross terms vanish because the transverse images are
orthogonal to the common axis. No other restriction on C follows.

Conversely, for any real C with ||C||<=1, put S_C=(I-C^T C)^(1/2), the
positive semidefinite real square root. In R direct-sum E direct-sum Dspace,
define

    J_L u=(<u,e>,u_perp,0),
    J_R v=(-<v,d>,C v_perp,S_C v_perp).                   (10)

The equality C^T C+S_C^2=I proves both isometries, their alignment, and (9).
Zero eigenvalues are retained; nothing is clipped or discarded. In chosen
orthonormal transverse bases, the exact PSD condition is

    [[I,C],[C^T,I]] >=0.                                (11)

Indeed its quadratic form at (z,v) is
||z+C v||^2+<v,(I-C^T C)v>. Setting z=-Cv proves necessity, and the same
identity proves sufficiency. Thus (11) is equivalent to realizability,
including singular cases. For the fixed 31-step children their full spans
have dimension 9 and C is an arbitrary 8 by 8 real matrix. The ranks 2,3,5,9
of the zero-map seed and successive joins follow from the direct-sum
realization (10): the two spans intersect exactly in their aligned axis.
The gradients span each child space, because the seed U,V do and every
join retains both embedded spanning families.

Use J_L(x_L,i,g_L,i,y_L,i) as all left data and
alpha J_R(x_R,j,g_R,j,y_R,j) as all right data. Let m=ell_L+1, so that
right child sample j has parent index m+j. The bridge equation is exact:

    J_L w_L-(1+r)J_L w_L=alpha J_R x_R,0.               (12)

Left values remain f_L,i, and right values are alpha^2 f_R,j. All original
constraints are precisely the following list:

    Q_(i,k)=Q^L_(i,k)                                      (left/left),
    Q_(m+j,m+l)=alpha^2 Q^R_(j,l)                         (right/right),
    Q_(m+j,i)=alpha B_C(y_L,i,g_R,j)-e_L,i,               (13L)
    Q_(i,m+j)=alpha B_C(g_L,i,y_R,j)-alpha^2 e_R,j,       (13R)
    Q_(i,star)=Q_(m+j,star)=0,
    Q_(star,i)=-e_L,i, Q_(star,m+j)=-alpha^2 e_R,j.

Every i,k ranges over the entire left sample list and every j,l over the
entire right sample list. Diagonal rows are zero. There are no other rows.
In particular the left endpoint/bridge sample i=ell_L has y_L,i=0, so
Q_(m+j,ell_L)=0, while its reverse rows are

    Q_(ell_L,m+j)
      = r||w_L||^2/R_R^2 [-<x_R,0,y_R,j>-r e_R,j].       (14)

These rows are invariant under C. All terminal-residual rows are zero
because y_R,ell_R=0. If a left residual happens to equal a_pos*w_L with
a_pos>0 and its gradient is w_L, the other invariant rows are

    Q_(m+j,i)=a_pos||w_L||^2[-r c_R,j-1],
    c_R,j=<g_R,j,x_R,0>/R_R^2.                          (15)

Equations (13L)-(13R) are the TWO full sets of original cross normals;
neither is replaced by a sign condition or by PSD. If and only if all their
rows are nonpositive, (7) for the parent convex hull constructs one common
function, checks every Q, and gives true initial distance R_L. The endpoint
is W=alpha J_R w_R. For capacities A=1+sum p_L and B=1+sum p_R, and
P=A+B+r, put kappa_L=A^2||w_L||^2/R_L^2 and
kappa_R=B^2||w_R||^2/R_R^2. Direct substitution gives

    kappa_parent=P^2 r^2 kappa_L kappa_R/(A^2 B^2).       (16)

The choice rP=AB yields their product. For identical capacities the
positive solution is r=theta A and P=lambda A, giving (1). This ratio law
does not imply legality. No ratio is used as an actual payoff when (13)
fails.

## 3. The actual seed, positive interval, and complete legal children

Let U,V be orthogonal vectors with ||U||^2=1 and ||V||^2=xi. Define

    (x_0,g_0)=(H(1+xi)U+HV,U),
    (x_1,g_1)=(bU+HV,U),
    (x_2,g_2)=(HV,V), (x_3,g_3)=(V,V).                 (17)

Its word is (a,b,a), and direct subtraction checks all three updates.
Its residuals are

    y_0=(a+Hxi)U+HV, y_1=Hxi U+HV, y_2=aV, y_3=0.

At samples 0 and 1 the normal against V is respectively -a and 0; the
normal against 0 is respectively -(a+Hxi) and -Hxi. The normals against U
are zero. At sample 2 the normals against U and 0 both equal -a xi and
the normal against V is zero. At sample 3 all normals are zero. These
list all distinct vertices of conv(0,U,V), including duplicate gradients.
Thus every Q is nonpositive, with e_i>0 for i=0,1,2. The seed is an actual
instance of (6)-(7), and

    R^2=H^2 D, ||w_0||^2=xi,
    k_0=<x_0,w_0>/R^2=xi/(HD),
    zeta_0=||w_0||^2/R^2=xi/(H^2D),
    kappa_0=xi(2+xi)^2/D,
    kappa_0-1=(1+xi)(xi^2+2xi-1)/D.                    (18)

Since xi^2+2xi-1=(xi-theta)(xi+lambda), the defect is positive precisely
when xi>theta. To check (4), the rational inequalities 7/5<s<10/7 follow
by squaring. They imply 0<p0<1/5 and q0>2/5, so a>0 throughout the stated
interval. Also H=lambda-p0 t>0 and
xi-theta=(q0+theta p0)t/H, whose coefficient is positive. This proves the
interval and positive-defect assertions without floating arithmetic.

Here is a complete legality check for the fixed zero-map children, needed
to ensure that the final obstruction is not caused by illegal inputs.
For e_i>0 retain the signed lists

    a_i=<g_i,w>/||w||^2, B_i=<w,y_i>/e_i,
    c_i=<g_i,x_0>/R^2, D_i=<x_0,y_i>/e_i.

All gradient samples are retained in a_i,c_i. Residuals with e_i=0 are
exactly zero and satisfy all their rows automatically. For two identical
children with C=0, (13) is equivalent to

    r max_(i,j)(-c_j B_i)<=1,
    max_(i,j)(-a_i D_j)<=r.                             (19)

The seed lists are nonnegative. Their attained maxima are

    max a_i=1, max B_i=1, max c_i=c_*=(1+xi)/(HD),
    max D_i=A_0.

For the last assertion, D_1=A_0, D_2=H, and
D_0=H(1+xi)+H^2xi/(a+Hxi)<=H(2+xi). Also B_0=Hxi/(a+Hxi)<=1,
B_1=B_2=1. Hence the first zero-map join is legal by (19).

For a zero-map join the exact transported lists are

    a_left=-k/(r zeta) a,   a_right=a,
    B_left=-rk B,          B_right=B,
    c_left=c,              c_right=-rk c,
    D_left=D,              D_right=-k/(r zeta) D,
    k_new=-r k^2,          zeta_new=r^2 zeta^2.           (20)

These formulas follow from (9) with C=0 and the definitions; the right
energies and endpoint squared norms carry alpha^2 and the left initial
norm is unchanged. Set omega=1/(2+xi), q=A_0 k_0=xi(2+xi)/D,
u=lambda omega, and v=theta q. For xi>=theta, u<=1, while 0<q<1 because
D-xi(2+xi)=1+xi. After the first join the attained extreme intervals are

    a in[-u,1], B in[-v,1], c in[-v c_*,c_*],
    D_i in[-u A_0,A_0].                                (21)

Induction in (20) gives, for j>=1,
A_j k_j=-q^(2^j) and k_j/(r_j zeta_j)=-lambda omega^(2^j).
The new copying factors in (20) are positive and at most one. Each old
extremum is retained by the unmultiplied copy of its corresponding list.
Therefore (21) remains the exact list of extrema at every later formal
zero-map join. For j>=1, (19) becomes

    r_j v c_* = lambda^(j-2) T(xi) <=1,
    u A_0 <= r_j.                                      (22)

The polynomial identity

    D^2-xi(2+xi)^2(1+xi)=1+2xi+3xi^2+xi^3>0

shows 0<T(xi)<1. Thus j=1 and j=2 satisfy both inequalities (22), since
r_1=A_0 and r_2=lambda A_0. This proves legality of h[1],h[2],h[3], with
all original normals. Formula (16) gives kappa_j=kappa_0^(2^j) at these
legal levels. Their functions, gradients, values and TRUE distance are
those in section 2, not just formal Gram matrices.

## 4. Why the old failed row moves, but a different row cannot move

For the actual fixed child h[3], index 0 is the original seed gradient U,
and the initial point is the original seed x_0 under the leftmost isometry.
Consequently the squared norm of its component orthogonal to x_0 is

    ||(g_0)_perp||^2=1-(1+xi)^2/D=xi/D>0.              (23)

Child residual y_25 comes from seed residual y_1 inside the first left
half of h[1], followed by two right-copy embeddings. Let beta=alpha_1
alpha_2>0 be their combined scale. The h[1] endpoint w_1 and its residual
y_1 satisfy

    ||y_1||^2=H^2 xi(1+xi),
    <y_1,w_1>=-r_0 xi^2/D,
    ||w_1||^2=r_0^2 xi^2/(H^2D).

These follow by substituting (17) into (9) at C=0. The right-copy
isometries carry BOTH vectors with the same beta. Thus the left residual
component orthogonal to w_3 has squared norm

    ||(y_25)_perp||^2
      =beta^2 H^2 xi [1+xi-xi/D]>0.                   (24)

Equations (23)-(24) prove that

    Q_(32,25)(C)=Q_(32,25)(0)
        +alpha_3 <(y_25)_perp,C(g_0)_perp>             (25)

has a nonzero linear coefficient. For example, the norm-one map
v -> -<(g_0)_perp,v>(y_25)_perp/
      (||(g_0)_perp|| ||(y_25)_perp||)
strictly decreases this row. This assertion concerns that row only; it
does not claim simultaneous feasibility of the other rows.

Now use the DIFFERENT residual y_30. At every join the final two seed
gradients remain equal to the new endpoint w. Since the last step is a,

    g_30=g_31=w_3, x_30=H w_3, y_30=a w_3,
    e_30=a||w_3||^2>0.                                (26)

This is an actual sampled residual, not a boundary surrogate. It has zero
transverse component. At the FIRST zero-map join the right copy of seed
gradient 0 has index 4. Formula (20) gives c_4=-r_0 k_0 c_*=-v c_*.
Its two later LEFT embeddings preserve this gradient/initial pairing and
R^2, so it remains c_4=-v c_* in h[3]. At the final join, child gradient 4
has parent index 32+4=36. Substitution of (26) into (15) gives exactly (2).

To establish its sign on the entire stated interval, logarithmic
differentiation and multiplication by the positive denominator gives

    [T'(xi)/T(xi)] xi(1+xi)(2+xi)D
       =2+xi+xi^2+xi^3>0.

Also T(theta)=2-s, by theta^2+2theta=1. Therefore
lambda T(xi)-1 >= lambda(2-s)-1 = s-1=theta>0.
This is a one-row exact affine separation. Its PSD multiplier can be
zero: after enforcing the alignment, the left side of this single
original required inequality is a positive constant on the ENTIRE
realizable cross-Gram set, indeed on the affine space of all C. No
floating solver verdict is used in the proof.

For comparison, the old row has Q_(32,25)(0)/e_25=lambda T(xi)-1 by (21):
B_25=-v and c_0=c_*. This equality does not make it invariant; (25) gives
its nonzero derivative. The invariant bridge-gradient obstruction for the
separate planar core is unnecessary here. No planar formula has been
applied to this orthogonal core.

## 5. A two-row separator when earlier cross maps are also free

We now rederive a stronger obstruction without assuming C=0 at ANY earlier
join. Every first join still has the exact bridge identity

    H(1+xi) g_4 + H g_6 = -r_0 w_0.                    (27)

Here g_4 is the scaled right copy of U, g_6 the scaled right copy of V,
and w_0 the left seed endpoint. Indeed the left side is the isometric
image of alpha_0 x_seed,0, so (12) proves (27) for every allowed transverse
map. Take inner product with the retained initial point x_0 and divide by
R^2. Left-copy embeddings preserve this equality and all its scalars.
Thus in the leftmost 7-step subtree of ANY 31-step right child,

    [(1+xi)c_4+c_6]/(2+xi)
        =-r_0 k_0/A_0=-theta k_0.                     (28)

Both gradients remain at indices 4 and 6 of that child. No agreement
between choices in different subtrees is needed: their initial point and
initial norm equal the common seed's up to an isometry, and (28) has the
same right side. The left 31-step child also retains its last seed pair,
so it satisfies (26) with its own nonzero w_3 and the SAME positive a.

Using (15) on the final join and averaging the rows j=4,6 with the
positive weights (1+xi)/(2+xi),1/(2+xi), the result is

    [(1+xi)Q_(36,30)+Q_(38,30)]/[(2+xi)e_30]
      =r_3 theta k_0-1 =lambda A_0 k_0-1
      =lambda xi(2+xi)/D-1.

The final factorization is

    lambda xi(2+xi)-D=(xi-theta)(s xi+lambda).           (29)

For xi>theta this proves (3) strictly, contradicting the required two
nonpositive original Q rows. The entire fixed-seed repeated operation
is therefore infeasible at length 63 for positive defect, regardless of
all transverse choices at all depths. This conclusion is conditional
only on the precisely specified isometric copies, bridge, projection
values, and absence of lifts/other changes. It is not a no-go theorem for
other children or for the unrestricted worst-case problem.

At xi=theta the right side of (3) is zero, so this argument does NOT
exclude arbitrary earlier couplings at length 63 in that limiting case.
For completeness, the same retained average at a later top bridge r_j
would require lambda^(j-2)q<=1. At j=4 this fails even when xi=theta,
since lambda^2 q=lambda>1. This is a textual statement about the recursive
rule, not a numerical evaluation above the assigned horizon cap.

Function values are rigid on these data. To prove this without assuming
the projection model as a restriction, give a seed arbitrary values
f_i=f_i^proj+delta_i, with f_star=0 and g_star=x_star=0, and require all
original Q inequalities. At sample 3, x_3=g_3 and y_3=0. Its two optimum
inequalities read delta_3<=0 and -delta_3<=0, so delta_3=0. Since g_2=g_3,
the two original rows (2,3),(3,2) both had zero projection residual and
now force delta_2=delta_3=0. The rows (2,1) and (1,3) also had zero
projection residual: the first is <V-U,Hxi U+HV>=0, and the second uses
y_3=0. They force delta_2<=delta_1<=delta_3, hence delta_1=0. Finally
g_0=g_1 makes both rows (0,1),(1,0) zero before the value perturbation,
and therefore delta_0=delta_1=0. Every step uses original Q rows.

An isometry followed by a positive homothety preserves these zero
residuals and the property x_3=g_3. Every queried sample in the recursive
construction belongs to a four-sample seed copy, since joins add only the
bridge step between existing endpoints, without adding another sample
type. Apply the preceding eight-row argument to each copy together with
the COMMON optimum sample 0. All its delta_i vanish. Thus every function
value is forced, even when arbitrary cross maps were used in building
the child spaces. Equations (2)-(3) exclude every convex differentiable
1-smooth interpolant of these positions and gradients having a minimizer
at 0; merely changing its values or its representation does not escape.
This does not address a changed optimum sample or altered vector data.

## 6. What signed state must be retained, and why this operation stops

An exact reusable state for arbitrary transverse joins may keep the full
Gram of (x_0,w,g_0,...,g_ell,y_0,...,y_ell), the word, and the energies
e_i. These data determine the projected transverse Gram blocks, (13),
and (11), including every nullspace. Four scalar extrema alone do not
parameterize an arbitrary transverse feasibility problem.

There is a small necessary signed part of that state which already
separates the present family. If a child has a residual y_i=a_pos*w and
g_i=w, a_pos>0, (15) forces

    c_R,j>=-1/r for EVERY right gradient j.             (30)

Thus the left endpoint-facing residual must be paired with the entire
signed list of right initial/gradient correlations. It cannot be deleted
as an irrelevant transverse direction. Any retained convex combination
of these c_R,j must obey the same bound. The positive combination (28)
forces r theta k_0<=1, which contradicts the actual fourth bridge for a
positive-defect seed. This invariant survives arbitrary transverse
rotations and contractions at all earlier levels.

For a general feasible join, the signed boundary pairings transport as

    <g_left,W>=alpha B_C(g_L,w_R),
    <y_left,W>=alpha B_C(y_L,w_R),
    <g_right,W>=alpha^2<g_R,w_R>,
    <y_right,W>=alpha^2<y_R,w_R>,
    <x_parent,0,g_left>=<x_L,0,g_L>,
    <x_parent,0,y_left>=<x_L,0,y_L>,
    <x_parent,0,g_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,g_R),
    <x_parent,0,y_right>=alpha B_C(x_L,0,y_R).            (31)

The cross entries of the full retained Gram are (9), with factor alpha
when exactly one vector comes from the right child. Internal right
entries have factor alpha^2. Equations (9)-(16),(31) give an exact full
coupling rule at any growing horizon WHEN feasible. They assert no
iteration theorem without proving feasibility at every step. Here the
concrete obstruction (3) decides it: arbitrary transverse freedom alone
does not make this seed family iterable.

## 7. Discovery ledger, calibration, and evidence preservation

The three recorded optimization evaluations all used t=1/100 and the
same explicit seed (5). The smaller calibration was h[2] of length 15,
whose legality is proved in section 3. Both h[4] cases used the SAME
actual h[3] children and the SAME h[4] word.

1. Length 15, C fixed to zero: numerical normalized residual objective
   8.7710378984e-11, maximum original Q about 8.33e-17.
2. Length 63, C fixed to zero: objective 0.4164822178621603;
   Q_(32,25) approximately 0.01256893369169749.
3. Length 63, full unrestricted 8 by 8 C: objective
   0.41648221754700987; largest dual weights are the duplicate original
   rows (36,30),(37,30). The contraction block is strictly PSD in this
   numerical minimax-residual solution, with smallest eigenvalue about
   0.597687. It is NOT a feasible interpolant: the positive residuals
   were neither discarded nor repaired.

The common exact predicted normalized row residual is lambda T(xi)-1,
approximately 0.416482217535. Discovery used 3 of 8 allowed optimization
evaluations and 18.623366069994517 measured build-plus-solve seconds of
60, including imports, model construction, and solver compilation. There
were 2 distinct seed/parent cases out of the allowed 4. Each attempt was
logged BEFORE evaluation and updated with actual cost afterward.

All 4,225 rows of the 63-step ordered-pair/optimum/diagonal matrix were
kept, along with full contraction PSD. The positive row scales alter only
the residual objective, not the feasibility set. Raw child coordinates,
all words, orthonormal bases, all original Q values, the entire C, all Q
dual entries, the PSD dual, the scalar dual, and complete contraction
eigenvalues are saved. No eigenvalue was clipped, no PSD repair applied,
and no nullspace deleted. Source and raw-file SHA256 hashes are recorded
in the public ledger. The executable source is
workers/max/research/max_general_transverse_discovery.py; raw files are
workers/max/research/transverse_raw/attempt_01 through attempt_03 (.npz
and .json). The ledger is research/max_general_transverse_discovery_budget.json.
The separate tiny exact audit max_transverse_exact_audit.py passed 19
checks in Q(sqrt(2)), including the seed formulas, both separator signs,
the factorization (29), and the two polynomial identities used in the
legality proof. It makes no optimization or verification call. A read-only
inspection of the saved arrays gave the identical invariant Q_(36,30)
in both length-63 runs and the two-row residual approximately
0.00256492296668. These are diagnostic consistency checks, not acceptance
by the proof verifier.

The derivations in sections 1-6 use no numerical output as a premise.
No installation, verifier call, additional solver replay, external
publication, or subagent was used. No allowance renews. This document is
an unverified proof candidate, and the project's original target remains
unresolved.

# C55: the prescribed three-boundary queried-pair recurrence is insufficient

Status: **complete scoped proof candidate, not verifier-accepted**. No
optimization or verifier allocation is used. The predecessor
`1252b8823bb5a290` supplies the interpolation convention and the requirement
that an objective certificate be positive semidefinite on all the original
formal vectors. Everything specific to this recursive family is derived
below. Prior C53/C55 findings are motivation, not mathematical premises.

The result covers precisely the H2 seed and the repeated-copy join with the
added balanced pair described below, including arbitrary horizon-dependent
join parameters and storage choices. It does not cover arbitrary seeds,
independent internal array corrections, or unrestricted certificates for the
same schedule. The test vectors are formal vectors, not a legal function.

## 1. Statement and notation

Fix an integer m>=128 and define

    epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2),
    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c,
    d_j=a c^j,
    H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+d_j,H_j),
    n_j=2^j-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

All steps are predetermined and strictly positive; the bridges grow with
the recursion level. No upper bound on the steps is assumed. The claims
below concern this explicit family, not arbitrary nonnegative schedules.

For formal gradients substitute the actual GD recursion with smoothness
one and optimum jet zero. For distinct samples define

    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,
    C_ij=-2 Q_ji
        =2(f_i-f_j)+2<g_j,x_j-x_i>-||g_i-g_j||^2.

The accepted predecessor proves C_ij>=0 for legal smooth convex data.
Formal vectors used to test a quadratic certificate need not be legal.

An array at level j has storage parameters u_j,v_j and star pattern

    lambda_(star,i)=h_i              (i<n_j),
    lambda_(star,n_j)=u_j,
    lambda_(i,star)=0                (i<n_j),
    lambda_(n_j,star)=s_j=S_j+u_j-v_j.                 (1)

Here lambda_ik denotes a multiplier array on C_ik; the scalar lambda without
indices denotes 1+sqrt(2). The full identity defines its remainder E_j:

    ||x_start||^2-||x_end-u_j g_end||^2-2v_j f_end
                   =sum lambda_ik C_ik+E_j,
    Z_j=(u_j-1)^2+v_j,
    z_j=sum_(i<n_j) h_i g_i+g_end.                    (2)

Start from the exact H2 seed in Section 2, and for j=2,...,m-1 apply any
join in Section 3, with a balanced multiplier gamma_j>=0 on the queried
pair (p,q-1). The same child array is embedded on the left and multiplied
by r_j>=0 on the right. All actual final multipliers must be nonnegative
to give a certificate. One may use any real u_j,v_j, with v_m>0, and need
not require positive semidefinite child or local remainders.

Then, if the complete objective matrix at level q_cert is PSD, necessarily

    q_cert >= 1/k_m^2
           > (2/5)^(2m) exp[2^(m-3)/m],              (3)

where the signed numbers k_j are defined in Section 5. The last expression
tends to infinity. In particular q_cert>1 for m>=128, so this family cannot
supply v_m>=M_m with E_m PSD, nor any useful bounded objective coefficient.
This is an obstruction for the enlarged C55 recurrence, not an upper or
lower bound on the actual schedule's worst-case objective.

The proof retains every internal child square and every new pair term. Its
key change from C53 is that the last-query gradient is c times the terminal
gradient, instead of equal to it.

## 2. The exact seed, its signs, and its attained boundary form

Put

    b0=1+a, M=M_1=2+a, d=d_1=1-epsilon,
    x=d/M, eta=x^2+2x-1, a0=(2-b0^2)/b0^2.

The one-step seed H1=(b0) has finite multipliers

    lambda_01=M, lambda_10=1,

star multipliers lambda_(star,0)=b0, lambda_(star,1)=M, and no incoming
star multipliers. Set u_1=M, v_1=2M-1, hence Z_1=M^2. Direct expansion in
(2) gives

    E_1=(2-b0^2)||g_0-g_1||^2.

To obtain H2, use the join of Section 3 with gamma=0 and

    A=1/x, B=2+x, r=3+x+1/x, t=1/x-2-x,
    P=(1+M eta)/x, Q=M,
    u_2=M_2=2M+d, v_2=2M_2-1.                        (4)

Here 1<b0<sqrt(2), 3/8<x<sqrt(2)-1, and eta<0. For the upper bound on x,
with s=sqrt(2)-1=1/lambda, expansion gives

    d-sM=-epsilon[1-2s^2/(1+epsilon)]<0.

The lower bound follows from d>=127/128 and M<5/2. Thus a0,t>0.
Moreover eta>=-7/64, so 1+M eta>1-35/128>0. Consequently A,B,P,Q,r are
positive. The physical star pattern has s_1=s_2=0, and the update described
in Section 3 yields nonnegative actual star multipliers as well.

For the four gradients of H2 define

    U=b0(g_0-g_1), V0=b0(g_2-g_3), D0=g_1-g_3.

Expanding the complete join gives

    E_2=a0||U||^2+(r a0+t)||V0||^2
                         +M^2(-eta)||D0||^2.          (5)

For an explicit coefficient check, (4) gives H=-t(M+d), ell=-tM in (8)
below. Substitute V=d g_1+b0 g_2+g_3, Z_1=M^2, Z_2=(2M+d)^2 into (8).
The mixed V0,D0 coefficient is A d-M=0; the D0 square coefficient is
M^2(1-2x-x^2). The two seed remainders provide a0||U||^2+r a0||V0||^2.
The remaining term is t||V0||^2, establishing (5).

Changing u_2,v_2 and only the two terminal star coefficients in (1) adds
(M_2^2-Z_2)||g_3||^2 to (5). Thus the obstruction also permits these
storage changes at the seed. The finite seed coefficients remain fixed.

With (z,e,w)=(z_2,g_3,g_2), the boundary relation is

    z-M_2 e=U+V0+(M+d)D0.

All three boundary vectors are independent. Set c0=M^2(-eta), l0=M+d.
The exact identity

    a0||U||^2+c0||D0||^2
      =a0 c0/(c0+a0 l0^2) ||U+l0 D0||^2
           +||a0 l0 U-c0 D0||^2/(c0+a0 l0^2)

has its second square equal to zero for every prescribed value of
U+l0 D0. Therefore the sharp, attained boundary form is

    K_2(z,e,w)=A2||z-M_2 e-b0(w-e)||^2
                         +B2||w-e||^2
                         +(M_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2,
    A2=[1/a0+(1+x)^2/(-eta)]^(-1),
    B2=(r a0+t)b0^2.                                 (6)

No unattainability of boundary minimizers is assumed in what follows.

## 3. The actual enlarged array and the full local identity

At a join write n=n_j, p=n, q=2n+1, S=S_j, M=M_j, d=d_j, b=1+d.
Choose A,B,P,Q with

    r=1+A+B, P-Q=b+(1-A)S.

Embed the old array on the left and r times that array on the right.
For each right queried index p<i<q add

    A h_i to (p,i), B h_i to (q,i),
    -(A+B)h_i to (star,i).

Add the six corrections

    (p,q):P, (q,p):Q,
    (p,star):-s_j, (q,star):s_(j+1)-r s_j,
    (star,p):b-u_j, (star,q):u_(j+1)-r u_j.            (7)

Finally add gamma>=0 to both (p,q-1) and (q-1,p). These are actual queried
samples. This balanced change preserves every function-value flow and
every star multiplier. It adds no incoming unqueried-terminal constraint.

For example A,B,P,Q>=0, u_j>=0 and s_j>=0 at the relevant levels suffice
for nonnegative actual arrays when the seed is nonnegative: right queried
star coefficients become (r-A-B)h_i=h_i; the two terminal star coefficients
become b and u_(j+1); the two incoming terminal star coefficients become
zero and s_(j+1). All other entries are copied or receive nonnegative
additions. The obstruction allows all real A,B,P,Q satisfying the flow
identity and r>=0, so it also covers choices whose signs are certified by
a more detailed inspection of the actual array.

Write g=g_p, e=g_q, w=g_(q-1), and define

    t=A-B,
    H=M+d-B(M-d)-tM,
    ell=P-A,
    V=x_p-x_q-g+e=d g+z_right.

The complete remainder identity is

    E_next=E_left+r E_right
       +t||V||^2+2<V,(H-ell)g+ell e>
       +(Z_j-rd^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_next)||e||^2
       +2gamma<g-w,e+a w-V>.                          (8)

Here a is the fixed parameter in b0=1+a, since every block ends with that
same step b0. To verify the new term, x_(q-1)=x_q+b0 w gives

    -gamma(C_(p,q-1)+C_(q-1,p))
       =2gamma[||g-w||^2-<g-w,x_p-x_(q-1)>]
       =2gamma<g-w,e+a w-V>.

For completeness the old part of (8) can be checked before the pair is
added. Put D=x_p-x_q and W=sum_(p<i<q)h_i g_i=x_p-b g-x_q. The rank
correction sums to

    2AS f_p+2BS f_q-AS||g||^2-BS||e||^2
                      -2<W,A(x_p-g)+B(x_q-e)>.

The value coefficient at p becomes

    -2v_j+2AS+2(P-Q)-2s_j-2(b-u_j)
            =2[(A-1)S+P-Q-b]=0.

The value coefficient at q becomes -2v_next using r=1+A+B and (1).
After subtracting the correction from the new potential minus the two
child potentials, the quadratic coefficients are

    D^2:t, (D,g):H-ell-t, (D,e):ell+t,
    g^2:Z_j-rd^2+t-2H+2ell,
    e^2:rZ_j-Z_next+t+2ell, (g,e):H-2ell-t,

where a mixed coefficient multiplies twice the indicated inner product.
Substituting V=D-g+e proves the old part of (8). Thus (8) is an identity
on the full formal space, with every inherited remainder retained.

## 4. Explicit eliminated matrix and every singular range condition

This section records the exact recurrence being obstructed, including
its singular cases. Write an attained child boundary form as

    K(z,e,w)=alpha||z||^2+2beta<z,e>+2chi<z,w>
                        +delta||e||^2+2etaK<e,w>+phi||w||^2.

In (8) substitute E_left=K(L,g,u)+its internal remainder and
E_right=K(R,e,w)+its internal remainder. The six-variable form has
parent boundary z=L+d g+R. Eliminate X=(g,u,R) and retain Y=(z,e,w).
After L=z-d g-R, its symmetric matrix is

                         [ D_el  B_el ]
                         [ B_el^T C_el].

Its entries are exactly

    D_el(1,1)=(alpha+t-r)d^2-2beta d+delta
                                      +2d(H-ell-gamma)+Z_j,
    D_el(1,2)=etaK-d chi,
    D_el(1,3)=d(alpha+t)-beta+H-ell-gamma,
    D_el(2,2)=phi,
    D_el(2,3)=-chi,
    D_el(3,3)=(1+r)alpha+t;

    B_el=[ -d alpha+beta,  d ell+gamma, gamma(a+d);
                 chi,          0,              0;
               -alpha,     r beta+ell,       r chi+gamma ];

    C_el=[ alpha,          0,                    0;
              0, r delta+rZ_j-Z_next,  r etaK-gamma;
              0,       r etaK-gamma,    r phi-2a gamma ].  (9)

The full six-variable matrix is PSD if and only if all of the following
hold:

    D_el is PSD;
    B_el^T xi=0 for EVERY xi in ker(D_el);
    C_el-B_el^T D_el^dagger B_el is PSD.                (10)

The middle condition is equivalently that every column of B_el belongs to
the range of D_el. The dagger is the inverse on its positive eigenspaces
and zero on its kernel. To prove (10), first restrict to Y=0. A nonzero
cross coefficient against a null vector of D_el makes the form unbounded
below by scaling that null direction. Once these coefficients vanish,
complete the square on the positive eigenspaces. This proves both
necessity and sufficiency, including every rank of D_el.

When the children have exact attained decompositions with PSD internal
remainders and r>=0, (10) is also equivalent to PSD of the entire parent
remainder: the independent boundary data can be lifted while setting all
internal child squares to zero. The new sharp boundary form is the last
Schur complement in (10). No such interpretation is needed for the
obstruction below: it directly evaluates the original E_j and does not
assume that any earlier elimination passed.

## 5. A different formal seed survives every new queried pair

Define

    k_2=M_2+6epsilon, theta=c.

At the H2 seed choose the following explicit four gradients, all multiples
of an arbitrary vector e:

    g_0=(2-c+6epsilon/b0)e,
    g_1=e, g_2=c e, g_3=e.                            (11)

Then the exact boundary is

    z_2=k_2 e, g_end=e, g_last_query=c e.

Indeed z_2=b0 g_0+(1+d_1)g_1+b0 g_2+g_3=(M_2+6epsilon)e.
In (5), D0=0, V0=b0(c-1)e, and U=[6epsilon-b0(c-1)]e.
We next prove the strict energy inequality

    E_2 < (k_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2                         (12)

for e!=0, for every m>=128. This includes all internal seed squares.

First, a>2/5. In fact sqrt(2)<10/7 gives lambda<17/7, so

    a >= (127/129)(7/17)=889/2193 >2/5.

Also a<s=sqrt(2)-1, b0<sqrt(2), M>12/5 and c<5/2. To bound a0, use

    b0=sqrt(2)-2s epsilon/(1+epsilon),
    2-b0^2<4sqrt(2)s epsilon=4(2-sqrt(2))epsilon
                                          <(12/5)epsilon,
    b0^2>49/25.

Therefore a0<(60/49)epsilon<(5/4)epsilon. Since 3/8<x<s<1/2,

    r=3+x+1/x<37/6<25/4.

Furthermore

    s-x=epsilon[1-2s^2/(1+epsilon)]/M<(5/12)epsilon,
    t=(s-x)(1+1/(sx))<(5/12)(23/3)epsilon<4epsilon.

The estimate on 1/(sx) uses s>2/5 and x>3/8. We have
0<6epsilon<b0(c-1), because 6epsilon<=3/64 and b0,c-1>7/5. Hence

    ||U||^2 <= b0^2(c-1)^2||e||^2,
    b0^2(c-1)^2<2(3/2)^2=9/2.

Substitution into the FULL seed formula (5) gives

    E_2-(M_2^2-Z_2)||e||^2
       <=[(r+1)a0+t] b0^2(c-1)^2||e||^2
       <[(29/4)(5/4)+4](9/2)epsilon||e||^2
       =(1881/32)epsilon||e||^2
       <60epsilon||e||^2.

Finally M_2=5-epsilon+2a>5, so

    k_2^2-M_2^2=12M_2 epsilon+36epsilon^2>60epsilon.

This proves (12). In particular the argument has not replaced the full
seed by an unattainable boundary minimum or dropped a positive square.

## 6. A uniform subcritical gap and its exact propagation

Set

    R0=k_2/(c d_2)=(M_2+6epsilon)/[(1-epsilon)c^2].

We have the explicit bound

    0<R0<1-epsilon/4.                                (13)

For its proof expand the available gap using lambda^2=2lambda+1:

    (c d_2-M_2)/epsilon
      =[6sqrt(2)+epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2
                                  -lambda^2 epsilon^3]/(1+epsilon).

This is greater than 8. Indeed after subtracting 8 and multiplying by
1+epsilon, the numerator is

    6sqrt(2)-8-7epsilon-2lambda^2 epsilon^2-lambda^2 epsilon^3
      >2/5-7/128-12/128^2-6/128^3>0.

Consequently c d_2-k_2>2epsilon. Since c d_2=(1-epsilon)c^2<25/4,

    1-R0>8epsilon/25>epsilon/4,

as claimed.

Now define signed numbers recursively by

    k_(j+1)=-k_j^2/d_j, j>=2.                         (14)

For every block H_j there is a formal gradient assignment linear in its
terminal vector e such that

    z_j=k_j e, g_end=e, g_last_query=c e,
    E_j <= (k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2.                        (15)

This holds simultaneously for every permitted choice of the join and
storage parameters, including choices depending on the target horizon.
The seed is (11)-(12). For the induction step put the right child on its
template with terminal e, and the left child on its template with terminal

    g=-k_j e/d_j.

Then V=d_j g+z_right=0, and the parent moment is

    z_next=z_left+d_j g+z_right=k_j g=k_(j+1)e.

Its last-query gradient remains c e. The crucial NEW pair term in (8) is

    2gamma(-k_j/d_j-c)(1+a c)||e||^2.                 (16)

It is nonpositive at every level. At j=2, k_2>0 proves this directly. For
j>=3, k_j<0; solving (14) gives

    |k_j|=(1-epsilon)c^j R0^(2^(j-2)),
    |k_j|/d_j=c R0^(2^(j-2))<c.                     (17)

Thus (16) is negative for gamma>0 and e!=0, since 1+a c=2-epsilon>0.

The other mixed local terms vanish because V=0. Since r>=0, the two
complete child estimates in (15) retain their direction under addition.
Keeping their full values and the nonpositive new pair term gives

    E_next <= (k_j^2-Z_j)||g||^2
                 +r(k_j^2-Z_j)||e||^2
                 +(Z_j-rd_j^2)||g||^2+(rZ_j-Z_next)||e||^2
             = (k_j^4/d_j^2-Z_next)||e||^2,

which is exactly (15) at the parent. No local PSD, nonsingularity, greedy
storage, or rank assumption was used.

In co-coercivity language the pair has

    C_(p,q-1)+C_(q-1,p)
          =2(2-epsilon)(c+k_j/d_j)||e||^2>=0.

This explains why a nonnegative weight on this actual pair cannot repair
this new formal template. The old C53 template had the opposite sign on
some such pairs; those are different formal vectors.

## 7. The original initial-vector objective conversion

Let u=u_m, v=v_m>0, Z=(u-1)^2+v and k=k_m. For the final array the full
objective certificate at any level q_cert is

    M_cert=q_cert||x_0||^2-2f_n
                     +sum_(i!=j) (2lambda_ji/v)Q_ij
           =(q_cert-1/v)||x_0||^2
                     +[||x_n-u g_n||^2+E_m]/v.        (18)

This follows directly from (2) and C_ij=-2Q_ji. In particular E_m PSD
and nonnegative actual multipliers yield the bound q_cert=1/v. The
normalization is the project's 2(f_n-f_star)/(L||x_0-x_star||^2), by the
scaling in predecessor `1252b8823bb5a290`.

Keep x_0 independent of the formal template in (15), and set
beta0=k+u-1. Since z_m=k e,

    x_n-u g_n=x_0-beta0 e.

The restriction of v M_cert is bounded ABOVE by the two-variable form
with matrix

                  [ q_cert v    -beta0       ]
                  [  -beta0    2k beta0-v    ].        (19)

The difference is a nonnegative multiple of ||e||^2, by (15). If the
original M_cert is PSD, this upper matrix must therefore also be PSD.
Its second diagonal is positive: if it were zero, PSD would force
beta0=0, making that diagonal -v<0. Its determinant now implies

    q_cert >= beta0^2/[v(2k beta0-v)] >=1/k^2,

where the last step is exactly

    k^2 beta0^2-v(2k beta0-v)=(v-k beta0)^2>=0.

This includes arbitrary increases of q_cert and indefinite child or final
remainders. It tests the original x_0 variable, not only the condensed
gradient state.

From (13),(17) and 1-x<=exp(-x),

    |k_m|<c^m exp[-2^(m-4)/m].                        (20)

Thus (3) follows from c<5/2. The logarithm of its right side is
2^(m-3)/m-2m log(5/2), which tends to infinity because 2^m/m^2 tends to
infinity. Its successive ratio is 2m^2/(m+1)^2>3/2 for m>=7.
For m>=128, (20) also gives |k_m|<1: log(5/2)<1 follows from
exp(1)>1+1+1/2+1/6>5/2, and 2^(m-4)>m^2 for m>=128. The latter inequality
holds at 128 and is preserved since 2m^2/(m+1)^2>1. Hence q_cert>1.

## 8. Undeformed calibration and scope checks

Set epsilon=0, so a=1/lambda, c=lambda. Then b0=sqrt(2), M_j=lambda^j,
d_j=lambda^(j-1), a0=t=eta=0, and the physical seed E_2 is identically
zero. At each higher join choose

    A=B=lambda, r=lambda^2, t=H=ell=0,
    P=lambda, Q=M_j,
    u_j=(1+sqrt(4M_j^2-3))/2, v_j=u_j, Z_j=M_j^2.

The same storage choice can be made at H2 by its permitted terminal-star
change, preserving E_2=0. It has s_j=S_j>=0. The flow equation follows
from M_j=lambda d_j, and (8) with gamma=0 gives E_next=0. Every actual
multiplier is nonnegative by Section 3. This is an exact valid undeformed
calibration of the full array and the objective conversion (18).

If one retains the physical H2 storage u_2=M_2,v_2=2M_2-1 instead, the
seed still has Z_2=M_2^2; switching to the displayed u_j,v_j at the next
level likewise changes only the two terminal star coefficients and is
allowed. No calibration identity is being carried from a differently
normalized performance measure.

At epsilon=0 the obstruction template has k_2=M_2 and R0=1, so it yields
only q_cert>=lambda^(-2m), entirely compatible with the valid bound
1/u_m of order lambda^(-m). The strict horizon-dependent gap in (13)
is essential.

The hypothesis r>=0 is exactly the scaling restriction in the C55 state
recurrence, not an extra PSD assumption on its children. It is also
forced by nonnegative final arrays in this prescribed construction. The
seed has the positive coefficient lambda_01=M. At every larger level,
the first two queried samples retain this coefficient unchanged, because
they lie in the left child and none of the join corrections touches their
ordered pair. In a right child the corresponding ordered pair has
coefficient rM and is untouched by the parent's fan, sparse, and new-pair
corrections. In the final leftmost occurrence of any such join it remains
untouched by all higher joins. Thus a negative r at any level would leave
a negative actual coefficient in the final array. This also shows that
the proof covers arrays required to be nonnegative only at the final
level. The case r=0 is already included in the energy induction.

The seed restriction matters: the result does not assert that every H2
array has (5), or that every three-boundary seed is covered. A different
seed has to be tested against the seed-energy and last-query conditions
(12),(15). Arbitrary additional queried pairs can change (16); no claim
about those corrections is made here. Likewise the formal template need
not satisfy omitted interpolation constraints, so it is not an actual
lower witness for H_m. Accepted terminal completion removes no queried
prefix constraints and cannot justify dropping them.

This analysis makes no lower-bound claim for schedules with zero steps.
The accepted predecessor handles those schedules without identifying
duplicated formal gradients. Any future unrestricted conclusion would
still have to retain both zero-step and arbitrarily-large-step cases.

All new mathematics in this file remains a proof candidate. The exact
minimax/profile target remains unresolved, and the fact graph is unchanged.

## 9. Reproducibility of the algebraic audit

One fixed rational substitution in the six-variable identity (9) was
checked by polarization of its 21 coefficients, and the four rational
constant margins in Sections 5-6 were checked exactly. These were tiny
arithmetic checks, not optimization or a numerical search. The displayed
expansions and inequalities are the proof; the checks are not premises.

For architecture context, the exact local text of Altschuler--Parrilo,
arXiv:2309.16530, Theorem 3.4 and its helper-lemma proof was reread. No
theorem from that paper is imported; its undeformed recursion motivated
the array, whose modified full identity is proved in Section 3.
