# Free terminal completion of queried-constant cells

Status: complete UNVERIFIED joint candidate responding to scope guidance
`232646b088cca20e`. No solver or fact_submit. The accepted mathematical
premise is `1252b8823bb5a290`, with the exact benchmark definitions in
`b98bb807d514e80d`. The shared evidence includes the complete independent
two-cell, ruler-mass and benchmark derivations used below.

The terminal gradient is FREE throughout this document. It need not equal
the last queried gradient. The actual last displacement is always retained.

## 1. Exact all-anchor completion, including all old values

Fix every legal queried prefix jet (x_i,g_i,f_i), including the optimum
(0,0,0), and put y_i=x_i-g_i, delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2. Accepted fact
1252b8823bb5a290 gives the one fixed maximal prefix interpolant

    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_i(<y_i,v>-delta_i),
    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).                            (1)

It retains EVERY supplied prefix value, position and gradient. It is
convex and 1-smooth with F(0)=min F=0. The next terminal point x_n is
computed from the actual prefix gradients and actual final step before
evaluating (1). No terminal gradient is prescribed. Every other legal
completion has value at most F(x_n).

An equivalent exact formula, with ALL anchors present, is

    F(x)=min_{alpha_i>=0, sum alpha_i=1}
             [sum_i alpha_i delta_i
                      +||x-sum_i alpha_i y_i||^2/2].  (2)

Here is a direct proof of equality. Every probability vector alpha in
(2) bounds the maximum of the affine anchors in (1) below by their
alpha average, so (1) is at most the displayed objective. That objective
has a minimizer on the compact simplex. At a minimizing alpha, put
v=x-sum alpha_i y_i. Its partial derivatives are
delta_i-<y_i,v>. The directional derivatives to each vertex show that
all these derivatives are at least their alpha average, with equality
at every positive alpha_i. Hence the largest affine anchor at v equals
its alpha average. Evaluating (1) at this v gives precisely the objective
in (2), proving the reverse inequality too. Thus (2) describes the actual
maximal interpolant, not a relaxed function-value surrogate.

Within a queried block with one common gradient, both interpolation
directions force the values to be affine in cumulative step mass. Its
y_i and delta_i are therefore affine in that same mass. Every interior
anchor is an exact convex combination of the first and last anchor of
that block. Retaining the two endpoints is an equality-preserving
compression of their maximum in (1), NOT deletion of a constraint.
Two queried cells require at most five anchors including the optimum;
three require at most seven. Additional previously retained anchors are
included unchanged. If 0 was a closest minimizer of a legal original
interpolant, it remains closest for F: F is pointwise greater, its zero
set is a subset of the old zero set, and it still contains 0.

## 2. A rigorous free-completion bound with its explicit finite loss

Consider two queried cells with ordinary first mass S, intervening
checkpoint B>=0, and total mass T after that checkpoint. Suppose the
last physical step is b in[0,2], T>=b, and the second queried cell is
nonempty. It has one common queried gradient g through x_(n-1), but
the gradient at x_n=x_(n-1)-b g is free. Let R be the initial distance
to the retained optimum. Set

    M=1+S, epsilon=(B-1)/M,
    sigma_b=1+2T-b^2>=1.                              (3)

Then the actual maximal-prefix completion (1) obeys

    2F(x_n)/R^2 <= max {
       1/[1+2(S+B+T)-b^2],
       (B-1)_+^2/[M^2(1+2T-b^2)] }.                  (4)

This is a proved upper bound, not a claim that (4) is the sharp finite
completed-domain value. Its finite denominator loss relative to the
constant-through-terminal two-cell formula is explicitly b^2.

To prove (4), apply 1-smoothness to the ACTUAL F from (1) at its last
queried jet, which is fixed by interpolation:

    2F(x_n)<=2f_(n-1)-(2b-b^2)||g||^2.                (5)

Write the first-block endpoint data exactly as in the two-cell proof:
initial point -R e, first gradient q, y=-R e-Mq,
delta=f_j-||q||^2/2, d=-Mq. The last queried point is
y+epsilon d-(T-b)g. Interpolation to the retained first-block endpoint
and to the optimum, followed by (5), gives

    2F(x_n)<=2delta+2epsilon<g,d>-sigma_b||g||^2,
    2F(x_n)<=2<g,y+epsilon d>-sigma_b||g||^2.          (6)

All anchors and all prefix pairs remain in (1). The two inequalities
in (6) are necessary properties of that same maximal interpolant. In
particular this is not an attempt to turn a reduced set of anchors
into a different function.

Use z=-M<q,e>/R and w=M^2||q||^2/R^2. The optimum still implies
z^2<=w<=z and delta<=R^2(z-w)/M. The exact J_s square calculation
in the joint two-cell proof applies with sigma=sigma_b and k=sigma_b/M.
Its case split remains valid: sigma_b>=1 ensures k+epsilon>=0 whenever
epsilon<0, and epsilon>=-1. It proves (4) without imposing any terminal
gradient equality. There is no terminal stationarity assumption.

## 3. The entire two-queried-cell class remains below the benchmark

Return to the ACTUAL ruler H_m, m>=128, with last physical step
b_*=d_0=1+a in(1,3/2), total mass M_rul=1+sum H_m, and

    K=1/(2M_rul-1), d=1+b_*^2<=13/4.

All gradients at queried samples 0,...,i are one arbitrary q, and all
at i+1,...,n-1 are one arbitrary g, for any split i<n-1. All queried
values and both vectors are otherwise unrestricted except legality;
the terminal value and gradient are completed by (1). Equation (4)
gives an upper bound by the larger of

    K_free=1/(2M_rul-1-b_*^2),
    K_i_free=(h_i-1)^2/
              [(1+P_i)^2(1+2T_i-b_*^2)],            (7)

where P_i,T_i are the actual masses before and after the checkpoint i.

We next bound all K_i_free at once, including level zero and late cuts.
Put p=1+P_i, t=1+T_i, r_i=h_i-1, r=c-2. Each ruler cut has its own
level subword on both sides, so the joint audit's mass identities give

    r_i<=r p, r_i<=r t, M_rul=p+t+r_i,
    c<=49/20<5/2, r<=9/20<1/2,
    t>=1+b_*, M_rul>=128.

Abbreviate M_rul to M for these estimates. If p<=t then M<=c t, so

    K_i_free/K <=r^2(2M-1)/(2M/c-d)<3/4.             (8)

For an explicit check, this is at most
M/[2((4/5)M-13/4)], which is <=3/4 for M>=195/8.

If p>=t then p>=M/c and t<=M/2. Hence

    K_i_free<=r^2 c^2 t^2/[M^2(2t-d)].

The derivative of t^2/(2t-d) is 2t(t-d)/(2t-d)^2, so its maximum
on [1+b_*,M/2] is at an endpoint. At the left endpoint,

    (1+b_*)^2/(1+2b_*-b_*^2)<=25/7.

Since r^2 c^2<5/4, its ratio to K is below 125/(14M)<3/4.
At the right endpoint the ratio is at most

    (r c)^2(2M-1)/[4(M-d)]
        <5M/[8(M-d)]<=3/4,                            (9)

where M>=128>6d. Thus EVERY K_i_free is <=(3/4)K.
Together (7)--(9) give the complete-domain conclusion

    sup_(two queried cells, free terminal) 2F(x_n)/R^2
           <=K_free=K/(1-b_*^2 K).                  (10)

If there is only one queried cell, including the split i=n-1, apply
the exact two-cell theorem with first ordinary mass sum H_m-b_*,
checkpoint b_*, and EMPTY final ordinary block. That theorem already
allows an arbitrary terminal gradient when the final block is empty.
It bounds this case by max{K,a^2/(M_rul-b_*)^2}=K for M_rul>=128.
Thus (10) includes one queried cell as well.

The joint audit proves for the actual benchmark B_n that
limsup B_(2^m-1)K<=2/e and K->0. Consequently

    limsup B_(2^m-1)
       sup_(two queried cells, free terminal) 2F(x_n)/R^2
           <=2/e<1.                                (11)

The finite statement K_free, rather than an unproved exact K bound,
is what is established for this relaxed class. Its multiplicative
loss is 1/(1-b_*^2 K)=1+O(K), explicitly vanishing with the horizon.

## 4. The retained positive-height prefix: even three queried cells fail

Retain the initial saturated first block p=e/(1+P), the first selected
checkpoint i, and its actual outgoing position -A e, A=(h_i-1)/(1+P).
Allow any legal constant second gradient through selected checkpoint
j>i, with ANY positive proximal height or affine intercept, and any
constant queried third gradient after j through the last query. Complete
the terminal freely using (1), with EVERY earlier anchor retained.
The old saturated jet forces the true initial distance to the retained
optimum to be exactly 1 for every completion.

If j<n-1, applying (4) to the actual suffix starting at -A e gives

    2F(x_n)<=max {K_i_free, R_ij^2/(1+2T-b_*^2)},     (12)

where P,S,T are the actual masses before i, between i,j, and after j,
and R_ij=(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/[(1+P)(1+S)]. No new initial normalization
has been substituted: the factor A^2 is included in both terms of (12).

The first term is <=(3/4)K by Section 3. For the second put t=1+T,
M=M_rul, q_pair=c(c-2)^2 and
D_pair=2(c-1)q_pair+(c-2)^2. The joint ruler proof gives

    R_ij<=min(q_pair,D_pair t/M),
    q_pair D_pair<=661/800<5/6,
    D_pair/q_pair<=199/60,
    D_pair<=661/400<5/3.

The crossover t_0=q_pair M/D_pair exceeds 1+b_* for M>=128.
The same derivative calculation as in Section 3 shows that the whole
modified pair bound is at most

    max { (D_pair^2/M^2)(1+b_*)^2/(1+2b_*-b_*^2),
          q_pair D_pair/[2M-d D_pair/q_pair] }.       (13)

The first alternative divided by K is below 1250/(63M)<9/10.
For the second, d D_pair/q_pair<=2587/240<11, so its ratio is below

    (5/6) 2M/(2M-11)<=128/147<9/10.                 (14)

If j=n-1, the terminal is the sole sample after the second checkpoint.
The exact two-cell theorem already allows it to be free, and gives
max{K_i,R_ij^2}. The first term is <=K_i_free<3K/4; the ruler bound
at t=1 gives R_ij^2<=D_pair^2/M^2, with ratio to K below 50/(9M).
Thus this boundary case satisfies the same conclusion.

Every legal retained-positive-height three-queried-cell completion
therefore satisfies the uniform bound

    2F(x_n)<= (9/10)K,
    limsup B_(2^m-1)2F(x_n)<=9/(5e)<1.               (15)

This closes the precise free-terminal loophole in that mechanism.
It is still a scoped upper bound, not a bound on unrestricted W.

## 5. Zero-height starts and the Markov obstruction need NO loss

For completeness, a sharper conclusion applies whenever the queried
tail starts at distance R_0 from an already retained actual minimizer,
and its queried gradient is constant until its last physical step b
in[0,3/2]. Let its total physical mass be T>=b. The exact two-cell
theorem, now with first ordinary mass T-b, checkpoint b and empty
terminal block, gives

    2F(x_end)<=R_0^2 max{1/(1+2T),
                         (b-1)_+^2/(1+T-b)^2}
                 =R_0^2/(1+2T).                    (16)

To check the equality of the alternatives, only b>1 needs attention.
Write e=b-1 in[0,1/2] and p=1+T-b>=1. The required inequality is
e^2(2p+2e+1)<=p^2. The difference increases with p>=1; at p=1 it is
1-e^2(3+2e)>=0, because the right polynomial increases to 1 at e=1/2.

If an old query has f_j=||g_j||^2/2, its proximal point y_j=x_j-g_j
is a true minimizer: smoothness gives f(y_j)<=0. This remains true for
the maximal completion (1), which retains that jet. Equation (16)
therefore applies to the actual displacement from y_j and entails
EXACTLY the old constant-through-terminal cap, now for a FREE terminal.

In particular the whole zero-height two-block family in the earlier
ruler audit retains its bound (5949/6088)K under this relaxation.
The ascending Markov relay in `max_ascending_markov_ruler_obstruction.md`
also retains its entire bound unchanged: replace its final Section 3
argument by (16) at the actual minimizer Z_v and distance
R_0=r_v alpha_v. Thus Gamma_0 and Gamma_1 eta^(k-1) remain valid even
when the terminal gradient is completed freely from ALL queried anchors.

The new scope check is now complete. A free last gradient cannot repair
these mechanisms at benchmark scale on the assigned ruler. Genuine
multiple queried-gradient changes with richer retained geometry remain
the open task. None of the present candidates is verifier-accepted.
# Exact two-cell objective and the positive-height ruler obstruction

Status: a self-contained UNVERIFIED candidate. No numerical search or
fact_submit. The accepted interpolation fact `1252b8823bb5a290` is the
only correctness premise in Sections 1--4. For the ruler specialization,
this file is part of the JOINT candidate with the complete independent
derivations in `max_near_critical_ruler_audit.md`; the shared publication
includes both texts. No unverified finding is adopted as an accepted fact.

## 1. The entire two-cell class has the exact single-cut value

Fix two finite, possibly empty lists of nonnegative GD steps, with sums
S and T. Put a checkpoint b>=0 between them. Consider all convex
differentiable 1-smooth functions f on any finite-dimensional Euclidean
space, with a minimizer x_star, and an initial point at distance R>0
from x_star. Require that one vector q is the gradient at EVERY query
in the first ordinary block and at its ending checkpoint query, and
that one vector g is the gradient at EVERY query after the checkpoint,
including the terminal sample. Allow q=g, arbitrary values, arbitrary
affine offsets, and any additional dimensions.

Then the supremum of 2(f_end-f_star)/R^2 in this entire class is exactly

    max { 1/[1+2(S+b+T)],
          (b-1)_+^2/[(1+S)^2(1+2T)] }.                 (1)

Both alternatives are attained by explicit scalar functions with unique
minimizer 0. The statement includes b=0, b=1 and zero ordinary steps.
It upper-bounds every legal interpolant in the class, not just the
envelope of a prescribed set of homogeneous planes.

Translate the chosen minimizer to 0 and subtract f_star. Set

    M=1+S, epsilon=(b-1)/M, sigma=1+2T, k=sigma/M.

Write the initial point as -R e, with ||e||=1. The last first-block
query is x_j=-R e-(M-1)q. Its proximal point and height are

    y=-R e-Mq, delta=f(x_j)-||q||^2/2.

The two interpolation inequalities with the optimum imply

    0<=delta<=<q,y>.

Define the dimensionless scalars

    z=-M<q,e>/R, w=M^2||q||^2/R^2.

Cauchy--Schwarz and the preceding bound give

    z^2<=w<=z, 0<=z<=1,
    delta<=R^2(z-w)/M.                                (2)

There is no restriction on q's direction beyond these necessary
conditions. In particular positive height and nonzero plane intercepts
are included, since delta may be any legal value below this upper bound.

Put d=-Mq. The checkpoint sends x_j to y+epsilon d. The terminal
point is y+epsilon d-Tg. The outgoing terminal interpolation inequality
to x_j, and the one to the optimum, give respectively

    2f_end<=2delta+2epsilon<g,d>-sigma||g||^2,
    2f_end<=2<g,y+epsilon d>-sigma||g||^2.              (3)

For every s in [0,1], weight the optimum inequality by s and the
old-query inequality by 1-s, then complete a square in g. This yields

    2f_end<=2(1-s)delta
             +||-R s e+(epsilon+s)d||^2/sigma
             -sigma||g-[-R s e+(epsilon+s)d]/sigma||^2.

Drop only the final nonpositive square and use (2). Thus

    (sigma/R^2) 2f_end <= J_s(z,w),                    (4)
    J_s = s^2+[2k(1-s)-2s(epsilon+s)]z
                  +[(epsilon+s)^2-2k(1-s)]w.

Every weight in this calculation is nonnegative. The old jet and the
actual optimum have both been retained at their actual positions.

## 2. A complete symbolic bound for (4)

First suppose epsilon>=1. Take s=1. Since w<=z and 0<=z<=1,

    J_1<=1+(epsilon^2-1)z<=epsilon^2.                  (5)

Next suppose 0<=epsilon<=1 and

    k(1-epsilon)<=2epsilon^2.

Take s=epsilon. The terms in (4) rearrange exactly as

    J_epsilon=epsilon^2
          +[2k(1-epsilon)-4epsilon^2](z-w)
          <=epsilon^2.                               (6)

In the remaining regime -1<epsilon<=1 and

    k(1-epsilon)>=2epsilon^2,

define

    D=1+2epsilon+k,
    s_0=(epsilon+k)/D,
    z_0=1/[k+2(1+epsilon)],
    L_0=k/[k+2(1+epsilon)],
    C_0=(1+epsilon)[k+2(1+epsilon)]
                    [2epsilon^2-k(1-epsilon)]/D^2.

Here D>0, 0<=s_0<=1, and C_0<=0. To check the first two assertions
when epsilon<0, use the physical identity

    k+epsilon=(2T+b)/M>=0,
    D=(1+epsilon)+(k+epsilon)>0.

Expansion of the coefficients in (4) gives the exact identity

    J_(s_0)(z,w)
       =L_0+C_0[(z-z_0)^2+w-z^2]<=L_0.              (7)

For clarity, the coefficient of w is C_0, that of z is -2C_0 z_0,
and the constant is L_0+C_0 z_0^2=s_0^2. These checks establish (7)
without any exchange of maximization and minimization.

All negative epsilon are covered by (7), apart from epsilon=-1:
indeed epsilon>=-1/M>=-1, and k>=-epsilon when epsilon<0, so

    k(1-epsilon)-2epsilon^2
       >=-epsilon(1+epsilon)>=0.

If epsilon=-1, necessarily M=1 and b=0. Taking s=1 in (4) gives
J_1=1 for all z,w, hence 2f_end<=R^2/sigma, which is exactly the
first alternative of (1). This includes T=0; no denominator vanishes.

For epsilon>=0, comparison of L_0 and epsilon^2 gives

    L_0>=epsilon^2
       iff epsilon<=1 and k(1-epsilon)>=2epsilon^2.

Thus (5)--(7) cover every b>=0 and give

    2f_end/R^2 <= max {1/[sigma+2M(1+epsilon)],
                       (epsilon_+)^2/sigma}.

Since sigma+2M(1+epsilon)=1+2(S+b+T), this is (1)'s upper bound.
This argument never assumes that a separately optimized height bound
or distance bound is attained. Their convex combination is essential.

## 3. Actual attaining functions and all-query checks

Let U=S+b+T and a=R/(1+2U). For the first alternative use the symmetric
Huber function whose derivative is clip(x,-a,a), whose value at 0 is 0,
and start at x_0=R. At every supplied query the cumulative step mass u
satisfies 0<=u<=U and

    x(u)=R-u a>=R-U a=(1+U)a>=a.

Hence every actual gradient is a, and the normalized final objective
is [2aR-(2U+1)a^2]/R^2=1/(1+2U). The derivative is continuous,
nondecreasing and 1-Lipschitz; its unique zero is 0.

For the second alternative suppose b>1. Put

    p=R/M, a=R epsilon/sigma>0,
    f'(x)=max(-a,min(p,x)), f(0)=0.

This derivative again defines one convex differentiable 1-smooth
function with unique minimizer 0. Before the checkpoint every query
is R-u p>=p for 0<=u<=S, so its gradient is p. The checkpoint starts
at p and lands at -(b-1)p=-R epsilon. On the tail, every cumulative
mass v in [0,T] gives

    x(v)=-R epsilon+v a
         <=-R epsilon+T a=-(T+1)a<=-a.

Thus every tail gradient, including the terminal one, is -a. Its exact
normalized objective is

    [2aR epsilon-(2T+1)a^2]/R^2=epsilon^2/sigma.

The same fixed function realizes every individual prescribed step in
its actual order. When b<=1 the second alternative of (1) is zero and
the first construction already attains the maximum. This proves (1).

## 4. Positive-height two-block prefixes cannot evade the bound

Now start at e with ||e||=1 and optimum 0. Select two checkpoint
indices i<j in an arbitrary nonnegative schedule, both with h_i,h_j>1.
Let P,S,T be the sums strictly before i, strictly between i and j,
and strictly after j. Assume only the following constant-gradient
structure of a legal trajectory:

* The gradient before i, including query i, is p=e/(1+P).
* The gradient strictly after i through query j is one arbitrary q.
* The gradient strictly after j through the terminal query is one g.

The function may have any extra dimensions, affine offsets, or
positive queried values. No particular polyhedral representation is
assumed. Let

    A=(h_i-1)/(1+P), epsilon=(h_j-1)/(1+S), sigma=1+2T.

The first checkpoint query is exactly p. The optimum interpolation
inequalities force f(p)=||p||^2/2. Its next iterate is -A e. Moreover
every minimizer v satisfies <p,v><=0, by interpolation with this
saturated jet. Consequently ||e-v||>=1 and 0 is the true closest
minimizer to the ORIGINAL starting point, at distance 1.

Apply Sections 1--3 to the actual suffix starting at -A e, its first
ordinary mass S, checkpoint h_j, and last ordinary mass T. This is an
upper bound relative to the retained optimum 0; it does not reset the
original normalization or claim that 0 is the suffix's closest minimum.
It gives the universal bound

    2f_end <= max { K_i, R_ij^2/(1+2T) },              (8)
    K_i=(h_i-1)^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2(S+h_j+T))],
    R_ij=(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/[(1+P)(1+S)].

Every prefix-to-suffix and suffix-to-prefix inequality remains
necessary for an actual trajectory. The upper argument uses a subset
of them, so none can invalidate the upper bound or enlarge the class
being bounded. In particular (8) covers every positive-height prefix
proposed in Section 12 of the joint near-critical audit, all its angles
and gradient norms, AND legal nonzero intercept variants of that prefix.

## 5. Quantitative specialization to the assigned ruler

Use precisely the fixed-per-horizon parameters and schedule

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    H_0=(), H_(ell+1)=(H_ell,1+a c^ell,H_ell),
    n=2^m-1, M_ell=1+sum H_ell, m>=128.

Put r=c-2 and K=1/(2M_m-1). The independent calculations in the
joint audit give M_ell<=c^ell, M_(ell+1)<=c M_ell,
a c^ell<=r M_ell, c<=49/20 and r<=9/20.

Here is the slight strengthening of the audit's single-cut estimate
needed for (8). Within all occurrences of one level, the cut denominator
(1+P)^2[1+2(total-step-P)] has its minimum at the first or last
occurrence, because its derivative has the sign of 2(total-step)-3P.
At either endpoint, the bounds from the immediate subwords imply

    K_i<=r^2/(2M_(m-1)-1).

Indeed the first occurrence has 1+P=M_level, later mass at least
M_(m-1)-1, and step excess at most r M_level. At the last occurrence
the preceding mass is at least M_(m-1)-1 and later mass is M_level-1;
use that x^2/(2x-1) increases for x>=1. Therefore

    K_i/K <=r^2(2c M_(m-1)-1)/(2M_(m-1)-1)
           <=r^2(2c-1)<=3159/4000<5949/6088.          (9)

The joint audit also proves, for every ordered pair with the ACTUAL
prefix/intermediate/tail masses, without any angle restriction,

    R_ij^2/(1+2T) <= (5949/6088)K.                  (10)

Its full proof is included in the shared joint evidence. In brief, with
p=1+P, x=1+S, t=1+T, q_pair=c r^2 and
D_pair=2(1+r)q_pair+r^2, the nested subword and palindrome bounds give

    R_ij<=min(q_pair,D_pair t/M_m),
    q_pair D_pair<=661/800, D_pair/q_pair<=199/60.

The increasing function t^2/(2t-1) and decreasing function 1/(2t-1)
give R_ij^2/(2t-1)<=q_pair D_pair/(2M_m-D_pair/q_pair).
For M_m>=8 its ratio to K is at most 5949/6088.

Combining (8)--(10), EVERY legal trajectory in Section 4's full
positive-height class satisfies

    2f_end <=(5949/6088)/(2 sum H_m+1).               (11)

The exact benchmark is B_n=2 S_bullet(n)+1 from accepted fact
`b98bb807d514e80d`. The joint audit derives from its actual recursions
B_n<=1+4n^rho, rho=log_2(lambda), and M_m/lambda^m -> e. Thus

    limsup_(m->infinity) B_(2^m-1)
        sup_(Section 4 class) 2f_end
             <=(5949/6088)*2/e<1.                  (12)

The supremum is over genuinely legal functions and hence accounts for
every original interpolation pair; (12) does not assume any relaxed
upper value is attained. Positive retained height, its actual location,
and the optimum have now been treated jointly for the WHOLE class.
Optimizing its height, norm, angle, intercept or ambient dimension cannot
reach the benchmark on all these horizons. This is not an upper bound
for unrestricted W_n(H_m).

## 6. The same calculation locates the remaining varying-tail budget

Keep Section 4's first two blocks, including arbitrary legal height in
the second block, but let the actual tail gradients vary. Define

    Delta=sum_(tail l) h_l(g_end-g_l),
    C_tail=2<g_end,Delta>.

Its terminal position is now y+epsilon d-T g_end+Delta. Both outgoing
inequalities (3) acquire the SAME extra term C_tail. All their convex
combinations acquire that term too. Consequently the complete proof
above gives, without any sign assumption on C_tail,

    2f_end<=max{K_i,R_ij^2/(1+2T)}+C_tail.            (13)

The proof also retains the nonpositive square in (4), using the explicit
weight appropriate to the schedule. On the assigned ruler, any such
positive-height/varying-tail trajectory achieving 2f_end>=1/B_n must
therefore obey

    C_tail>=1/B_n-(5949/6088)K,
    liminf B_n C_tail>=1-(5949/6088)*2/e.             (14)

Thus changing the retained second height does not remove the previous
quantitative correlation obligation. The materially different next
object must use actual correlated changes in the tail, or change the
first saturated-prefix geometry itself. Neither a new one-cell
optimization nor a height-only state can repair this closed mechanism.

No positive all-horizon witness or full-class improving upper
certificate is claimed. The original target remains unresolved.
# A uniform obstruction for the ascending Markov ruler relay

Status: complete UNVERIFIED candidate, with no solver or fact_submit.
This screens an explicit varying-gradient family on the ACTUAL assigned
ruler. It is not an unrestricted objective upper certificate. The only
accepted mathematical premise for the geometric proof is interpolation
fact `1252b8823bb5a290`. The shared joint evidence includes the independent
mass and exact-benchmark calculations from the near-critical audit.

## 1. Actual partition and the full candidate data family

Fix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c, and

    H_0=(), H_(l+1)=(H_l,1+a c^l,H_l), n=2^m-1,
    M_l=1+sum H_l, r_l=a c^l, theta_l=r_l/M_l, r=c-2.

Every parameter is fixed throughout the horizon. Choose ANY integers
0<=u<=v<=m-1. Select the FIRST occurrence of each consecutive level
u,u+1,...,v as checkpoints. The initial ordinary block is exactly H_u;
between the first occurrences of level l and l+1 the ordinary block is
exactly H_l. Let T be the total mass strictly after the selected level v.
Then T>=M_(m-1)-1, since this first occurrence is in or at the end of
the left half of H_m. The entire right copy H_(m-1) follows it.

The construction uses one constant gradient omega_l on the ordinary
block ending at checkpoint l. Its ordinary capacity is

    C_u=M_u, C_l=M_(l-1) for u<l<=v.

Choose arbitrary parameters 0<beta_l<=theta_l for u<=l<v, and an
orthonormal sequence e_u,...,e_v in an otherwise unrestricted ambient
space. Define actual Euclidean vectors by

    alpha_u=1/M_u, zeta_u=e_u,
    alpha_(l+1)^2=theta_l beta_l alpha_l^2,
    a_l=sqrt(beta_l/theta_l),
    zeta_(l+1)=-a_l zeta_l+sqrt(1-a_l^2)e_(l+1),
    omega_l=alpha_l zeta_l.                            (1)

Start at x_0=e_u. Let X_l be the start of block l, and put

    Z_l=X_l-C_l omega_l,
    X_(l+1)=Z_l-r_l omega_l,
    Z_(l+1)=Z_l-r_l omega_l-M_l omega_(l+1).           (2)

At the actual ending query of block l, prescribe the gradient omega_l
and value ||omega_l||^2/2: its proximal height is zero. Within that
ordinary block the gradient remains omega_l at each actual query.
After checkpoint v, allow ANY single common terminal-cell gradient,
arbitrary heights and intercepts, and additional dimensions.

The family under consideration consists of ALL legal realizations of
these data by one fixed convex differentiable 1-smooth function f,
with f(0)=min f=0. We do NOT claim every parameter choice in (1) is
realizable. The theorem below upper-bounds every legal choice, using
necessary old/new interpolation inequalities. Zero beta degeneracies
can be included by continuity, or directly: a zero gradient at a zero
height endpoint is a minimizer and all subsequent prescribed Markov
gradients and the actual trajectory are zero.

The target of the screen is whether beta_l/theta_l can stay close to 1
over many levels, so that the apparent amplification c r>1 survives.

## 2. Old-query constraints force a fixed loss at every interior transfer

From (1), every zeta_l is unit. For l<j, taking successive inner
products gives

    <omega_l,omega_j>
       =(-1)^(j-l) alpha_l^2 product_(k=l)^(j-1) beta_k.

Also M_l alpha_(l+1)=r_l alpha_l a_l. Hence the increment in (2)
is -r_l alpha_l(zeta_l+a_l zeta_(l+1)), orthogonal to every later
zeta_j, j>=l+1. Since Z_u=0, it follows that

    <omega_l,Z_l>=0.

Define the adjacent residual R_l=r_l-M_l beta_l. For l+2<=v, (2)
and the inner products above give the exact identities

    <omega_l,Z_(l+2)>
       =-alpha_l^2[R_l-beta_l R_(l+1)],
    <omega_l,omega_(l+2)>=alpha_l^2 beta_l beta_(l+1),
    alpha_(l+2)^2
       =alpha_l^2 theta_l theta_(l+1) beta_l beta_(l+1).

The first actual query in block l+2 has proximal point
Z_(l+2)+(M_(l+1)-1)omega_(l+2). Its height equals
(M_(l+1)-1)alpha_(l+2)^2. To see the latter equality, both directions
of interpolation between constant-gradient queries force their values
to differ by the common gradient dotted with their position difference;
the ending height of this block is zero by assumption.

In proximal notation y_i=x_i-g_i and delta_i=f_i-||g_i||^2/2,
the accepted interpolation inequality is precisely

    delta_j>=delta_i+<g_i,y_j-y_i>.

Apply it from the ending query of block l to the first query in block
l+2. The first is an old ACTUAL sample, not a discarded auxiliary point.
Using the displayed identities yields the necessary condition

    r_l-beta_l(M_l+r_(l+1))
                   +D_l beta_l beta_(l+1)>=0,         (3)
    D_l=1+(M_(l+1)-1)theta_l theta_(l+1).

Thus no choice of further planes, offsets, ambient dimension, or final
completion can evade (3) while retaining the stated actual data.

For the assigned parameters the exact mass formula from the joint audit
is M_l=A_mass c^l+(1-A_mass)2^l, with A_mass=a/(c-2) in(0,1).
In particular

    a<=theta_l<=r<=9/20,
    c<=49/20, ac>=127/128,
    M_(l+1)/M_l=2+theta_l.

These identities give

    D_l/M_l
       =(1-theta_l theta_(l+1))/M_l
                         +(2+theta_l)theta_l theta_(l+1)
       <=1+(1+r)r^2.

Since beta_(l+1)<=theta_(l+1)<=r and c theta_l>=ac,

    1+c theta_l-(D_l/M_l)beta_(l+1)
       >=1+127/128-[r+(1+r)r^3]
       >=225609/160000 >7/5.                          (4)

Here the last equality bound substitutes r=9/20 into the increasing
polynomial r+r^3+r^4; the difference from 7/5 is 1609/160000.
Divide (3) by M_l and use (4). For EVERY l with l+2<=v,

    beta_l <= (5/7)theta_l,
    a_l^2 <=5/7.                                     (5)

This is a fixed loss, not a small perturbation of full antialignment.
In this Markov realization the fresh squared component 1-a_l^2 is
at least 2/7 at every such transfer. The same requirement arises from
just one retained old query; all remaining inequalities can only
further restrict the family.

## 3. The loss dominates the near-critical amplification

Let k=v-u be the number of Markov transfers. From (1),(5), with the
last beta_(v-1) using only beta_(v-1)<=theta_(v-1),

    alpha_v^2 <= M_u^(-2) product_(l=u)^(v-1) theta_l^2
                                    (5/7)^max(k-1,0).

Since r_v/M_u=c^k theta_u and all theta_l<=r,

    r_v^2 alpha_v^2
       <=r^2(c r)^(2k)(5/7)^max(k-1,0).               (6)

The ending query at checkpoint v has zero proximal height. Smoothness
along its unit negative-gradient step gives f(Z_v)<=0, so Z_v is an
actual minimizer of the SAME f, with f(Z_v)=0. The checkpoint sends the
iterate to Z_v-r_v omega_v. If the terminal-cell gradient is g and its
ordinary mass is T, the terminal point is Z_v-r_v omega_v-Tg.
Interpolation to the actual minimizer Z_v gives

    2f_end<=-2r_v<omega_v,g>-(2T+1)||g||^2
             <=r_v^2 alpha_v^2/(2T+1).               (7)

This allows any constant terminal direction, any affine offset, and any
positive terminal height. The original closest-minimizer normalization
is still 1: the first checkpoint query is omega_u=e_u/M_u with value
||omega_u||^2/2, forcing every minimizer z to satisfy <e_u,z><=0.
Origin is present, so its distance from x_0=e_u is exactly 1.

Set K=1/(2M_m-1), the global Huber score on H_m. The tail-mass bound
and M_m<=c M_(m-1) imply

    (2M_m-1)/(2T+1)<=2c-1<=39/10.

Put the explicit rational constants

    eta=(5/7)(441/400)^2=972405/1120000<1,
    Gamma_0=(9/20)^2(39/10)=3159/4000,
    Gamma_1=Gamma_0(441/400)^2
                          =614365479/640000000<1.

Here c r<=441/400. Combining (6),(7), uniformly over EVERY legal
parameter family and EVERY chosen u,v, yields

    2f_end/K <= Gamma_0                         if k=0,
    2f_end/K <= Gamma_1 eta^(k-1)                if k>=1. (8)

Thus the whole ascending Markov relay, even with a freely optimized
constant terminal cell, stays uniformly below the global Huber value.
If the number of transfers k tends to infinity, its relative value
tends to zero geometrically, uniformly in how m,u,v and all beta vary.
No dimension, amplitude, or initial-distance factor has been omitted.

## 4. Exact benchmark and the required next change

For the actual benchmark B_n from accepted `b98bb807d514e80d`, the
independent recursion calculation in the joint audit gives

    B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 lambda),
    M_m/lambda^m -> e,
    limsup B_(2^m-1) K<=2/e.

Consequently (8) gives, for the entire class,

    limsup B_(2^m-1) 2f_end<=Gamma_1*2/e<1,

and gives a limit of zero whenever k->infinity. This is an all-horizon
obstruction for this explicit family, not a finite calibration.

The tempting choice beta_l/theta_l=1-O(1/m) is impossible at every
interior transfer by (5). The initial profile excess c(c-2)>1 cannot
offset the fixed retained-query loss. A successful near-critical
varying-tail construction must therefore leave this first-order Markov
normal recursion, retain positive heights at interior relay endpoints,
use a different checkpoint organization, or genuinely vary gradients
inside the ordinary gaps. Correlated changes by themselves do not
remove the old-query obligation.

This does not close non-Markov correlations, nonconsecutive checkpoint
partitions, positive-height intermediate relays, or unrestricted W.
It supplies no full target theorem and adds no verifier-accepted fact.
# Near-critical ruler: complete quadratic screen and a quantitative cap obstruction

Status: UNVERIFIED joint proof candidate. No solver, numerical sweep, or
fact_submit was used. All new estimates are proved here; prior unverified
findings are not premises. Accepted fact `b98bb807d514e80d` supplies the
definitions and validity of K_n and the exact benchmark recursions; accepted
fact `1252b8823bb5a290` supplies smooth-convex interpolation. This does not
upper-bound the unrestricted W_n and does not settle the project target.

## 1. Exact schedule and all quadratic curvatures, including arbitrary b

Fix an integer m>=2. Within this document write

    lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+1/m), a=(1-1/m)/c,
    d_j=1+a c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j),
    n=2^m-1, S_j=sum H_j, M_j=1+S_j.

The parameters c,a remain fixed for this entire horizon. They are not
recomputed when j changes. The symbol H_j here names a schedule, not the
conjugate jet quadratic from the project glossary. Put r=c-2 and A_mass=a/r.
Counting the occurrences of each level gives, for 0<=j<=m,

    S_j=2^j-1+a(c^j-2^j)/(c-2),
    M_j=A_mass c^j+(1-A_mass)2^j.                       (1)

There are 2^(m-j-1) occurrences of d_j in H_m. Its scalar multiplier at
curvature t in [0,1] is exactly

    P_m(t)=product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^(2^(m-j-1)).     (2)

Let p_m be H_m without its final d_0 and let h^(m,b)=(p_m,b), b>=0. Then

    sum h^(m,b)=S_m-d_0+b,
    R_(m,b)(t)=(1-bt) product_(j=0)^(m-1)(1-d_j t)^N_j,
    N_0=2^(m-1)-1, N_j=2^(m-j-1) for 1<=j<m.           (3)

The exact worst normalized quadratic objective over ALL finite dimensions
is

    Q_m(b)=max_(0<=t<=1) t R_(m,b)(t)^2.                (4)

Indeed diagonalizing a positive semidefinite Hessian bounded by the identity
makes the normalized error a weighted average of the displayed scalar
quantities. A scalar quadratic attains the maximum. Zero eigenvalues have
zero objective contribution; affine translations to a minimizer change
neither calculation. Thus this is a full quadratic-class computation.

For an exact finite characterization of (4), subdivide [0,1] at all its
distinct interior roots 1/d_j and, when b>0, 1/b. On each root-free open
interval the logarithmic derivative of the nonzero objective is

    L(t)=1/t-2b/(1-bt)-2sum_j N_j d_j/(1-d_j t),
    L'(t)=-1/t^2-2b^2/(1-bt)^2
                    -2sum_j N_j d_j^2/(1-d_j t)^2<0.   (5)

Consequently there is exactly one interior maximizer between consecutive
roots, also between zero and the first root. On the last interval the
maximum is either its unique root of L or t=1. Values at roots are zero.
Coincident roots are combined, and b=0 simply removes its term. Equations
(3)--(5) specify the complete spectrum without a numerical sweep.

The unit-curvature values alone are

    P_m(1)=-(ac)^(2^m-1)c^(-m),
    R_(m,b)(1)=(1-b)(ac)^(2^m-2)c^(1-m).                (6)

They are not used as a replacement for (4).

## 2. Exact single-cut screen for every appended b

For a cut with step d, preceding mass P and following mass T, the accepted
cut value is (d-1)_+^2/[(1+P)^2(1+2T)]. For a fixed d and total schedule
mass S, its reciprocal denominator is

    D(P)=(1+P)^2[1+2(S-d-P)].

Its derivative has the sign of 2(S-d)-3P. Therefore its minimum on any
closed interval is attained at an endpoint. Among all occurrences of a
given level it suffices to check the first and last occurrence.

For h^(m,b), all levels 1<=j<m remain present. Their first and last
preceding masses are, respectively,

    P_j^first=S_j, P_j^last=S_m-d_j-S_j.                (7)

Their following masses are S_m-d_0+b-d_j-P. At level zero, after deleting
the original last occurrence, the endpoint preceding masses are

    P_0^first=0, P_0^last=S_m-2d_0-d_1,                 (8)

with following masses S_m-2d_0+b and d_1+b. For m=2 the two occurrences
in (8) refer to the same remaining index. The appended b contributes

    (b-1)_+^2/(1+S_m-d_0)^2.                           (9)

Taking the maximum of the cuts in (7)--(9) and
1/[1+2(S_m-d_0+b)] is EXACTLY K_n(h^(m,b)). No omitted internal cut can
be larger, by the denominator argument. In particular the accepted final
cut already proves W_n(h^(m,b))>=1/B_n whenever

    b>=1+(1+S_m-d_0)/sqrt(B_n).                        (10)

The global Huber term proves the same comparison whenever
b<=(B_n-1)/2-(S_m-d_0), if that right side is nonnegative. These tests use
the actual B_n, not a guessed dyadic constant.

## 3. An exact all-cut result at the original final step

For the remainder take m>=128 unless indicated, and first set b=d_0, so
the actual schedule is H_m. The following rational bounds will be used:

    12/5<lambda<c<49/20<5/2,
    3/8<a<lambda-2<1/2,
    ac>=127/128>7/8,
    0<a<c-2=r<=9/20.                                   (11)

For example lambda<17/7 gives
c<2193/896<49/20 for m>=128; a=(1-1/m)/c then exceeds3/8.
The upper bound a<lambda-2 follows from a=(m-1)/[(m+1)lambda].
Consequently 0<A_mass<1, and (1) gives

    M_j<=c^j, M_(j+1)<=c M_j,
    a c^j<=r M_j.                                      (12)

Let M=M_(m-1). For a first occurrence of any level j, P=S_j and its
following mass is at least S_(m-1). Its cut is at most
r^2/(2M-1). For a last occurrence, its preceding mass is at least
S_(m-1), and its following mass is S_j. Since x^2/(2x-1) is increasing
for x>=1 and M_j<=M, its cut is also at most

    r^2 M_j^2/[M^2(2M_j-1)]<=r^2/(2M-1).              (13)

These statements include the unique central occurrence, for which the
first and last positions coincide. Using c<=5/2, r<=1/2 and M>=1,

    r^2(2M_m-1)<= (5M-1)/4 <= 2M-1.

Thus every single-cut term is no larger than the global Huber term:

    K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                               (14)

## 4. Full-spectrum quadratic bound at b=d_0

This argument controls every t in [0,1], not only t=1. Write
v_j=a c^j t and, for the partial ruler H_j,

    Y_j=c v_j |P_j(t)|.

If no level 0,...,m-1 satisfies d_j t>=1, all factors in (2) are
nonnegative, and 1-u<=exp(-u) gives

    t P_m(t)^2<=t exp(-2S_m t)<=1/(2e S_m).             (15)

The last maximum is over all nonnegative t, so it also bounds the actual
restricted interval. Suppose instead that j is the first level with
d_j t>=1. If j=0, Y_0=ac t<=1. If j>=1, all factors in P_j are
nonnegative and t<=1/(1+a)<=8/11. Equations (1), (11) give

    t S_j>=v_j/r-t,
    Y_j<=c v_j exp(-v_j/r+t)<=cr exp(t-1)<1.            (16)

Here the maximum of v exp(-v/r) is r/e, cr<5/4, and
exp(3/11)>1+3/11=14/11>5/4. For every subsequent level l>=j the
factor is on its nonpositive side, and the exact recursion is

    Y_(l+1)=[1-(1-t)/v_l]Y_l^2<=Y_l^2<=1.             (17)

The bracket lies in [0,1]. If an earlier factor is zero the conclusion
holds directly as well. Therefore

    t P_m(t)^2<=1/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)t]
                      <=d_(m-1)/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m)),      (18)

using t>=1/d_(m-1). Equations (15),(18) prove the explicit full-spectrum
estimate

    Q_m(d_0)<=max{1/(2e S_m),
                 (1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))}.      (19)

Both entries are strictly below (14). For the first, S_m>=1 gives
(2S_m+1)/(2e S_m)<=3/(2e)<3/4. For the second, using (12) and ac>7/8,

    (2S_m+1)(1+a c^(m-1))/(c^2 a^2 c^(2m))
       <=2/[(ac)^2 c^m]+2/[c^2 ac]
       <=16/49+25/63=2233/3087<3/4.                   (20)

For the first term of this last bound it already suffices that m>=3
and c>2. Thus, with considerable slack,

    Q_m(d_0)<(3/4)K_n(H_m).                            (21)

## 5. Exact benchmark comparison, with no fitted constant

Let rho=log_2(lambda). The accepted benchmark defines the primitive mass
C_n=S_circle(n)+1 with join

    Omega(A,B)=(A+B+sqrt((A+B)^2+4AB))/2,

and B_n with join G(A,D)=2A+D/2+sqrt(D^2+8AD)/2. We prove directly
from these recursions that

    C_n<=(n+1)^rho, B_n<=1+4n^rho.                     (22)

Put F(t)=(1+t)^rho-1, t>0. Direct differentiation, with t=exp(z), gives

    d^2/dz^2 log F(exp z)
      =rho t(1+t)^(rho-2)[(1+t)^rho-1-rho t]/F(t)^2>0.

The strict inequality follows by integrating the positive derivative of
(1+t)^rho-1-rho t for t>0. Convexity at z and -z gives
F(t)F(1/t)>=F(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. If A=t^rho B and
D=(1+t)^rho B, this says (D-A)(D-B)>=2AB, or
D^2-(A+B)D-AB>=0. Hence

    Omega(A,B)<=(A^(1/rho)+B^(1/rho))^rho.

Zero arguments follow by continuity. Induction in the primitive
recursion proves the first bound in (22). Since G(A,D)<=4A+D,
induction in the dominant recursion, with a split k and l=n-k-1,
gives B_n<=1+4[(k+1)^rho+l^rho]<=1+4n^rho. The last inequality follows
by integrating the increasing derivative of x^rho, including l=0.

In our family (1) implies

    M_m/lambda^m
       =(1+1/m)^m[A_mass+(1-A_mass)(2/c)^m] -> e,        (23)

since A_mass->1 and 2/c is bounded below1. Thus the EXACT finite bound

    K_n(H_m) B_n
      <=[1+4(2^m-1)^rho]/(2M_m-1)                     (24)

has limit 2/e<1. This uses an upper bound derived for the actual B_n
at every integer horizon; it assumes no asymptotic phase or decimal
value for B_n/lambda^m. In particular, the full quadratic and all-cut
baseline is strictly below U_n^ZJ for every sufficiently large m.

## 6. Retained two-block geometry and all constant tails in its cap range

Here is the precise mechanism tested next. For ANY two selected cuts i<j
of an arbitrary schedule, let P be the mass before i, S the mass strictly
between i and j, T the mass after j, and define

    M_0=1+P, M_1=1+S,
    A=(h_i-1)/M_0, epsilon=(h_j-1)/M_1, sigma=2T+1.

Assume h_i,h_j>1 and choose the cap-range angle

    0<kappa<=A/(1+epsilon).                            (25)

Use the retained prefix gradients and proximal point

    p=(1,0)/M_0,
    q=(-kappa,sqrt(kappa(A-kappa)))/M_1,
    X=(-A,0), y=X-M_1 q, d=-M_1 q.                    (26)

These prefix data are actual jets of the single Moreau envelope of
J(z)=max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>), starting at (1,0). Before the first checkpoint
the proximal points are (1-(u+1)/M_0,0), 0<=u<=P, so the p-plane is
active. That checkpoint, of size1+A M_0, sends its input p to X. In
the second block, the proposed proximal points X-tq, 1<=t<=M_1, have

    <q,X-tq>=(A kappa/M_1)(1-t/M_1)>=0,
    <q-p,X-tq>
      =A kappa/M_1+A/M_0
          -t[A kappa/M_1^2+kappa/(M_0 M_1)]
      >=(A-kappa)/M_0>0.

This verifies every actual old query, even if individual ordinary steps
are large. At the second checkpoint its jet is

    x_j=y+q, g_j=q, f_j=||q||^2/2.

For completeness, the envelope minimizes J(z)+||x-z||^2/2. This has a
unique minimizer, and an active-plane slope g at z proves optimality at
x=z+g. At two proximal points, monotonicity of the subgradients gives
||g-g'||^2<=<g-g',x-x'>, hence a 1-Lipschitz gradient. The two minimizing
inequalities identify the derivative as g; joint convexity proves
convexity of the envelope. Thus the claimed function has the required
global regularity, not only valid formal data.

The second checkpoint, of size1+M_1 epsilon, sends the iterate to
X_tail=y+epsilon d. The optimum jet is (0,0,0). Every interpolant of
this prefix has origin as an actual closest minimizer to (1,0): the
retained first checkpoint jet (p,p,||p||^2/2), interpolated with any
minimizer z, forces <p,z><=0. Its squared distance from (1,0) is
therefore at least1, attained at origin. This normalization persists
for all extensions below.

Allow ANY legal continuation with a single common gradient g at every
tail query, including the terminal sample; allow affine intercepts,
positive values and extra dimensions, and retain every prefix jet.
Its endpoint is y+epsilon d-Tg. The accepted interpolation inequality
from this endpoint to the old jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) gives

    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g>-sigma||g||^2
                         <=epsilon^2||d||^2/sigma
                         =A kappa epsilon^2/sigma.

This uses a retained old query, not a reset to a new problem. From (25),
EVERY such constant-tail continuation obeys the exact universal cap

    2f_end<=U_ij:=A^2 epsilon^2/[(1+epsilon)(1+2T)].     (27)

In particular it covers all angles and all optimized tail planes of the
previous explicit two-checkpoint cap policy. It does not cover angles
kappa>A/(1+epsilon), different two-block prefixes, or variable-gradient
tails. No claim that (27) is attained for every pair is needed below.

## 7. The complete cap-range mechanism is dominated by K on H_m

Return to the full H_m, m>=128. For a selected pair i<j let their levels
be u,v; write d=a c^v=h_j-1, x=1+S, and epsilon=d/x. The individual
accepted cut terms at i and j are K_i,K_j. Direct cancellation gives

    U_ij/K_i=epsilon^2/(1+epsilon)
                          +2d epsilon/(1+2T),          (28)
    U_ij/K_j=(h_i-1)^2(1+P+h_i+S)^2
                          /[(1+P)^2 x(x+d)].           (29)

Every occurrence of level v has a full H_v immediately after it, so
T>=S_v. If v>=2 then M_v>=M_2=4+2a+ac>5, and by (11),(12),

    2d/(1+2T)<=2r M_v/(2M_v-1)<=10r/9<=1/2.

Consequently, for epsilon<=1, (28) is at most1.

If epsilon>1, then x<d. The first selected cut must lie INSIDE the
copy of H_v immediately preceding j and its level satisfies u<v.
Otherwise that copy lies between the cuts, giving S>=S_v and
x>=M_v>d. Inside this copy put P_loc for its mass before i. The
subcopies of H_u immediately before and after i yield

    P>=P_loc, x>=M_u,
    1+P_loc=M_v-(h_i-1)-x.

Also h_i-1<=d/c<=r M_v/c and x<d<=r M_v. For fixed x, the factor
(1+P+h_i+S)/(1+P) decreases with P, so substituting P_loc in (29)
gives

    U_ij/K_j
      <=(h_i-1)^2 M_v^2/
          [(M_v-(h_i-1)-x)^2 x(x+d)]
      <r^2/[2(1-r-r/c)^2]
      <=648/841<1.                                    (30)

The penultimate step uses h_i-1<=r M_u<=r x and x/(x+d)<1/2.
For the last, r<=9/20 and c>=12/5 imply 1-r-r/c>=29/80.

It remains to include the two small levels. If v=0, then d=a<1/2 and
epsilon<=d. Equation (28) is at most d^2/(1+d)+2d^2<=2/3. If v=1,
then d=ac<1. Unless i is the one level-zero step immediately before j,
the intervening mass includes H_1, so epsilon<=1/2 and T>=S_1>=1;
(28) is then at most1/6+1/3=1/2. For that one adjacent pair x=1 and
h_i-1=a. Equation (29) is bounded by

    a^2(2+a)^2/(1+ac)<=1/(1+ac)<1,

because a<=sqrt(2)-1 implies a(2+a)<=1. This covers EVERY ordered pair,
including pairs near the end and repeated occurrences of either level.

Combining (14),(27)--(30), every member of the complete constant-tail
mechanism in Section6 has normalized error at most K_n(H_m). Therefore

    max{Q_m(d_0), K_n(H_m),
        supremum of the Section6 constant-tail scores}
      =1/(2S_m+1),
    limsup B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.             (31)

This is a quantitative failure of a concrete retained-history mechanism
on the ASSIGNED constant-level family. Its tail bound allows all
dimensions, intercepts and positive values once its prefix and angle
range are fixed. Thus retuning only its two cuts, its angle inside (25),
or its final constant-gradient plane cannot produce the target comparison
for all appended b: b=d_0 already defeats that possibility.

The unrestricted W_n may be larger. In particular, (31) gives no upper
certificate for it, no primitive-validity conclusion, and no decision of
the original all-schedule/all-horizon problem. The remaining exact branch
is to leave (25), change more than two prefix cells, or allow genuinely
varying gradients within the remaining ruler block while retaining all
queried jets and the closest-minimizer normalization.

## 8. The entire accepted alternating-ray family also collapses to K

We now invoke the exact ray recurrence from accepted fact
`1fca3e5dd555cb4a`. No unverified ray exclusion is used. For any two
indices i<j in H_m, write P,S for their preceding and intervening masses,
and let u,v be their levels. Put r_u=a c^u and r_v=a c^v. We claim

    r_u r_v<(1+P)(1+S).                               (32)

If u>=v, or if i lies before the full H_v immediately preceding j,
the local copies give 1+P>=M_u and 1+S>=M_v. Thus (32) follows from
r_u<=r M_u, r_v<=r M_v and r<1.

Otherwise u<v and i lies inside that preceding H_v. Define P_loc there.
The local H_u copies on both sides of i give

    1+P_loc>=M_u, 1+S>=M_u,
    (1+P_loc)+(1+S)=M_v-r_u.

Expanding [(1+P_loc)-M_u][(1+S)-M_u]>=0 shows

    (1+P)(1+S)>=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u).

For fixed u define D_v=M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u)-r_u r_v.
At v=u+1, the identity M_(u+1)=2M_u+r_u gives

    D_(u+1)=M_u^2-c r_u^2
               >=(1-cr^2)M_u^2>0,

since cr^2<=3969/8000<1. At successive levels,

    D_(v+1)-D_v=M_u M_v+[M_u-(c-1)r_u]r_v>0,

because (c-1)r<=261/400<1. This proves (32) for every pair, including
the small levels with empty local blocks.

Suppose a ray construction selected at least two checkpoints i,j. Its
first two gradients and first old offset are

    m_0=1/(1+P), m_1=r_u m_0/(1+S), O_1=P m_0.

By (32), the second-return budget satisfies

    r_v m_1-O_1
       =m_0[r_u r_v/(1+S)-P]<m_0.                    (33)

With exactly two checkpoints, the terminal old-slope constraint requires
this quantity to be at least (1+T)m_0>=m_0. With three or more, the next
nonterminal gradient is this quantity divided by1 plus its block mass,
and hence is strictly below m_0, violating same-ray slope monotonicity.
Thus no accepted ray construction has two or more checkpoints. The
zero- and one-checkpoint cases recover K exactly, so

    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).                  (34)

## 9. The complete universal complex lens is also below Huber

This concerns the accepted Lens_n and maximal path-value Gap_h from fact
`dfde2fa326c908bf`, not the larger schedule-dependent domain D_h. The
accepted bound is

    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=(2alpha-|z|^2)|P_m(z)|^2,
    z=alpha+i beta,
    kappa_lens=alpha-|z|^2>=|beta|cot(pi/(n+2)).        (35)

All chronological prefix jets and their interpolation cycles remain those
of the accepted definition. We only bound their actual terminal polynomial.
We prove, for m>=128,

    sup_(z in Lens_n) Gap_(H_m)(z)<1/(2S_m+1).         (36)

The endpoints z=0 and z=1 follow directly: the former has zero error,
and at the latter the upper bound in (35) is P_m(1)^2, covered by (21).
Every other lens point has 0<alpha<1 and kappa_lens>0. Put

    delta=tan(pi/(n+2))<=1/100,
    tau_z=|1-z|^2/(2kappa_lens),
    q_decay=99/100.

The delta bound follows from tan x<=2x for 0<=x<=1/2 and pi<4,
since n=2^m-1. The tangent inequality follows from sin x<=x and
cos x>=1-x^2/2>=7/8 on that interval. From the lens inequality,

    |beta|<=delta alpha(1-alpha),
    kappa_lens>=alpha(1-alpha)(1-delta^2/4),
    tau_z<=F_delta(1-alpha)/(2alpha),
    F_delta=(1+delta^2)/(1-delta^2/4)<101/100.           (37)

Consider a partial ruler H_j all of whose levels l<j satisfy
a c^l<tau_z, with j>=1. In particular tau_z>a>3/8, and (37) gives
alpha<101/176<3/5. Each step d_l of H_j satisfies

    d_l alpha<(1+tau_z)alpha
       <=alpha+F_delta(1-alpha)/2<81/100.              (38)

Its real factor is therefore positive, at least19/100. The inequalities
log(1-u)<=-u and log(1+u)<=u imply

    log|1-d_l z|
      <=-d_l alpha+d_l^2 beta^2/[2(19/100)^2].

Sum with the exact multiplicities of the partial ruler. Its sum of
squared steps is at most its largest step times S_j, and (38) bounds
that largest step by81/(100alpha). Hence

    |P_j(z)|<=exp[-(1-12delta^2)alpha S_j]
                       <=exp(-q_decay alpha S_j).    (39)

For the numerical coefficient here,
(81/100)/[2(19/100)^2]=4050/361<12, and
1-12/10000>99/100. This is a symbolic bound; no spectral sampling occurs.

If no level l<m reaches a c^l>=tau_z, (39) applies to H_m. From (35),

    Gap_(H_m)(z)<=2alpha exp(-2q_decay S_m alpha)
                        <=1/(q_decay e S_m).

Since S_m>=2 and e>8/3, its ratio to Huber is strictly less than

    (5/2)/(q_decay(8/3))=125/132<1.                   (40)

Otherwise let j<m be the first level with a c^j>=tau_z. Put
Y_j=c a c^j |z| |P_j(z)|. If j=0, Y_0=ac|z|<=ac<1.
If j>=1, (39) and S_j>=a c^j/r-1 give, by maximizing
v exp(-q_decay v/r) over v>=0,

    Y_j<= [cr sqrt(1+delta^2)/q_decay]
                              exp(q_decay alpha-1)
       <=(9/8)exp(-2/5)<1.                           (41)

Indeed cr<=441/400, sqrt(1+delta^2)<=10001/10000,
and dividing their product by99/100 gives a number below9/8.
Also alpha<3/5 and exp(2/5)>1+2/5>9/8.

For every level l>=j, the definition of tau_z gives exactly

    |1-d_l z|^2
      =a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2+|1-z|^2-2a c^l kappa_lens
      <=a^2 c^(2l)|z|^2.

Consequently the ruler recursion implies Y_(l+1)<=Y_l^2, so Y_m<=1.
If alpha>1/16, (35) and |z|>=alpha imply that the ratio of its bound
to Huber is at most

    (2S_m+1) 32/[c^2 a^2 c^(2m)]
      <=64/[(ac)^2 c^m]<128/2^m<1.                   (42)

If alpha<=1/16, then tau_z>=(1-alpha)/(2alpha)>=15/2>a,
so j>=1. The same estimate before (41) now gives

    Y_j<=(9/8)exp(1/16)/e
                  <(9/8)(16/15)(3/8)=9/20<1/2.

Here exp(t)<=1/(1-t) for 0<=t<1 follows termwise from its series,
and e>8/3 follows by retaining its terms through degree four.
There is at least one update after index j, hence Y_m<1/4. The fact
that tau_z<=a c^(m-1), together with
|1-z|^2>=1-2alpha and kappa_lens<=alpha, yields

    alpha>=1/[2(1+a c^(m-1))]=1/(2d_(m-1)).

Equation (35) is therefore at most

    1/[8c^2 a^2 c^(2m)alpha]
       <=d_(m-1)/[4c^2 a^2 c^(2m)].                  (43)

By (20), its ratio to Huber is less than3/16. Equations (40)--(43)
cover every lens point and prove (36). Consequently

    V_lens(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=F_ray_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1).      (44)

Combining (21), (31), (34), (44) gives the same exact 2/e limit-superior
obstruction even after including ALL accepted single-cut, alternating-ray,
and universal-lens baselines, as well as the complete quadratic class and
the retained two-block constant-tail mechanism from Section6. The full
schedule-dependent complex domain, general multidimensional nonlinear
orbits, and variable-gradient tails remain outside this statement.

## 10. Remove the angle restriction: every constant tail of this two-block prefix

The cap-angle condition (25) is not needed for the following stronger
obstruction. Allow EVERY 0<=kappa<=A in the two-block prefix (26), and
retain all its actual jets and the optimum jet. The prefix legality
calculation in Section6 still holds: the only strict comparison there
becomes nonnegative at kappa=A. At kappa=0 the second block is already
at a minimum. The argument proving origin is the closest minimizer is
unchanged. For ANY legal constant-gradient tail, (27)'s derivation before
using the cap range gives

    2f_end<=A kappa epsilon^2/(1+2T)
                  <=A^2 epsilon^2/(1+2T).             (45)

No claim that this outgoing upper bound is attainable is made. We show
its right side is itself smaller than the old Huber value on H_m.

First strengthen (32). Define q_pair=c r^2<1/2, r=c-2 as before.
For ANY selected i<j, with their levels u,v and preceding/intervening
masses P,S, one has

    R_pair:=(h_i-1)(h_j-1)/[(1+P)(1+S)]<=q_pair.       (46)

In the outside-copy case of Section8 the bound r^2 already suffices.
In the inside-copy case its denominator is at least
M_u(M_v-r_u-M_u). Write theta=r_u/M_u<=r and z=M_v/M_u.
Here z>=M_(u+1)/M_u=2+theta and r_v<=r M_v. Consequently

    R_pair<=theta r z/(z-1-theta)
              <=theta r(2+theta)<=r^2(2+r)=q_pair.    (47)

The middle inequality follows because z/(z-1-theta) decreases for
z>1+theta. This includes u=0. It proves (46) with an explicit uniform
margin, not just the strict inequality used for the ray exclusion.

Let p=1+P, x=1+S, t=1+T and let r_i=h_i-1, r_j=h_j-1.
Here p,x,t are positive scalars, not the vector p from Section6. The
palindrome H_m gives the same (46) in the reverse chronological order:

    r_i r_j/(t x)<=q_pair.

Every cut has its own H_level on both sides. Thus r_i<=r p and
r_j<=r t. Combining these three bounds yields

    R_pair<=q_pair,
    p<=q_pair t/R_pair,
    x<=r^2 t/R_pair.                                  (48)

All quantities divided by are positive. The exact total mass is

    M_m=p+x+t+r_i+r_j
           <=(1+r)(p+t)+x.

Substituting (48), and then R_pair<=q_pair, gives

    R_pair M_m/t
       <=(1+r)R_pair+(1+r)q_pair+r^2
       <=D_pair:=2(1+r)q_pair+r^2.

Therefore

    R_pair<=min(q_pair,D_pair t/M_m).                 (49)

This retains both the true preceding mass and the true remaining tail;
neither is absorbed into a new initialization distance.

Write M=M_m, q=q_pair and D=D_pair temporarily. Since M>=8 and
D/q=2(1+r)+1/c<=199/60<4, the crossover t_0=qM/D exceeds1.
The function t^2/(2t-1) is increasing for t>=1 and 1/(2t-1) is
decreasing. Maximizing the bound from (49) on the two sides of t_0
therefore gives the exact elementary envelope

    R_pair^2/(2t-1)
       <=q^2/(2qM/D-1)=qD/(2M-D/q).                  (50)

For the permitted parameters,

    qD<=661/800,
    D/q<=199/60.

Indeed q<1/2, r<=9/20 give
D<=29/20+81/400=661/400, and the displayed formula for D/q gives
its bound using c>=12/5. Consequently, since M>=8,

    [R_pair^2/(2t-1)] / [1/(2M-1)]
       <=(661/800)(2M-1)/(2M-199/60)
       <=5949/6088<1.                                (51)

The ratio in the middle decreases with M because199/60>1; its value
at M=8 is5949/6088. Equations (45),(51) prove that EVERY legal
constant-gradient extension of ANY angle in (26) satisfies

    2f_end<=(5949/6088)/(2S_m+1).                     (52)

This covers all choices of the two checkpoints, all ordinary block
contents in their actual order, both endpoint angles, affine offsets,
positive values, additional dimensions, and arbitrary interpolation
choices for the tail, provided that its gradient is constant at all
actual tail queries and the terminal point. If no such tail exists the
statement is vacuous. No tail feasibility or direction optimization is
silently assumed. At kappa=0 its error is zero as noted above.

In particular, removing only the cap-angle restriction is NOT an escape
from the quantitative obstruction. Sections1--10 give the single joint
conclusion, for m>=128 and the original appended b=d_0:

    max{all quadratic scores, F_ray_n(H_m), V_lens(H_m),
        all Section10 two-block constant-tail scores}
       =K_n(H_m)=1/(2S_m+1),
    limsup of B_n times this maximum <=2/e<1.          (53)

The original all-schedule target remains unresolved. The remaining
geometric change must alter the retained two-block prefix itself or use
varying gradients inside the remaining ruler tail. The full
schedule-dependent complex domain and the other unrestricted mechanisms
identified in Section9 are also still open.

## 11. A quantitative requirement for a genuinely varying tail

Keep ANY angle 0<=kappa<=A in the exact retained prefix (26), but now
permit a varying tail. Denote its physical steps by t_l>=0, its gradients
at those queries by g_l, its terminal gradient by g_end, and put

    T=sum_l t_l, sigma=2T+1,
    Delta_tail=sum_l t_l(g_end-g_l).

This notation changes neither the prescribed steps nor the retained jets.
Its terminal point is y+epsilon d-sum_l t_l g_l. The outgoing
interpolation inequality to the retained jet (y+q,q,||q||^2/2) is

    2f_end<=2epsilon<d,g_end>-sigma||g_end||^2
                                  +2<g_end,Delta_tail>.

Completing the square gives the more useful exact bound

    2f_end<=A kappa epsilon^2/sigma
       +2<g_end,Delta_tail>
       -sigma||g_end-epsilon d/sigma||^2.             (54)

For the H_m geometry, the first term is bounded by gamma K, where
gamma=5949/6088 and K=1/(2S_m+1), by the complete calculation (45)--(51).
Therefore any such witness reaching 2f_end>=1/B_n must satisfy

    2<g_end,Delta_tail>
       >=1/B_n-gamma K
                   +sigma||g_end-epsilon d/sigma||^2. (55)

The true closest-minimizer normalization is still1 by the first retained
checkpoint jet. In particular, (24) implies the quantitative necessary
condition

    liminf B_n * 2<g_end,Delta_tail> >=1-2gamma/e>0     (56)

for any unbounded sequence of successful witnesses of this form.
This requirement keeps every parameter loss. Merely introducing new
gradient directions is insufficient: if
<g_end,g_l>>=||g_end||^2 at every positive tail step, the left side
of (55) is nonpositive. It includes all collinear contracting tails
g_l=c_l g_end with c_l>=1, and all changes g_l=g_end+w_l with
<g_end,w_l>=0. Neither class can escape the obstruction.

When T>0 and g_end!=0, Cauchy--Schwarz additionally gives

    sum_l t_l||g_l-g_end||^2
      >= [ (1/B_n-gamma K)_+ ]^2/[4T||g_end||^2]        (57)

for any successful witness. Equation (55) is stronger because it
retains the sign of the required terminal correlation and the square
loss. No sufficient construction with the required positive correlation
has been established. This is a necessary quantitative interface, not a
new lower bound on W_n and not an assertion of feasibility.

## 12. A different positive-height prefix, and its first failed shortcut

The preceding obstruction fixes the zero height at the second checkpoint.
One can change that geometry while retaining a legal function and exact
normalization. Fix M_0,M_1>=1, A>0, epsilon>0, choose

    0<=kappa<=A, 0<=nu^2<=kappa(A-kappa),
    v= kappa^2+nu^2,
    p=(1,0)/M_0, q=(-kappa,nu)/M_1,
    X=(-A,0), d=-M_1q, y=X+d,
    delta_second=(A kappa-v)/M_1>=0.                  (58)

Use the same actual ordinary blocks and checkpoints of sizes1+A M_0
and1+M_1 epsilon. The one fixed envelope of max(0,<p,z>,<q,z>)
realizes the first two blocks: the first p-plane comparison is unchanged;
in the second block, for 1<=t<=M_1,

    <q,X-tq>=A kappa/M_1-tv/M_1^2>=delta_second>=0,
    <p,X-tq>=(-A+t kappa/M_1)/M_0<=0.

Every query is thus legal. The second checkpoint jet has proximal point
y and value f_j=delta_second+||q||^2/2. The origin is the true closest
minimizer by the same saturated first checkpoint jet, without assuming
anything about the subsequent interpolant.

For ANY legal constant-gradient tail of total mass T, write sigma=2T+1.
The retained second-checkpoint inequality and the optimum inequality,
completed separately as squares, give

    2f_end<=min {
      2delta_second+epsilon^2 v/sigma,
      [A^2-2A(1+epsilon)kappa+(1+epsilon)^2v]/sigma
    }.                                                (59)

These are simultaneous necessary conditions, not an attained formula.
All incoming interpolation inequalities to the old positive-height
queries must still be imposed to produce a witness. They are not removed
by the accepted unqueried-terminal reduction: these are constraints on
the queried tail itself.

The tempting shortcut of treating the first term of (59) as an attainable
retained-height payoff is false. Take epsilon=1, kappa=A/2 and nu=0.
Then delta_second=A^2/(4M_1)>0, but

    X_tail=y+epsilon d=(-A,0)+2(A/2,0)=0.

This is the retained minimizer, so every subsequent gradient and every
terminal error are zero, for EVERY nonnegative tail. The first term
of (59) is A^2/(2M_1)+A^2/(4sigma)>0, whereas the optimum term is zero.
This is an explicit legal fixed-function counterexample to that shortcut,
not merely inconsistent formal data. It also shows why raising the
checkpoint height cannot be evaluated independently of its location.

The screened next tasks are consequently precise: either construct an
actual varying tail satisfying (55) with every old-query inequality, or
solve the joint positive-height prefix/tail constraints behind (58)--(59)
and obtain a value at least1/B_n for all appended b. No such payoff
comparison is claimed here. The present round's completed result is the
quantitative baseline and two-block-mechanism obstruction (53).
